1. **Reuse the existing Rhai lexical pass for spawn-reference discovery, while retaining spawn-specific interpretation.**

   **Observed implementations and callers.** `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/config.rs:3436` starts a source-text spawn scanner with its own comment/string handling at `:3595`, string traversal at `:3806`, delimiter matching at `:3828`, literal extraction at `:3850` and identifier-boundary handling at `:3866`. Separately, `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/script/validate.rs:382` implements `lex_significant_raw`, including comments, string literals and identifiers. The latter is already reused by `world/script/recipient_refs.rs:4`; recipient literals preserve raw spellings and use Rhai to decode individual strings at `:69`. This is an actual reuse precedent in the same package, not a proposed new dependency.

   Spawn scanning is live: `world/script/load.rs:229` discovers templates from each exact resolved source using `resolved_script_spawn_refs`, and `world/config.rs:3234` supplies template paths for preparation. It therefore participates in load-time content admission, rather than serving only editor decoration. Script validation's lexer serves function-location and reference checks (`world/script/validate.rs:587`, `:734`) as well as recipient validation.

   **Why it matters.** These passes duplicate the same lexical rules but already disagree. The spawn scanner's block-comment branch at `world/config.rs:3651` stops at the first closing `*/`; the validation lexer at `world/script/validate.rs:410` tracks nested comment depth. The spawn scanner also returns quoted string interiors without decoding escapes (`world/config.rs:3850`), whereas recipient reference extraction deliberately decodes escaped strings through Rhai (`world/script/recipient_refs.rs:69`). Consequently the maintenance burden includes separate fixes for comment nesting, quote handling and escaped identifiers. A nested-comment source containing spawn-looking text after the inner terminator is a plausible false-reference case; this is an inspection-based hypothesis, not a reproduced runtime failure.

   **Concrete opportunity.** Make spawn discovery consume the existing lexical mechanism, exposing it through an appropriate package-private lexical helper if necessary. Keep its spawn-call, map-field and override classification separate: those semantics are not duplicates of recipient selection. Preserve raw spelling and source locations in the shared token stream, and apply Rhai literal decoding only after proving the input is one literal, following the existing recipient implementation. Do not evaluate arbitrary map expressions or callbacks during scanning.

   **Compatibility and invariants.** This is not a direct substitution of `Tok::Str` for every current string slice. Spawn discovery currently needs byte spans and expression shape to classify static overrides, whereas existing tokens carry lines and raw spelling rather than byte offsets. It deliberately suppresses backtick contents; `Tok::Str` covers several literal forms, and the consumer must preserve that distinction. Computed templates must remain runtime-only, comments/prose must not create preload requests, source lines must still identify the resolved script, and existing warning-versus-error rules for uncertain overrides must survive. Content ledger inputs and activation admission must remain based on the same actual resolved sources.

   **Existing verification and gaps.** `world/config_tests.rs:2956` covers scripted template discovery. Parent `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/world/validate_tests.rs:1539`, `:1657`, `:1719`, `:1779` and the following scanner cases cover missing templates, anchor requirements, uncertain overrides and comments. `world/script/validate_tests.rs:259`, `:354`, `:461` exercise code-like text in comments/strings; `world/script/recipient_refs_tests.rs:34` and `:69` cover computed/reference distinctions and escape decoding. Consolidation should retain all consumer tests, add common nested-comment and escaped-string fixtures, and check byte/line correspondence with Unicode and multiline text. The tests were read, not executed.

   **Tradeoff and confidence.** Medium-high confidence that the lexical mechanism is duplicated; medium confidence on the final consolidation size because the current span needs differ. Sharing only lexing avoids building a general Rhai parser or erasing intentional consumer-specific rules. Savings are not estimated. No scenario tuning is implicated.

2. **Computer-message duration conversion can now reuse Contracts' existing seconds-to-ticks implementation.**

   **Observed implementations and callers.** `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/core/computer_message.rs:193` repeats the f64 multiply/round/cast formula used by `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/sim_tick.rs:114`. Its adjacent comment explains that duplication avoids depending from `core` into `world`. That explanation predates the present ownership: `world/script/schedule.rs:626` now merely re-exports the Contracts helper, and World already directly depends on Contracts (`Cargo.toml:12`) and re-exports `sim_tick` (`src/lib.rs:6`). There is no need for a World-to-parent or sibling dependency.

   `ActiveComputerMessage::show` calls the local conversion at `core/computer_message.rs:147`; the actual ECS caller is `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/narrative.rs:481`. The shared conversion already serves scheduled callbacks (`world/script/schedule.rs:398`) and named deadlines (`world/deadlines.rs:289`). Gameplay reaches it through its own compatibility facade (`crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/lib.rs:28`).

   **Concrete opportunity.** Keep the message-specific wrapper but implement it as the Contracts conversion of `duration_secs.max(1)`, followed by `.max(1)` on the resulting ticks. This removes the independently maintained arithmetic and updates the obsolete dependency explanation without changing ownership or exposing a new abstraction.

   **Compatibility differences that must survive.** A bare call is incorrect: shared `seconds_to_ticks` returns zero for nonpositive seconds and floors the rounded value at zero. Computer messages first clamp seconds to at least one, then clamp ticks to at least one. Thus a zero-duration request at 60 Hz currently lasts 60 ticks, not one tick; a valid low tick rate must still yield at least one. Preserve positive rounding, the existing expiry addition in `show`, supersession and expiry on the exact due tick. This is a small maintenance improvement, not evidence of a currently incorrect duration.

   **Existing verification and gaps.** `core/computer_message_tests.rs:109` and `:117` cover expiry boundaries; `:131` asserts that a nonpositive duration cannot expire immediately, but does not pin the full current one-second clamp. `world/script/schedule_tests.rs:12` verifies shared conversion at authored rates. Add narrowly scoped parity cases for negative/zero/positive duration and low/fractional tick rates before replacing the formula, preserving behavior for the wrapper's defensive inputs as well as normal authored values. Parent narrative tests beginning near `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/narrative_tests.rs:1036` exercise the ECS integration. None were run for this audit.

   **Tradeoff and confidence.** High confidence, low priority. The arithmetic is only a few lines; the benefit is one authoritative conversion rule, not material code-volume reduction. The message's distinct clamping policy remains local and legible.

**Similarities inspected that are justified rather than actionable duplication**

- `world/delayed.rs:43` and `world/script/schedule.rs:212` both partition due work. Delayed effects use elapsed f32 seconds and `TriggerAction`; callbacks use logical u64 ticks plus retained-AST keys and support single-equal-entry retraction. `world/script/schedule.rs:383` already converts deferred effects into the existing delayed-action path. Replacing both with one new scheduler would conflate clock and lifecycle semantics for little demonstrated benefit.
- The namespace shells in `src/lib.rs:10` and `world/mod.rs:1` are compatibility re-exports, not second implementations. Objective utility at `objectives.rs:6` also comes from Contracts. Removing those paths requires caller migration and is not justified by a reuse lens alone.
- `world/script/authoring.rs:14` explicitly records why Rhai metadata extraction is not enabled. Host descriptors are already derived from the same registrations through `HostRegistry` and `host_fn!` (`world/script/registry.rs:53`, `world/script/authoring.rs:96`). Recommending the dependency's metadata API solely because it exists would ignore configured-feature and WASM constraints.
- Handwritten JSON in `core/narrative.rs:358` and `core/report.rs:142` resembles work serde_json can perform, but AGENTS.md:897 explicitly confines that dependency's use to codec modules. The available Simulation codec is in World's parent and cannot be imported back into World. Narrative real formatting also intentionally fixes four decimal places. I do not recommend a direct dependency call or a parent import as a reuse fix. A compliant codec restructuring and format-compatibility assessment would be separate work.

**Coverage limitations**

This was a targeted reuse audit, not an exhaustive proof over every branch. The full `WorldConfig` parser, every effect registration/command arm, all GM decision models, commitment/workforce transitions, Objective-instance algorithms and dossier behavior were not compared line by line against all other repository modules. External vellum implementation internals were not inspected, so no claim is made that its engine facilities can replace Phoenix-specific budgeting or load registration. Scenario values and design causality were outside scope. The opportunities above are based on concrete implementations and callers in the inspected checkout; no test-pass claim accompanies them.


Audit 15/72 · Agent: `/root/audit15_world_architecture` · Lens: architecture

### Architecture

Module: Simulation World (`crates/phoenix-sim-world/`). Source inspected in `C:/Users/jkeyw/.codex/worktrees/reusable-game-layers/project-phoenix-v2`, HEAD `2de7d5329fba55e06ad00fbd8ca13f0cb4e8c73c`, against the instructed source baseline `07fc2e025bb2f5385b2fadf4fb468039b77c0b4e8c73c`. No source edits or test executions were performed.

Scope and orientation: read the architecture skill and LANGUAGE.md, repository instructions, wiki schema/index, domain vocabulary, reusable-layer guidance, World Plugin navigation and relevant reusable-layers, Objectives and scenario-scripting PASM material. Inspected the crate manifest and exports; Objective manager and instance projections; script engine, scheduling interface and fixture; trigger state/evaluation and registry interfaces; command vocabulary; and selected parent Simulation consumers and tests. No `docs/adr/` directory or nested crate AGENTS.md was found. PASM explicitly requires independent World/Gameplay/Session siblings, including development dependencies (`pasm/spec/architecture/reusable-layers.yaml:13`). Recommendations below preserve that arrangement.

#### 1. Deepen ship-effective Objective reading so consumers do not assemble the same two-stage projection

**Files.** `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/objectives.rs:438`, `:455`, `:478`; `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/objective_instances.rs:358`, `:389`; consumers at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/console_ai/server.rs:711`, `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/server_app/broadcast_publish.rs:378`, and `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/console/captain/server.rs:778`.

**Problem.** The World module contains both Objective representations, but its Interface still asks each reader to know how they combine. `ObjectiveManager::scored_pool_with_boost_for` applies definition scoring and legacy recipient filtering. A separate `ObjectiveInstanceManager::project_scored_for_ship` then substitutes instance identities and directives and suppresses terminal/unassigned instances. The Power AI consumer performs both steps, including an optional-resource fallback; broadcast publication repeats the arrangement with a Captain boost. Captain display instead collects visible snapshots and invokes the corresponding snapshot projection. These are real independent callers with a shared semantic obligation, not merely similar-looking loops.

The implementation is usefully split into legacy definitions and additive named instances, but that internal split leaks through the reading seam. A new reader can obtain something called a ship-scoped scored pool without having applied the named-instance rules. The observed code is not evidence that the listed callers currently omit the step; all three examples explicitly perform it. The architectural risk is that knowledge of the mandatory combination is distributed, while display and actionability have intentionally different terminal/history behavior.

**Solution.** Concentrate the existing combination of Objective definitions and instance projections inside the World module, retaining separate state ownership and serialization. Let parent Simulation adapters supply the live ship identity, World conditions and Captain selection; keep live ECS lookup and cross-domain scheduling in the parent. Provide a coherent reading seam for the existing use cases without inventing a new generic projection framework or merging the two persistence models. Exact Interface design is outside this audit.

**Benefits.** More Depth and Leverage: a consumer asks for the effective Objectives of one ship without reproducing the representation choreography. Better Locality: legacy fallback, terminal-instance exclusion and frozen display history are maintained together. The combined Interface becomes a test surface for the behavior consumers actually need.

**Invariants and verification.** Preserve legacy rows for definitions with no instances (`objective_instances.rs:369`), frozen last-known display after reassignment (`:374`), active/assigned-only directives (`:403`), directive override and relevance recomputation (`:412`), ship-specific Captain priority, subject targets versus recipient membership, and deterministic row order. Existing parent tests cover precedence, frozen history and legacy coexistence (`crates/phoenix-simulation/src/objective_instances_tests.rs:41`, `:237`, `:350`); Objective scoring tests remain useful. Add combined reading tests covering mixed legacy/named rows, a departed ship, terminal instances and Captain selection. This inspection did not establish a boost-ordering defect; verify existing behavior before refactoring rather than assuming the two stages commute.

**Deletion test, tradeoffs and confidence.** Deleting either manager would move considerable behavior into callers and is inappropriate. The candidate is to hide their repeated composition, not delete their implementation. A helper that merely renames one call without taking ownership of the combination would provide little Depth. Care is needed not to erase the display/actionability distinction or drag parent ECS types into World. High confidence in the repeated caller knowledge; medium confidence in the best extent of consolidation pending a full inventory of every Objective consumer.

#### 2. Put pure Objective Interface tests beside the World implementation rather than requiring the parent Simulation package

**Files.** `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/objective_instances.rs:142`, `:214`, `:270`, `:358`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/objective_instances.rs:179`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/objective_instances_tests.rs:41`, `:64`, `:98`, `:237`, `:350`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/objectives.rs:41` and `:45`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/objectives_tests.rs:243`.

**Problem.** Several public-Interface tests for the extracted World implementation remain attached to its parent compatibility facade. The instance test at line 41 constructs `ObjectiveInstanceManager`, activates two selectors and asserts ship views; line 64 exercises terminal credit, serde round-trip and reconciliation; line 98 asserts atomic conflict refusal. These need the World types and ordinary serialization, not live parent composition. Yet the test module is declared by `phoenix-simulation/src/objective_instances.rs`, which re-exports the World implementation at line 181. World has no sibling test declaration for these manager tests. The parent Objective test file similarly includes elementary manager tests such as empty snapshots and dirty state alongside tests belonging elsewhere.

This does not make the algorithms untested: parent tests exist and should continue to run. It makes their test surface poorly aligned with the package that owns them. The repository's targeted `cargo test -p <owner>` workflow can validate World while missing these fundamental World contracts, and understanding that coverage requires following a parent facade back out of the independent branch.

**Solution.** Relocate the pure public-Interface cases into sibling test files in World. Split mixed parent files by actual dependencies, leaving live faction rollback, ECS projection and integration cases in Simulation. Preserve the parent re-export compatibility paths. Do not add a parent or sibling development dependency to make a whole mixed test file compile unchanged.

**Benefits.** Improved Locality and test Leverage without changing production behavior or adding abstractions. A maintainer changing Objective assignment, lifecycle or serialization can validate the owning package and find the relevant specifications adjacent to it. This also makes the independent branch's practical testability match its declared architectural independence.

**Invariants and verification.** Preserve all assertions and fixture semantics, especially immutable completion membership, no transition replay on restore, equal-specificity atomic refusal, independent progress and frozen history. `objective_instances_tests.rs:119` exercises live faction rollback and must remain an integration test in the parent. `:412` uses live target projection and likewise needs dependency inspection before movement. Review the relocated inventory to prove no coverage was lost; no test execution was authorized for this audit. Parent facade coverage can remain small and explicit if a compatibility check is needed.

**Deletion test, tradeoffs and confidence.** Deleting the facade does not remove real compatibility needs and is not proposed. Relocating tests saves no meaningful maintained lines; the benefit is where behavior can be verified. Test helpers may need to be separated, and an indiscriminate file move would breach the sibling rule. High confidence for the individually inspected pure manager cases; the rest of the large Objective test file was not classified exhaustively.

#### 3. Make the script fixture preserve source identity when it invokes a registered trigger

**Files.** `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/script/fixture.rs:46`, `:94`, `:119`; `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/script/engine.rs:109`; production counterpart `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/world/script_invocation.rs:45`; callers `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/content_tests.rs:1077` and `:1186`, `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/config_tests.rs:2201`.

**Problem.** `ScriptedWorld::compile` supports all inline `[script.*]` bodies and stores the resulting ASTs in a map. Its `call_at` nevertheless always chooses `self.asts.keys().next()`, and `fire(index)` passes only the selected trigger's handler name into that operation. The registered `ScriptTrigger` already carries a `source_path` (`engine.rs:109`), but this path is discarded at the fixture's invocation seam. Production `invoke_effects` explicitly looks up `context.script_path` before calling the host.

This is a test-architecture mismatch: the fixture claims to model the compiled World yet callers must implicitly restrict it to handlers found in the first script unit. A handler unique to another unit can fail despite valid source, and same-named handlers in two units can run the wrong body. Those outcomes follow from the inspected lookup, but this audit did not execute a reproduction or establish that a shipped test currently hits either case. The existing content test's `world.fire(idx, ...)` is a concrete user of this seam.

**Solution.** Deepen the fixture's invocation responsibility so trigger invocation retains its registered source identity. Make direct named-handler fixture calls unambiguous when multiple compiled units exist, instead of silently selecting one by map order. Keep this work within the World-owned fixture/compiler/host modules; do not import the parent live invocation adapter just to reuse its ECS context preparation.

**Benefits.** Better Locality: AST selection belongs with the compiled script fixture, not with each test author's knowledge of lexical source order. Better Leverage: the same fixture can faithfully test a multi-unit World. The Interface test surface matches the production invariant that function identity includes its script unit.

**Invariants and verification.** Preserve inline virtual paths, deterministic map ordering, collected trigger order, the actual runtime host vocabulary and the documented fresh-per-fixture-call TickBudget. That fresh budget is intentional for tests driving many mission handlers and is not a production-budget defect. Add a fixture test with two inline units and identical handler names yielding distinguishable effects, plus a trigger whose handler exists only in the second unit. Assert effects and registered-source selection through `fire`, rather than internal AST counts. Existing engine and load tests cover their own machinery; they cannot prove the fixture selects the matching unit.

**Deletion test, tradeoffs and confidence.** The fixture earns its keep: deleting it would spread compilation and host setup across several tests. The opportunity is to make that module deeper and accurate, not create another production scripting abstraction. A full production adapter would introduce unnecessary live-state knowledge and threaten package independence. High confidence in the source-selection mismatch; medium confidence in its current practical reach because all fixture call sites and shipped multi-unit content were not exhaustively inspected.

#### Deliberate structures not treated as findings

- The exports in `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/lib.rs:5` and its small compatibility modules are not automatically shallow waste. They adapt the extracted source to shared Contracts/Model vocabulary and preserve useful paths without sibling imports. No deletion is suggested without a complete caller migration.
- `WorldTriggerRegistry` already concentrates trigger registrations and runtime state (`crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/trigger_registry.rs:63`, `:132`). Its replace/restore Interface is preferable to exposing independent mutable arrays. This audit does not recommend recreating the parallel handler-table design that the current wiki records as removed.
- `WorldScriptRuntime::invoke_effects` and `invoke_dialogue` in the parent already own shared budget preparation and live layered read-context construction (`crates/phoenix-simulation/src/world/script_invocation.rs:45`, `:75`). Moving this whole adapter into World would conflict with the deliberate parent-composition role. The fixture finding does not imply that those live adapters should be merged into the independent branch.
- The generic `ActionCmd<SpawnConfig>` command vocabulary and World's non-spawning alias (`crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/commands.rs:27`, `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/mod.rs:15`) support the existing dependency direction. A large enum is not by itself evidence of a shallow module; deleting it would redistribute effect interpretation across producers and consumers.

#### Inspection limits

This was a selective architecture audit of a large module, not an exhaustive line-by-line review. GM attention, NPC, quiet/workload, information/report and presentation reducers; the full WorldConfig parser; every Rhai host function; Comms dialogue evaluation; deadline/commitment/workforce internals; dossier evidence; and task/narrative/report lifecycle internals were not individually traced end to end. Their presence and some interfaces were inspected, but no findings or coverage claims are inferred from file size or naming. Parent runtime paths were read only to ground the selected World seams. No scenario pacing, deadline arithmetic or authored World design was analyzed or changed, so this report makes no scenario-design conclusions. No native/WASM build, determinism comparison or integration gate was run.

#### 1. Retire the unused two-way immediate-entity partition implementation, retaining its cases against the live three-way partition

**Evidence and callers.** `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/config.rs:3910` defines `partition_immediate_entities`, a complete loop which filters out `GameStart` entries and separates asteroid fields from everything else. `config.rs:4076` defines the production three-way version, which performs the same filter/classification and additionally distinguishes named from anonymous non-fields. Repository-wide Rust call searches found four callers of the older function, all in `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/config_tests.rs:3045`, `:3065`, `:3076`, and `:3226`. A broader symbol search found its definition and those tests, but no runtime caller.

The actual spawn path calls the three-way version at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/world/server.rs:1520`, using the overlay/cache-aware template classifier and consuming the fields and named buckets. The complementary setup path uses `is_owned_by_unified_pipeline` at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/server_app/world_setup.rs:242`; that predicate is live and is not a deletion candidate. PASM explicitly lists the three-way function under the world entity spawner (`pasm/spec/architecture/world-files.yaml:88`). The old function's documentation at `config.rs:3901` discusses named-entity routing, but its return value never distinguishes names. Keeping this unused implementation preserves a second place to update the immediate-spawn filter without helping either production caller.

**Concrete opportunity.** Delete the old two-way function and migrate its four tests to `partition_immediate_entities_three_way`. Those fixtures contain anonymous non-field entries, so their existing `others` assertions map directly to the anonymous bucket. Add an assertion that the named bucket is empty in each migrated case. Preserve the fixtures and their assertions about two separate asteroid fields, exclusion of GameStart, empty worlds, and an always-false classifier. Do not merely delete those tests because a newer test exists.

**Countable reduction.** The obsolete function occupies 25 physical lines (`config.rs:3910–3934`), plus 15 lines of directly attached API documentation (`:3895–3909`). Removing that whole unused API therefore removes 40 physical maintained lines. Four additional named-bucket assertions leave roughly **36 net physical lines removed**, assuming call/name substitutions do not introduce extra wrapping. This is an approximate formatting-sensitive total; the substantive deletion is the independent loop and unused signature, not removed test coverage or compressed syntax. No replacement helper or moved module is needed.

**Behavior and invariants.** Preserve the live three-way function's file order within each bucket, Immediate-only selection, classifier semantics, and named-versus-anonymous distinction. Do not change classifier call ordering, UUID minting, spawn schedules, or the cache/loader lookup used by production. The existing three-way test at `config_tests.rs:628` already checks fields, named, anonymous, and GameStart in one configuration. The four migrated tests retain the old edge cases while exercising the function the runtime actually uses. An explicit within-bucket order assertion would strengthen the regression coverage if the implementation itself is subsequently changed; no implementation change to the three-way helper is necessary for this deletion.

**Verification and tradeoff.** This audit ran no tests or builds. For implementation, inspect the migrated tests, run the owner-local World library suite, and retain normal layer-independence validation under the repository's integration process. There is a public-API caveat: `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/world/config.rs:1` glob-reexports this module, so absence of repository callers does not prove absence of downstream consumers outside this repository. The audit establishes a strong internal deletion candidate, not a guarantee about uninspected external API contracts. If that legacy export must be supported, retain it until the compatibility decision allows removal; replacing it with a wrapper would have smaller savings and would need care to preserve the original combined non-field order. Concatenating named and anonymous buckets would not preserve that order.

**Confidence:** high for the absence of in-repository runtime callers and the countable duplicate loop; conditional on compatibility policy for outright public-symbol removal.

#### Examined shapes that do not justify further LOC findings

- **Compatibility facades and independent fixtures.** `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/lib.rs:5` and `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/mod.rs:1` expose lower-layer contracts through established module paths. Removing those modules would mostly require longer imports or moving lines into callers. The 29-line `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/repo_fixtures.rs` resolves owner-local test file reads without mutating process CWD. Consolidating it by importing the parent or a sibling would violate the independent-branch requirement in `pasm/spec/architecture/reusable-layers.yaml:13`. No reduction is credited for moving either responsibility elsewhere.
- **Script registration verbosity.** `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/script/registry.rs:13` already documents and implements a single registration source for runtime functions and editor descriptors. `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/script/effects.rs:299` versus `:305` shows why similarly named registrations remain: legacy and instance-addressed Objective completion have different arguments and emitted commands. The integer and opaque fractional infrastructure overloads at `effects.rs:770`, `:787`, `:796`, and `:812` also preserve real Rhai type distinctions. Collapsing them into a large generic registration macro is not demonstrated to reduce total maintained code or preserve readability. No speculative savings are claimed.
- **Buffered state handles.** Flags use an overlay and the shared ordered effect sink (`crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/script/flags.rs:119`), whereas Deadlines hold a mutable table snapshot and a separate change buffer (`crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/script/deadlines.rs:70`). Their common Arc/Mutex shape does not establish duplicate semantics: read-after-write, scoped lookup, success-only draining, and the deadline queue edits are distinct. - **Pure delayed scheduling.** `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/delayed.rs:44` separates ready and pending actions in order; its unload policy at `:73` either cancels only the selected origin or makes it due. The test cases at `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/delayed_tests.rs:33`, `:50`, `:105`, and `:129` cover those distinctions, the inclusive boundary and stable order. An iterator rewrite could shorten the first loop slightly, but it would be syntax compression rather than elimination of a maintained responsibility, so it is not presented as a meaningful finding.
- **Small vocabulary repetition.** Objective instance ID validation (`crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/gm_objective.rs:19`) repeats the bounded-string expression from `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/gm_npc.rs:3`. Substituting the existing helper would centralize policy, but removes little physical code; it is not a substantial LOC opportunity. Likewise, replacing the explicit three-value stance parser at `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/script/effects.rs:1628` with a serde bridge changes diagnostic construction and is not shown to produce a net reduction while preserving the existing error surface.

#### Coverage limits

This is a focused deletion audit, not an exhaustive proof that all World code is necessary. The complete 4,112-line configuration parser, its 4,438-line test file, and the entire 1,823-line effect module were not read line by line. Script scanning/validation, trigger evaluation and registry state, schedule serialization, comms, commitments/workforce/deadline table internals, recipients, complete Objective instance lifecycle, task lifecycle/narrative stores, evidence storage, and the remaining GM decision models were inventoried or sampled but not fully traced. Their transitive callers and cross-target behavior remain unexplored for this lens. No scenario values, scenario design, script vocabulary, timing policy, schedule order, or tests were edited. There are no measured build-size or runtime savings claims.
