Audit 17/72 · Agent: `/root/audit17_session_overengineering` · Lens: overengineering

### Overengineering

Module: **Simulation Session** (`crates/phoenix-sim-session/`). Source inspected in `C:/Users/jkeyw/.codex/worktrees/reusable-game-layers/project-phoenix-v2`, against the audit baseline specified in `INSTRUCTIONS.md` (source revision `07fc2e025bb2f5385b2fadf4fb468039b77c0b4e`; subsequent HEAD change is documentation-only).

Inspected areas: crate manifest and exports; `SessionManager`; pure lobby handlers; station lookup and scenario arbitration; GM roster and collective start policy; the Phoenix connection-identity adapter; command queue/log and replay-contract model; fleet membership, origin authentication, frame vocabulary, agreement, host-loss and replacement-claim state. Caller tracing included Simulation's lobby compatibility adapter, result applier, command authorization adapter, headless replay, and selected owner/integration tests. Read orientation material in `AGENTS.md`, `wiki/SCHEMA.md`, `CONTEXT.md`, `wiki/index.md`, `wiki/concepts/reusable-layers.md`, and `wiki/entities/session.md`; consulted `pasm/spec/architecture/reusable-layers.yaml`, `sessions-replication.yaml`, and command-log/lockstep PASM search results. No nested `AGENTS.md` was found under `crates`; `docs/adr` is absent in this checkout.

#### 1. The small replay-contract simulator is exposed as production Session machinery despite having only test consumers

**Files and evidence.** `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/command_admission/log.rs:537` describes `CommandLogReplay` as a model of ordering rather than the Phoenix simulation. Its state at line 552 is a clock, applied-entry count and rolling digest; the `vellum_replay::Simulation` implementation at line 586 rejects a decreasing tick and otherwise updates those values. Lines 620–645 implement a separate FNV fingerprint, including a formatted `Debug` representation of the command payload. `crates/phoenix-sim-session/Cargo.toml:15` declares `vellum-replay` as an ordinary dependency, in addition to its testkit dev dependency. `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/command_admission/mod.rs:47` publicly re-exports the model.

The repository-wide symbol search found actual construction and execution of `CommandLogReplay` only in `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/command_admission/log_tests.rs:136`, `:147`, and `:169`. The full replay path instead implements the same external trait for `PhoenixSim` at `src/headless/replay.rs:475`. That implementation advances a real App and submits through production admission; its module documentation explicitly distinguishes the two models at `src/headless/replay.rs:5`. Its production callers use `vellum_replay::replay_into` at lines 878, 901 and 924. The important exception is `ReplayRejection`: `src/headless/replay.rs:109` imports it from Session, and `:495` constructs it. That type cannot simply be deleted along with the miniature model.

**Observed cost and deletion test.** The contract fixture is useful, but its production placement creates another public simulation-looking abstraction and a direct normal dependency solely to support that fixture. The API and comments require readers to distinguish the ordering model's clock/fingerprint from the actual replay engine and authoritative digest. Approximately one hundred lines of model/fingerprint implementation remain part of the ordinary library source surface even though no inspected runtime path invokes them. This is a source/API and dependency-scope cost, not a measured binary-size or runtime-speed problem; dead-code elimination may remove emitted code, and other workspace crates already use the dependency.

Removing this model from the **production API** leaves the real queue, command log, replay error and full replay engine intact. Removing the *tests* would lose a deliberately independent contract check and is not the recommendation. The deletion test therefore supports test-scoping this implementation, rather than deleting its verification or making the Session tests import the parent simulation.

**Concrete opportunity.** Keep the small replay implementation as a private test helper alongside `log_tests.rs`, retain the contract tests unchanged in purpose, remove its ordinary public re-export, and retain `vellum-replay` only as a Session dev dependency if a fresh usage check confirms the same sole production use. Keep `ReplayRejection` available to the actual headless replay caller. Update the few production comments that imply the miniature model is the runtime replay driver. This removes a production abstraction/dependency edge; it does not reduce the amount of maintained test logic and should not be advertised as a hundred-line deletion.

**Behavior and invariants to preserve.** The real `PendingCommands` wrapper is not part of this removal: `log.rs:422` records from `drain_due` as commands leave the canonical queue, preserving apply order and excluding bearer tokens. `stamp_accepted_command` at `:475` retains the existing local/peer order semantics. Solo delay remains zero; fleet ordering continues to use origin/sequence; no admission or Station-tenure predicate changes. `ReplayRejection` must still reject a backwards stamp before the real App advances. The independent Session test crate must continue to build without importing Gameplay, World, or its parent.

**Existing tests and missing verification.** The local contract test at `log_tests.rs:136` checks the external replay contract; `:147` checks the refused backwards entry and `:169` distinguishes fingerprints. Direct production queue tests already cover tick/order drain (`:44`), secret-token exclusion (`:72`), applied logging (`:99`), merged peer order (`:286`) and retained peer sequence (`:328`). `tests/command_log_replay.rs:165` exercises replaying a real recorded run. Those tests provide distinct evidence and should survive. A future change needs both the owner-local tests and the real headless replay test, plus an independent package dependency check; no checks were executed in this read-only audit.

**Tradeoffs, confidence and limits.** Confidence is high that the inspected repository has no runtime use of `CommandLogReplay`; confidence is moderate that removing its public export is acceptable, because repository searches cannot establish whether an external consumer uses this public Rust API. If compatibility must be maintained, defer export removal or arrange a migration instead of silently breaking consumers. The lightweight contract model itself remains justified as test infrastructure; only its production exposure is unnecessary on the observed evidence.

#### Structures checked that are justified by current requirements

- **The several Session side maps are not interchangeable caches.** `lobby/session.rs:42` stores native station reservations independently of connected tenure; `station_claim_allowed` at `:119` blocks both a reserved token switching seats and another token taking its reserved seat. `holder_for_station` at `:370` deliberately sees connected holders only. Pending ratings belong to a Station before spawn, eligibility is intentionally private rather than serialized on `Player`, and the AFK snapshot preserves a pre-AFK rating that disconnect's `last_rating` can no longer represent. Their different lifetimes and disclosure rules defeat a blanket “merge the maps” simplification. `session_tests.rs:580` covers AFK state/snapshot survival across disconnect; `result_application_tests.rs:221` covers disconnect before same-tick Identify and yielding to a new holder. Authority consumes these distinctions through `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/command_admission/policy.rs:49` and `:56`, where spectator/AFK and connected Station tenure are separate inputs.

- **Pure lobby results are an actual boundary, not a speculative event framework.** `lobby/handler.rs:22` returns outbound messages, a phase change, a rating change and countdown action. Simulation's `lobby/result_application.rs` applies those changes to its live ship and schedule-owned state, while `lobby/handler.rs:56` in that parent adds active content catalogues before calling Session. The separate pre-spawn and loaded-ship handling is exercised at `result_application_tests.rs:82` and `:157`. Collapsing the pure handlers into the Bevy adapter would reintroduce knowledge of Gameplay/World into an independently owned branch. Repetitive empty-result construction is ordinary boilerplate, not evidence of an overengineered abstraction.

- **Physical connection ownership is correctly separate from Session tenure.** `session_connections.rs:3` re-exports reusable transport types, while lines 4–13 add only Phoenix's reserved-identity policy. It does not duplicate the registry. The registry binds physical incarnations, whereas Session retains participant identity across reconnects; merging them would erase the distinction that keeps superseded sockets from commanding or disconnecting their replacement. The underlying registry implementation and complete native/browser ingress paths were not re-audited here.

- **Fleet ships, technical participants and public GM identities cannot be one roster.** `lockstep/mod.rs:98` keeps ships and technical participants separate; `with_participants_and_gms` at `:200` validates explicit technical membership and GM bindings. A GM-only process participates in the barrier without a ship; a departed ship survives on Backfill; a saved fleet can restart without the old mesh (`into_uncrewed`, `:391`). `uses_live_sessions` at `:440` distinguishes local standalone crew from frozen fleet authority. GM public projection (`gm_roster.rs:45`) excludes technical slots and credentials. Those differences justify the representations and constructors rather than a generic participant framework.

- **Queued loss/claim state and wire frames protect timing and admission.** `lockstep/host_loss.rs:41` records pending versus applied loss; `slot_recovery.rs:15` separately remembers a winning claim and whether recovery already opened. `slot_recovery_tests.rs:17` verifies arrival-order-independent winner selection and `:58` verifies opening once. `MeshOrigin::refuses` in `lockstep/mod.rs:503` expresses the star's authenticated sender/relay rule; a declared frame origin alone cannot replace it. The command wire projection and local queue also intentionally differ: local `Entity` routing and response tokens cannot cross the fleet wire. Similar shapes do not establish redundant machinery.

- **The Rust scenario arbiter has a necessary native caller.** `lobby/scenario_arbiter.rs:5` explains the browser's pre-App JS selection and the native running-App requirement. The returned normalized selection and three outcomes preserve first-valid-wins behavior and JS parity. It names the shared parity fixture at `:38`; deleting the Rust implementation because a JS counterpart exists would leave the native host without its arbitration policy. The entire native world-load path was not inspected in this lens.

#### Coverage limitations

This is a source inspection, not a runtime, security, or determinism certification. Large parent modules were followed only where they establish actual consumers or required invariants. In particular, the complete transport adapters, fleet snapshot/recovery orchestration, every host-loss edge case, complete schedule registration graph, browser arbitration implementation and all native pane tests were not exhaustively read. Owner-local test files and parent test sections were sampled around the proposed change and protected Session behavior. No tests, builds, dependency resolution, formatting, or network requests were run. No broad configuration/abstraction removal is supported by the inspected evidence beyond the narrowly scoped production replay-model candidate above.


Audit 18/72 · Agent: `/root/audit18_session_reuse` · Lens: reuse

### Reuse

Module: Simulation Session (`crates/phoenix-sim-session/`). Source inspected in `C:/Users/jkeyw/.codex/worktrees/reusable-game-layers/project-phoenix-v2`, against source baseline `07fc2e025bb2f5385b2fadf4fb468039b77c0b4e` and the documented navigation-only HEAD `2de7d5329fba55e06ad00fbd8ca13f0cb4e8c73c`.

Scope and inspection: the crate manifest and exports; SessionManager; pure lobby handlers, station lookup, scenario arbitration and start policy; GM roster validation; command queue/log/replay adapter; fleet roster, frames, crew canonicalization, agreement, host-loss and slot-claim state; selected owner-local tests and parent Simulation handler/session/lockstep tests. Compared those implementations with Runtime command/digest facilities, Transport identity facilities, browser scenario arbitration, native world selection and the parent LobbyResultApplier. Read repository instructions, wiki schema/index, domain vocabulary, reusable-layer guidance, Session wiki and relevant sessions-replication/reusable-layers PASM declarations. No nested AGENTS.md was found under crates; `docs/adr` is absent. No tests or builds were run, and no source was edited.

#### 1. Share the vacated-Station rating reset already implemented independently by three handlers

**Evidence and actual callers.** `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lobby/handler.rs:410` releases the previous Station during a successful switch. Its branch at line 420 emits Backfill and returns a Station rating update during InProgress; otherwise it clears the old pending rating and emits the authored first rating, falling back to `Std`. `handle_release_station` repeats this decision at line 506. `handle_set_spectator` repeats it at line 678, explicitly describing it as mirroring release. These are three implementations of the same rule about a vacated Station, rather than merely three similar message constructors.

All are live. `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/server.rs:1279`, `:1309` and `:1380` dispatch the corresponding messages to these handlers. Their outputs converge on `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/result_application.rs:87`, which either applies the returned rating to the live ship or queues the fleet rating intent, then emits the handler's outbound messages. The parent facade at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/handler.rs:1` re-exports these functions; it is not a fourth copy of the policy.

**Why it matters.** A change to the default-rating fallback, pre-game pending-choice cleanup or Backfill projection must currently be made three times. The code already recognizes that release through Spectator must leave exactly the same rating state as explicit release. The shared implementation is missing at precisely that repeated decision, while result application has already been consolidated.

**Concrete opportunity.** Extract the existing vacated-Station rating-reset operation inside the pure Session handler module and use it from those three paths. It should own only the common phase-sensitive pending-rating mutation, RatingChanged construction and optional runtime update. Keep the message-specific Station, readiness and role changes in their current handlers. This reuses existing policy rather than introducing another session abstraction or moving game rules into Runtime.

**Invariants and differences to preserve.** A Station switch validates the new destination before releasing the old one; it does not unready the player in every phase. Explicit release emits StationAssigned(None) and ReadyChanged(false), even though Spectator entry emits StationAssigned(None) only when a Station was actually held. Spectator entry also changes the role. Host-reserved consoles refuse release/Spectator and cannot switch to an unreserved Station. Preserve each operation's outbound ordering and its single optional Station rating update. Disconnect and AFK are not additional vacating callers: both retain the seat and maintain different restoration snapshots. Do not route those through a generic release operation.

**Existing verification and gaps.** `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/handler_tests.rs:1020` and `:1058` cover Spectator pending cleanup and in-progress Backfill; `:1656` covers release Backfill; `:2789` and `:2816` cover lobby release/switch cleanup; `:2850` covers host-assigned refusal. `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/result_application_tests.rs:157` covers the resulting loaded projections. Keep these public-operation tests. Add or extend a compact parity matrix for all three operations across Lobby/Loading/InProgress, authored non-Std defaults, missing Station definitions and no held Station, including exact message ordering. Shared-helper-only tests would not prove that each public path invokes it correctly.

**Tradeoff, confidence and limitations.** High confidence that the three policy bodies duplicate behavior; moderate benefit concentrated in maintenance consistency. A narrowly named helper improves locality, whereas having select/spectate call the entire release handler would change semantics and is not recommended. No current gameplay failure is asserted, and no line-savings estimate is needed to justify the duplication finding.

#### 2. Reuse the common crew countdown decision without erasing the existing trigger differences

**Evidence and actual callers.** `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lobby/handler.rs:581` maps crew readiness to a five-second Start or Cancel during SetReady. The same Start construction, five-second value, Lobby restriction and readiness query appear in the station-aware disconnect at `:1011` and the stationless disconnect at `:1067`. Their effect flows through the timer handling at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/result_application.rs:88`.

Both disconnect variants remain necessary in the inspected implementation. `LobbyResultApplier::disconnect` at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/result_application.rs:138` selects the station-aware path only when a live ship exists; otherwise it calls the stationless function. This is not an unused compatibility copy that can simply be deleted.

**Why it matters and opportunity.** The common countdown value and decision construction are maintained three times inside one module. A small private pure decision helper could express the common Lobby/readiness/start-target portion, with callers supplying the already-resolved loading readiness and their existing cancel condition. Keep public handler entry points and their Station effects. This is a smaller opportunity than finding 1; consolidation is worthwhile only if it remains clearer than the three short branches.

**Compatibility differences.** SetReady cancels when readiness is false only on an explicit unready request; SetReady(true) from one of several unready players yields no countdown action. Disconnect cancels whenever the remaining cohort is not ready. The station-aware paths treat an empty Station roster as sufficient to bypass Loading; stationless disconnect currently consults only `preload_complete`. A helper must not silently make those cases identical. Collective GM policy is also different: `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lobby/start_policy.rs:54` aggregates crew and GM cohorts with validation and force authority, and `:117` additionally blocks a standalone host on a disconnected local GM. `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/server.rs:1824` rechecks collective readiness in the live timer. Do not replace that policy with `SessionManager::all_ready`, move ECS timer ownership into Session, or bypass `local_start_allowed` for managed fleets.

**Verification.** `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/handler_tests.rs:253`, `:283` and `:312` cover disconnect start/loading/zero-player behavior; `:1117` and `:1142` cover spectator exclusion from readiness. `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/result_application_tests.rs:82` covers pre-spawn countdown projection and ordering; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/server_tests.rs:18` covers local GM start/cancel and `:1418` guards managed fleets against the legacy timer. Any consolidation should add table cases explicitly distinguishing SetReady(true) with another unready participant, disconnect while another remains unready, and empty roster with incomplete preload. Those differences are easy to accidentally erase by treating these bodies as exact copies.

**Tradeoff and confidence.** High confidence in repeated construction; medium confidence that extraction is a net clarity improvement because cancellation semantics are deliberately unequal. This is a modest reuse candidate, not evidence that the current start policy should be redesigned.

#### Existing reuse and investigated similarities that should not be consolidated blindly

- **Command ordering is already reused.** `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/command_admission/log.rs:326` stores `phoenix_runtime::commands::CommandQueue<PendingCommand>` and forwards origin, sequence, insertion and draining to it (`crates/phoenix-runtime/src/commands.rs:7`, `:62`). The Session layer's additional work at `log.rs:423` records an applied, credential-free LoggedCommand while retaining the bearer credential and entity route in the pending command. Removing the wrapper would remove that Phoenix-specific invariant. `log_tests.rs:72`, `:286` and `:328` exercise credential separation and peer-independent ordering. Re-exported CommandOrder/HostSlot at `log.rs:147` are compatibility, not duplicate implementations.

- **Physical identity and Session identity are separate, with real reuse at the seam.** `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/session_connections.rs:3` re-exports Transport identifiers and aliases its generic ConnectionRegistry; its IdentityPolicy delegates reserved-token checks to the Session handler at `lobby/handler.rs:106`. SessionManager at `lobby/session.rs:40` owns retained player identity, Station tenure, pending rating and private AFK/eligibility state. Replacing it with the connection registry would confuse a physical connection lifetime with a reconnectable player lifetime. The separate AFK and disconnect rating memories are intentional because a disconnect while AFK must not overwrite the pre-AFK rating; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/handler_tests.rs:1324` covers the interaction.

- **Digest agreement extends an existing ledger rather than copying its comparison contract.** `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lockstep/mod.rs:529` stores Runtime DigestLedger instances. Its `compare_sample` at `:584` checks an exact tick and suppresses duplicate discoveries; inbound and locally sampled checkpoints both call it (`crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lockstep/mod.rs:1129`, `:1792`). Runtime's `first_divergence` at `crates/phoenix-runtime/src/digest.rs:167` instead describes the first differing interval between entire ledgers. Substituting it would alter discovery-order and exact-sample semantics. Parent tests at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lockstep/tests.rs:498` exercise the exact-sample behavior.

- **The replay FNV loop resembles an existing dependency but has a material interface difference.** `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/command_admission/log.rs:619` and `:622` implement seeded incremental FNV; `:639` folds fields and subsequent commands into the existing accumulator. Runtime exposes only the fresh-seed `hash_bytes` alias at `crates/phoenix-runtime/src/digest.rs:3`. Inspected pinned vellum-digest source (`Cargo.lock:7141`, revision `606b0c0`) provides fresh-seed `fnv1a` and `fold_digest` for exactly eight digest bytes, not an arbitrary seeded byte slice. Neither is a drop-in replacement. Changing to postcard/whole-record hashes changes the existing fingerprint; buffering the entire log just to call the one-shot function is not a worthwhile reuse improvement. A future seeded dependency API could remove the loop, but no current reusable capability was found, so this is not an actionable finding.

- **Host-loss and command queues have different keys and merge laws.** `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lockstep/host_loss.rs:61` merges reports by lost slot and maximum agreed tick, ignoring already-applied slots. Its drain at `:107` sorts the resulting events by tick/slot. Runtime CommandQueue instead preserves individual command orders. Slot claims at `lockstep/slot_recovery.rs:31` choose minimum claim sequence and carry the winning tick, then remember opened recoveries. Similar maps/sets do not make these the same queue.

- **Browser/native scenario arbiters are justified counterparts, with caller differences.** Rust `lobby/scenario_arbiter.rs:1` explains why native arbitration cannot simply invoke the browser host's pre-WASM JS path. The JS implementation at `gui/scenario-arbiter.js:63` also manages claimant reservations, while native `src/native_host/world_load.rs:329` checks claimant identity around the Rust hull decision and `:387` supplies the slot-completeness gate, including pinned-ship and GM cases. Rust `is_complete` at `lobby/scenario_arbiter.rs:113` is therefore not an interchangeable replacement for JS `isComplete` at `gui/scenario-arbiter.js:118`. Shared baseline cases already live in `tests/fixtures/scenario-arbiter-parity.json` and are read by `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/scenario_arbiter_tests.rs:186` and the JS parity test. Preserve those shared cases and test native adapter compensation; do not introduce phone-side WASM or claim the adapters are redundant merely from matching function names.

#### Inspection limits

This was a static reuse audit, not an exhaustive functional or security review. All crate source areas were inspected, but parent integration tests were selected for the compared behavior rather than read in full. Browser/native scenario parity beyond the identified caller branches, all fleet recovery and continuation scheduling, every network admission path, and every downstream public consumer were not exhaustively audited. No external library recommendation is made. In particular, no conclusion here establishes full slot-arbitration parity or correct behavior under every recovery race. Existing independent Simulation siblings and parent-owned adapters must remain independent after any consolidation; sharing these small Session-local operations does not justify a dependency on Gameplay, World or the parent Simulation crate.


Audit 19/72 · Agent: `/root/audit19_session_architecture` · Lens: architecture

### Architecture

Module: Simulation Session (`crates/phoenix-sim-session/`). Source inspected in `C:/Users/jkeyw/.codex/worktrees/reusable-game-layers/project-phoenix-v2`, against the audit's code baseline `07fc2e025bb2f5385b2fadf4fb468039b77c0b4e`; the supplied current HEAD differs only in navigation documentation.

Scope: crate manifest and exports; Session identity and Station operations; pure lobby handlers and start policy; scenario selection and Station definitions; public GM roster; command queue/log interface; fleet roster, authentication-origin rule, agreement tracking, host-loss queue, slot-claim decisions and frame declarations. Caller tracing focused on the parent Simulation lobby adapters and lockstep ingress/digest publication/recovery, plus their existing tests. Read `AGENTS.md`, `wiki/SCHEMA.md`, `CONTEXT.md`, `wiki/index.md`, reusable-layers guidance, the Session wiki page, the reusable-layers PASM slice and relevant portions of sessions-replication and host-mesh-lockstep. No nested AGENTS.md was found by the repository file inventory; `docs/adr/` is absent. The architecture skill and its LANGUAGE.md informed this read-only exploration.

#### 1. Deepen fleet agreement tracking so arrival-order reconciliation lives behind its interface

**Files.** `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lockstep/mod.rs:529` (`MeshAgreement`), `:584` (`compare_sample`), `:616` (`forget_through`); `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lockstep/mod.rs:1124` (inbound digest handling), `:1774` (local sampling); `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lockstep/recovery.rs:410`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lockstep/slot_recovery.rs:222`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lockstep/tests.rs:453` and `:495`.

**Problem.** The Session module owns agreement state and duplicate-disagreement suppression, but its callers still implement the essential two-sided arrival protocol. On peer arrival the parent calls `compare_sample` and then directly creates/updates the peer ledger (`lockstep/mod.rs:1128`, `:1139`). On local arrival the parent directly records the local digest, enumerates every peer ledger for an already-received checkpoint at that tick, collects those samples and calls `compare_sample` for each (`:1782`). A caller therefore needs to know that comparison does not retain the sample, that a missing local sample means deferred comparison rather than agreement, and that recording a later local sample must re-check early peer samples. The public mutable `local`, `peers` and `disagreements` fields permit those obligations to be bypassed.

This is observable architectural friction, not a claim that current arrival handling is broken. The current implementation deliberately handles peer-first arrival correctly, and its regression test proves that exact orchestration. Nevertheless, the ordering rule spans the Session implementation and two distant parent-system paths. The comment on `compare_sample` describes shared discovery behavior, but its interface still requires callers to supply the rest of that behavior.

**Solution.** Move the pure sample-retention, peer-ledger creation and both-arrival-order comparison work into the existing agreement module. Keep actual world digest calculation, authenticated ingress filtering, schedule placement, egress and logging in the parent adapters. Retain access to the ledger evidence needed by recovery through the existing conceptual seam, without making ordinary sampling callers edit agreement internals. This is a responsibility change within the current modules; it does not require a new protocol, abstraction layer or sibling dependency.

**Benefits.** Greater depth: callers obtain the complete agreement update from one owned behavior instead of coordinating several internal state changes. Greater locality: arrival-order regressions and duplicate suppression can be reasoned about together. Greater leverage: inbound digest handling and local publication both use the same invariant-preserving implementation. The interface becomes the test surface for local-first and peer-first sample sequences, rather than requiring test fixtures to write the very internals whose orchestration needs verification.

**Deletion test.** Deleting `MeshAgreement` would spread ledger pairing, disagreement history and recovery cleanup across its callers, so the module earns its keep. The opportunity is to deepen it, not remove it. Conversely, retaining only `compare_sample` as the behavioral seam does not hide the protocol's full complexity.

**Behavior to preserve.** Pair checkpoints by logical tick; ignore absent counterparts until they arrive; preserve the existing first-discovery order rather than sorting by smallest tick; suppress repeated identical disagreements; remove only samples/disagreements at or before recovery's agreed cutoff. Preserve local-sampling cadence and no-double-publication behavior, and keep recovery suppression and departed/self-peer admission in the parent where live protocol state is available. The reusable Runtime `DigestLedger` remains the ledger implementation; no replacement comparator is proposed.

**Existing tests and verification gap.** `local_sampling_compares_a_peer_checkpoint_received_earlier_once` (`crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lockstep/tests.rs:453`) inserts a remote ledger directly, invokes real parent sampling, checks the mismatch and verifies one outbound publication. `checkpoint_comparison_preserves_discovery_order_and_recovery_cleanup` (`:495`) exercises comparison and cleanup but directly records local samples. Those are useful existing guards and should survive. An owner-local behavioral sequence test could additionally feed both sample orders through the deepened interface and verify the same disagreement evidence without constructing internal maps. Keep the parent publication regression because a pure agreement test cannot prove scheduling/egress integration.

**Tradeoffs, confidence and limits.** Recovery currently legitimately reads both ledgers (`recovery.rs:410`) and the interval (`slot_recovery.rs:222`), so making every detail inaccessible indiscriminately would merely create accessor noise. Concentrate on invariant-bearing writes and arrival choreography. Confidence: high that the split is real, medium that changing it deserves priority over feature work. No runtime failure was reproduced; no tests were executed, as instructed.

#### 2. Make the independent Session package's interface its own verification surface

**Files.** `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lobby/session.rs:43`; `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lobby/handler.rs:115`, `:308`, `:988`; `crates/phoenix-sim-session/Cargo.toml:7`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/session.rs:1`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/session_tests.rs:11`, `:44`, `:93`, `:103`, `:229`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/handler.rs:8`, `:55`, `:83`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/handler_tests.rs:11`, `:2526`, `:2850`, `:2918`.

**Problem.** Core Session behavior moved into the independent Session branch, but substantial verification of that behavior remains compiled only inside the parent Simulation crate. The parent's `lobby/session.rs` is a re-export plus a test module; its tests directly exercise `SessionManager` operations such as registration, duplicate rejection, reconnect and the privacy-preserving fleet Station-rating projection. These are the child module's interface and implementation, yet running that child's own tests does not run these assertions.

Some fixtures unnecessarily raise the test surface to gameplay configuration. `session_tests.rs:11` already constructs the required `ShipStations` value, then `test_ship_config` at `:44` turns it into a `ShipConfig`, and the availability tests beginning at `:229` convert that back through `stations_from_ship_config` before querying Session. That is appropriate only if the projection is what the test intends to verify; these named tests principally assert occupied/disconnected Station availability. The fixture consequently makes simple Session tests depend on unrelated gameplay configuration fields.

The lobby handler suite has a related split. The parent hosts a test-only `dispatch` (`handler_tests.rs:11`) that reconstructs the old message dispatch shape, while production invokes per-variant systems. Some cases exercise pure Session decisions; others genuinely exercise the parent's compatibility adapter that applies ratings to a gameplay resolver. Treating the entire file as inseparable hides that distinction and makes it harder to discover which crate owns a failed rule.

**Solution.** Relocate the tests that exercise only Session-owned decisions into the Session package and feed them Model/Contracts inputs directly. Start with the direct `SessionManager` cases, using the existing `ShipStations` fixture without a `ShipConfig` round trip. Separate pure handler-result assertions from integration assertions about `ControlSourceResolver`, catalogue enrichment or registered Bevy systems. Keep the latter in Simulation. Exercise pure per-variant handlers through their current callable interface rather than relying on the obsolete dispatch helper for tests that do not need message routing. This is a test-ownership and seam-alignment change, not a request to merge the independent branches or duplicate tests wholesale.

**Benefits.** Greater locality between the Session rule and the regression proving it; useful targeted validation with `cargo test -p phoenix-sim-session --lib`; fewer unrelated fixture changes when gameplay configuration evolves. The child package's independence becomes verifiable behaviorally as well as through dependency declarations. Parent integration tests retain their leverage by concentrating on the work the parent actually owns: live input projection, schedule ordering and applying returned effects.

**Deletion test.** Deleting these tests loses meaningful Session regression coverage, so deletion is not proposed. Removing the gameplay fixture round trip from tests whose only subject is Session availability removes incidental complexity instead of distributing it among production callers. The compatibility re-export itself still has real callers and is not a deletion candidate.

**Behavior to preserve.** Disconnected Session identities remain available for token-based reconnect; a disconnected remembered Station does not block an ordinary connected claimant; host-assigned native Stations retain their separate reservation protection; readiness and spectator/AFK semantics remain distinct; private eligibility remains outside `Player`; pending lobby ratings and reconnect/AFK snapshots keep their separate lifetimes. In particular, preserve the native reservation cases at `handler_tests.rs:2850` and `:2918`. Tests that verify disconnect actually applies Backfill through the gameplay resolver must remain at the parent seam rather than being reduced to checking an optional tuple.

**Existing tests and verification gap.** Existing tests already cover registration (`session_tests.rs:93`), fleet rating projection (`:103`), duplicate tokens (`:138`), disconnect clearing readiness (`:156`) and reconnect (`:166`). The problem is not absence of these assertions across the repository; it is their absence from the owning package's test surface. The Session package does already keep GM-roster, start-policy and command-log tests beside their implementation, demonstrating that owner-local testing is an established pattern. Parent registered-App coverage in `lobby/result_application_tests.rs` remains necessary and is explicitly documented by the Session wiki/PASM; it must not be replaced by pure tests.

**Tradeoffs, confidence and limits.** Moving the whole handler suite would be wrong: its imports and compatibility disconnect adapter deliberately reach gameplay. Classify tests individually, retaining a small projection/integration set in the parent. Shared fixtures should remain small and concrete; introducing a general-purpose fixture framework would erase the simplicity gained. Confidence: high for direct SessionManager cases, medium for the eventual division of the large handler suite because not every test body was individually inspected. No recommendation depends on adding forbidden parent or sibling dev-dependencies.

#### Investigated seams that are justified by current requirements

- `session_connections.rs:4` supplies Phoenix's reserved-identity policy to the reusable transport registry. Although small, it is a real game-policy adapter: deleting it would either scatter policy among callers or make Transport depend on Phoenix authority. Its type alias and forwarding validator are not evidence of a needless abstraction by themselves.
- `lobby/handler.rs:22` returns lobby effects instead of importing gameplay. The parent `lobby/handler.rs:83` compatibility adapter and `LobbyResultApplier` apply those effects to live Ship state. This is the declared independent-branch design, not an invitation to pull gameplay into Session. PASM explicitly records the applier's order and different pre-spawn inputs.
- `command_admission/log.rs:423` drains due commands and records their non-secret projection together. That is useful depth: callers should not separately maintain an applied-order log. The wrapper at `:474` is not by itself a compelling architecture finding because the queue already concentrates the stateful behavior and the public path preserves compatibility.
- `lockstep/host_loss.rs:41` keeps observed and applied losses separate and orders due work deterministically. `lockstep/slot_recovery.rs:15` hides winning claims and already-opened claims behind decisions. These modules earn their keep under the deletion test. Applying changes to the live world remains the parent's responsibility.
- The Rust scenario arbiter deliberately accompanies a JavaScript implementation because browser selection precedes WASM application startup (`lobby/scenario_arbiter.rs:5`). Its shared parity fixture is a requirement, not removable duplicate logic. This audit does not propose making phone clients load WASM.
- GM public presence is intentionally distinct from crew Sessions and fleet technical participation (`gm_roster.rs:3`; `lockstep/mod.rs:85`). Combining them would confuse Station capacity, readiness and privileged identity rather than deepen their interface.

#### Coverage limits

This was a static architecture audit, not a full behavior or security review. All source areas in the crate were inventoried; detailed reading concentrated on the interfaces and implementation paths named above. Command-log replay/hash implementation, every scenario-arbiter branch and fixture, every parent handler/server test, the entire lockstep recovery implementation, browser/native transport adapters, codec parity and cross-host end-to-end tests were not exhaustively inspected. PASM sections outside the relevant ownership/session/fleet portions were not exhaustively read. No builds, tests, network calls, code edits or specification edits were performed. The two candidates concern demonstrated interface/test locality, not claimed protocol defects or proven performance problems.


Audit 20/72 · Agent: `/root/audit20_session_loc` · Lens: loc

### Reducing maintained lines

Module: Simulation Session (`crates/phoenix-sim-session/`). Audit source: the `reusable-game-layers` worktree, source baseline `07fc2e025bb2f5385b2fadf4fb468039b77c0b4e` (HEAD `2de7d5329fba55e06ad00fbd8ca13f0cb4e8c73c` contains the documented navigation-only changes).

Inspected areas: package manifest and exports; Session records and Station tenure; lobby handler decisions, start policy, scenario selection and station lookup; GM roster; the transport identity adapter; fleet membership, command frames, agreement, host-loss and slot-claim state; command-log/queue implementation. Traced proposed lobby changes through the parent compatibility facade, registered handler systems and `LobbyResultApplier`; inspected relevant test cases and test inventories. Read the wiki schema, vocabulary, session page, reusable-layer guidance and the `sessions-replication` and `reusable-layers` PASM slices. No nested `AGENTS.md` was returned under `crates/`; `docs/adr/` is absent in this worktree. This was a read-only audit: no tests or builds were run.

#### 1. Replace repeated empty lobby-result literals with a single derived default

**Observed code and callers.** `LobbyHandlerResult` consists of three optional effects and an outbound vector (`crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lobby/handler.rs:22`). Eleven early returns spell out the identical neutral result: at lines 139, 320, 330, 338, 347, 358, 372, 387, 479, 562 and 646. They cover reserved Identify tokens, absent/invalid/auxiliary Stations, forbidden native claims, own-seat reselection, occupied/ineligible seats, reserved native release/spectate and spectator readiness. In every case the outbound vector is still empty when the return executes.

These are real live decision paths. The parent re-exports the handlers (`crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/handler.rs:1`), and the registered systems call selection, release and spectator handlers at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/server.rs:1297`, `:1328` and `:1398`. The result adapter acts only on present effects and appends the vector (`crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/result_application.rs:91`). Thus an empty result already has a precise, useful meaning: do nothing.

**Change opportunity.** Derive `Default` on `LobbyHandlerResult` and return its default at these eleven neutral exits. This removes repeated field initialization without merging admission conditions, altering control flow, or inventing an abstraction. Keep the guards and their explanations in place. Nonempty result construction may use explicit changed fields plus the default where that improves clarity, but this is not needed to justify the finding.

**Actual line reduction.** Each current empty literal occupies six physical lines; a default return takes one. Eleven replacements delete 55 lines, minus one line for the derive: approximately **54 net production lines**, without touching tests or explanatory comments. This is genuine deletion of initialization boilerplate, not relocation. Savings for partially populated results are not included.

**Invariants and verification.** The guards must remain before mutations and preserve native reservations, accessibility neutrality and reserved-token authority. A default must not acquire an implicit phase, countdown, rating or broadcast. Existing tests include own/occupied/unknown Station no-ops (`crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/handler_tests.rs:1367`, `:1398`, `:1430`), ineligible refusal (`:722`), spectator readiness refusal (`:1117`), reserved-token refusal (`:1933`) and native assignment restrictions (`:2850`). Retain these tests; inspect that refusal assertions cover all four output fields as well as Session state when implementing. The registered-App result tests at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/result_application_tests.rs:82` also protect application ordering.

**Tradeoff, confidence and limitations.** High confidence. Default construction hides the four explicit empty fields at a call site, but a named result type makes its purpose clear. Future effect fields need deliberate default semantics; using a default should never replace an admission guard. The numerical estimate covers only the verified eleven early returns.

#### 2. Keep one implementation of the vacated-Station rating reset

**Observed duplication.** Three handlers independently perform the same rating policy after vacating a Station:

- Station switch: `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lobby/handler.rs:418`–442.
- Explicit release: `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lobby/handler.rs:505`–531.
- Entering spectator mode: `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lobby/handler.rs:680`–704.

The policy is the same in all three: during `InProgress`, broadcast `Backfill` and return that Station/rating as an applied update; otherwise clear the pending rating and broadcast the first authored rating, falling back to `Std`, without requesting a live applied update. The spectator path explicitly describes itself as mirroring release. All three feed the same adapter, which applies locally or queues a fleet rating intent (`crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/result_application.rs:120`).

**Change opportunity.** Extract only this vacated-Station rating operation into a private helper in the Session handler module and invoke it at the existing three positions. Let it append the rating message and return the optional applied update. Preserve the handlers' distinct Station mutation, ready-state and role behavior outside the helper. This puts one policy in one place while removing two maintained implementations. It requires no dependency on Gameplay, World or the parent Simulation crate.

**Actual line reduction.** The three rating blocks occupy roughly 80 lines today. One helper of roughly 30–35 lines plus three multiline calls would replace them. A reasonable gross estimate is **25–40 net production lines**, after helper/call overhead and retaining the policy explanation once. This estimate is deliberately narrower than simply counting two whole blocks as deleted; formatter output and the chosen call shape determine the exact result. Do not count moving the remaining implementation to another file as additional savings.

**Behavior that must survive.** Keep the existing outbound order. Selection emits its old-seat clear, then old rating reset, then new assignment; release emits seat clear and ReadyChanged before the rating; spectator entry emits an optional seat clear, ReadyChanged, rating, then SpectatorChanged. Release emits its cleared-seat message even without a held Station, while spectator entry does not. Native-assigned consoles cannot voluntarily switch/release/spectate. A mid-game claimant remains on Backfill until Ready; the helper must not grant authority over the newly selected Station. Pre-game release must continue clearing pending ratings without producing a live rating update. These differences rule out replacing all three handlers with one generalized release/claim workflow.

**Existing tests and missing verification.** Relevant coverage is unusually good: spectator reset in Lobby and InProgress (`crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/handler_tests.rs:1020`, `:1058`), switch broadcasts (`:1450`), mid-game claim pending readiness (`:1520`), release to Backfill (`:1656`), pre-game release and switch clearing pending ratings (`:2789`, `:2816`) and native reservations (`:2850`). The parent result-application tests exercise loaded and pre-spawn contexts (`crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/result_application_tests.rs:82`). Preserve this caller-level coverage. Before implementation, ensure the tests assert the complete ordered message sequence, not merely existence, and cover an absent authored base rating falling back to `Std`; those exact combinations were not established by this audit.

**Tradeoff and confidence.** High confidence that the three policies are duplicated; medium confidence in the final savings until an implementation is formatted. A small domain-specific helper improves maintenance. A broad effect builder or generic transition engine would likely consume the saved lines and make tenure harder to inspect. Disconnect and AFK also emit Backfill, but they retain the seat and maintain different rating snapshots; they should not be pulled into a helper whose meaning is vacating a Station.

#### 3. Derive SessionManager's empty initialization instead of maintaining it twice

**Observed code and callers.** Every field of `SessionManager` is an initially empty vector or map (`crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lobby/session.rs:42`). Nevertheless `Default` manually delegates to `new`, and `new` lists the five empty collections (`:84`–99). `LobbyPlugin` constructs it through `SessionManager::new()` (`crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/server.rs:382`), as do the Session test fixtures (`crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/session_tests.rs:8`).

**Change opportunity and savings.** Derive `Default`, preserve the public `new()` entry point, and have `new()` call the derived default. This removes the handwritten Default implementation and the five-field empty initializer, for approximately **10–12 net production lines**. It is small, safe cleanup to combine with another edit rather than a standalone architectural project. It is true deletion: the compiler supplies the ordinary collection defaults.

**Invariants, tests and tradeoff.** Keep all five state stores distinct. In particular, `afk_prev_rating` must not be merged with `Player.last_rating`: AFK followed by disconnect records Backfill as the reconnect rating while preserving the earlier AFK restore target (`crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lobby/session.rs:68` and the interaction regression at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/handler_tests.rs:1324`). Native assignment reservations and anonymous eligibility remain separate private stores. Existing Session tests exercise construction followed by registration/tenure; there is no need for a new test mirroring Rust's derive. High confidence. This suggestion applies only to this all-empty state: custom defaults such as `FleetRoster` deliberately create a solo ship and participant (`crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lockstep/mod.rs:144`) and must remain semantic constructors.

#### Examined similarities that do not justify deletion

- **Connection and lockstep facades already reuse their mechanisms.** `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/session_connections.rs:3` re-exports Transport connection types and supplies Phoenix's reserved-identity policy; `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lockstep/mod.rs:3` re-exports Runtime's lockstep algorithm. Replacing these with direct imports everywhere mostly relocates wiring and disperses policy. No additional maintained implementation was found to delete there.
- **Readiness variants and disconnect paths have meaningful differences.** `handle_set_ready` cancels only when the incoming request is unready if the cohort is not all ready (`crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lobby/handler.rs:580`); disconnect cancels whenever the remaining Lobby cohort is not ready (`:1010`). Loaded disconnect treats an empty Station roster as immediately startable; pre-spawn disconnect does not (`:1062`). `LobbyResultApplier::disconnect` deliberately selects by presence of a loaded Ship (`crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/result_application.rs:145`). Blindly merging these countdown blocks changes behavior. A smaller countdown constructor might save a few lines, but its net benefit was not established, so it is not a finding.
- **Rust and JavaScript scenario arbiters serve different host lifecycles.** The native rule's counterpart and shared parity fixture are documented at `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lobby/scenario_arbiter.rs:5` and `:35`. Browser arbitration runs before the ECS app exists; native arbitration runs inside its established host lifecycle. Deleting either or introducing phone-side WASM is not a behavior-preserving LOC reduction.
- **Queue/log and host-loss records are not interchangeable just because both drain due work.** `PendingCommands::drain_due` records the nonsecret projection of applied commands (`crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/command_admission/log.rs:423`), while `PendingHostLoss` converges reports by slot, remembers applied slots and sorts due transitions by tick/slot (`crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lockstep/host_loss.rs:42`, `:107`). Runtime already owns the reusable command queue. Retain application-order, supplied peer order, run reset and token-redaction tests (`crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/command_admission/log_tests.rs:44`, `:72`, `:286`, `:328`, `:386`). A generic queue refactor would need evidence of net deletion and semantic compatibility that is absent here.

#### Coverage limits

The detailed caller/test tracing focused on lobby handlers, where the countable reductions are. Fleet frame schemas, membership, agreement, loss and claim implementations were inspected, but every transport sender, authoritative snapshot reader and native/WASM fleet integration was not traced. Test inventories were inspected and selected regression bodies read; this is not a claim that all tests in the package or parent were read. Scenario selection received a targeted implementation/parity review, not an exhaustive catalogue and host-loading audit. The report does not propose deleting public fleet or replay APIs on the basis of limited local usage, removing independence checks, shortening design documentation, or moving tests across the independent Session/Gameplay/World boundaries to make the crate appear smaller.

#### 1. Low-priority deletion opportunity: logging wraps empty collection constructors without adding policy

**Observed code and callers.** `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/logging/mod.rs:187` defines `empty_per_cat()` as exactly `HashMap::new()`; `:191` defines `empty_entities()` as exactly `HashSet::new()`. Neither chooses a different hasher, reserves capacity, installs defaults, filters categories or maintains an invariant. Their production consumers are `logging/filter.rs:27` and `:56`, and `logging/spec.rs:51`. The filter module already imports `HashMap` and `HashSet` at `:5`. The parser additionally constructs a `LogFilterConfig` with `per_cat: empty_per_cat()` and `..Default::default()` at `spec.rs:50`, even though the default implementation already constructs exactly that empty map at `filter.rs:52`.

Repository-wide search also found an independent `empty_per_cat()` in the parent compatibility area's `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/logging/filter.rs:7`, consumed by its filter tests at `filter_tests.rs:16`. That is not a caller of the Contracts helper, which is crate-private. It must not be treated as evidence that the Contracts helper is an externally required interface.

**Why this matters for this lens.** This is a small, concrete case of unnecessary indirection: a reader follows two domain-named factory functions to discover standard empty collections, and the parser restates a default it immediately inherits. It adds maintenance/import surface without hiding a decision. The cost is source navigation and redundant initialization, not a demonstrated runtime performance issue; empty standard collections do not imply heap allocation here.

**Change opportunity and deletion test.** Use the standard constructors in `LogFilterConfig::default` and `EntityFilter::new`, initialize `parse_log_spec` from `LogFilterConfig::default()`, and delete the two private wrappers and their now-unused imports. This removes the machinery outright rather than moving it or introducing another factory. The parent test-local helper can be considered separately; it does not need to change for this deletion.

**Preserve.** Default severity stays `Warn`, no entity filter stays unrestricted, category overrides start empty, and parse ordering/aliases/errors remain unchanged. Do not remove `AsLogFilter`, entity resolution, or the macro gate: those have separate behavior and are not implicated by this finding.

**Existing verification and missing verification.** `logging/spec_tests.rs` covers parser behavior; `logging/macros_tests.rs:8` exercises accepted macro argument shapes, and its later bare-App cases protect resource-optional logging. Parent `logging/filter_tests.rs` covers filter decisions. Existing tests are adequate for this narrow cleanup; a test asserting which empty constructor is called would merely mirror implementation. These tests were inspected, not executed.

**Tradeoff, confidence and limitations.** High confidence that these particular wrappers carry no current policy; low impact and low priority. A domain-named constructor could become useful if collection construction gains actual policy, but no such policy exists now. This is not a reason for a broad logging rewrite.

#### 2. Investigated and retained: aggregate RNG/ID state plus typed live handles is necessary ownership machinery

**Evidence and consumers.** `sim_rng.rs:493` introduces the per-stream resource, `:508` the `LiveStream` SystemParam, and `:537` the installation path. `world_id.rs:313` retains aggregate namespace cells; `:417` defines typed namespace resources, `:438` the live parameter, and `:458` installs all handles together with the aggregate. Real draws include parent `console/weapons/beam.rs:352` and `:1453`, and `console/comms/server.rs:1598`; real ID allocations include `console/weapons/torpedo.rs:550` and `comms/scripted.rs:194`. This is not unused generality introduced for hypothetical consumers.

The physical handles share the aggregate's actual cells. `LiveMint::as_deref` checks pointer identity and incomplete installation (`world_id.rs:443`); the corresponding RNG accessor checks the same lifecycle invariant. `server_app/registration.rs:530`–`:540` declares the physical resources as aliases of canonical RNG/mint owners. PASM's `headless-balance-telemetry.yaml:190` explicitly describes this as removing false cross-stream scheduling coupling while preserving serialized state, draw order and restoration behavior.

**Deletion test.** Replacing typed resources with one aggregate mutable resource would restore unrelated scheduler conflicts. Reading an internally mutable aggregate through shared ECS access alone would conceal writes from scheduler ownership. Copying streams into handles would break continuation/fingerprinting coherence. Removing installation checks would permit replacing an aggregate while systems continue drawing from stale cells. Consequently, the visible number of types and install helpers does not establish overengineering.

**Required invariants and verification.** Preserve exact stream identities, namespace values, UUID ordering/rendering, draw counts, same-stream ordering and coherent snapshot state. `sim_rng_live_stream_tests.rs:13` compares live and aggregate state; `:45` tests reseed/restore/rollback first draws; `:76` and `:84` reject incomplete and stale installation. `world_id_live_namespace_tests.rs:13` checks shared counters and tick reset; `:33` and `:41` reject missing/stale handles. No execution or independent concurrency profiling was performed, so this audit makes no new throughput claim.

**Change opportunity and tradeoff.** Retain the structure. A future simplification would need equal scheduler access and restoration semantics, not merely fewer types. Confidence is high that deleting the shared ownership layer now would discard current requirements.

#### 3. Investigated and retained: shared registries, directives and small queue contracts do not constitute a speculative framework

**State registry.** `authoritative.rs:131` keeps canonical declarations and explicit aliases in separate maps; `:157` rejects alias chains, missing owners and conflicting ownership. These rules are used by RNG/mint aliases above and by `phoenix-simulation/src/core/broadcast/reconnect.rs:100`. The enumeration guard reads the registry at `tests/authoritative_state_enumeration.rs:132` and resolves physical aliases at `:770`. Removing it would require recreating the classification/ownership relationship elsewhere and weaken the existing coverage boundary. `authoritative_owner_alias_tests.rs` covers owner bindings and rejection cases. The mechanism is therefore justified.

There is a documentation maintenance defect worth distinguishing from an abstraction defect: `authoritative.rs:21`–`:30` still says no production plugin declares state and the enumeration guard does not use it. The cited current callers disprove that description. Update this historical preamble when the file is next maintained; do not use it as evidence that the registry is dead machinery. This is a documentation correction, not a second runtime overengineering finding.

**Directive normalization.** `directive.rs:117`–`:161` distinguishes authoring surfaces, semantic slots and exact authored fields; `:326` represents raw directives and `:545` interprets them. Contracts' doctrine adapter (`doctrine.rs:128`, `:176`) and World's adapter (`phoenix-sim-world/src/world/config.rs:1859`, `:1882`) both consume it, and Gameplay uses the interpretation at `ai/core.rs:721`. Dedicated doctrine target spellings and World's generic target spelling require different forbidden-field diagnostics even when they ultimately form the same `AiDirective`. Removing the intermediate vocabulary and parsing each surface directly would duplicate those rules or lose diagnostics. `directive_tests.rs:18` specifically checks missing, empty, cross-kind and unknown-field rejection. Retain the common interpreter; do not replace it with a generic extensible schema framework without a new requirement.

**Effect queues and outboxes.** `effect_queue.rs:46` is essentially a typed `Vec` resource with a non-payload-constraining default. Parent `civilian/server.rs:136` registers its actual queue; `:170` consumes it, while `debris/server.rs:224` and `:323` use another payload. Type distinction enables independent ownership and state-census classification. By contrast, `lifecycle.rs:39` keeps SimOutbox entries private and requires producers to choose delivery class (`:51`, `:56`), with the parent bridge at `server_app/broadcast_publish.rs:191`. Lobby delivery drains separately at `lobby/server.rs:1943`, including transition-frame messages. These are different lifecycle/delivery contracts; replacing all of them with one generic event bus would introduce more mechanism and risk delivery semantics. Queue deletion must preserve drain-before-fold and message order; existing consumer tests, lobby `outbox_access_tests.rs`, and parent broadcaster tests are the relevant verification surfaces. Not every producer/drainer schedule was independently audited.

**Confidence and tradeoff.** High confidence in the traced current consumers, with moderate confidence in whole-system implications because this is a contracts audit rather than a complete schedule audit. No deletion is recommended for these mechanisms.

#### 4. Investigated and retained: multiple predicate entry points and presentation facades serve distinct present-day boundaries

`flags.rs:817`, `:829`, `:851` and `:930` supply progressively different borrowed inputs to one recursive evaluator at `:946`; they do not implement parallel evaluators. Gameplay's policy executor uses stateful evaluation at `ai/policy.rs:521`, `:726`, `:762`, while target selection uses selector evaluation at `ai/selector.rs:170` and `:176`. Missing facts/parameters return false, history is read through the owning policy memory, and boolean operators preserve short-circuiting. A single public method requiring every context would transfer empty/default plumbing to those callers rather than eliminate necessary knowledge. Tests in `flags_tests.rs:588`, `:662` and `:756` onward exercise selector, stateful and history behavior. Retain the convenient entry points; no generalized evaluator configuration is needed.

`presentation_contracts.rs:6` carries only preload readiness, read by parent lobby code at `lobby/server.rs:1340` and produced by Presentation at `registration.rs:108`. `TestWindowVisibility` (`presentation_contracts.rs:20`) is written by the simulation Workshop test clock (`workshop/test_clock.rs:87`) and consumed by the host adapter (`src/presentation_adapters.rs:5`). These tiny records avoid a reverse renderer dependency. Removing the facade would couple the simulation to the rendering host or duplicate the records. Readiness defaults and headless operation must remain supported; the parent lobby tests include readiness fixtures at `lobby/server_tests.rs:1013`. These are justified abstractions, not findings of excess machinery.

#### Coverage limits

This was a source inspection, not an exhaustive execution or performance audit. The module inventory was complete, but large files were sampled: the entire flags tokenizer/parser, every reference walker, all GlobalConfig fields and validators, all schedule labels, every directive diagnostic branch, and all world-ID/RNG historical tests were not line-by-line revalidated. Small identity, stance, modifier and debug-schema modules were inventoried rather than deeply traced. Consumer searches establish the cited uses; they do not establish that every public API has a caller. Dependency features were read but not rebuilt independently. No code, documentation or specifications were changed, and no reported test is claimed to have passed during this audit.
