#### 1. The two authored cadence-ratio checks repeat the same numeric rule

**Evidence and callers.** `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/scene.rs:300` (`checked_snapshot_every_ticks`) and `scene.rs:328` (`checked_sim_ticks_per_ai_tick`) contain the same sequence: require two positive finite `f32` values, divide them, round the result, reject a result below one or farther than `SNAPSHOT_RATIO_EPSILON` from an integer, then cast to `u32`. Only the selected fields differ: AI/snapshot versus simulation/AI. Both already use the same tolerance at `scene.rs:354`.

These are live implementations, not parallel test expectations. World load calls the first at `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/config.rs:2631` and the second at `config.rs:2697`, retaining a different authored-field error message for each relationship. The hot-path wrappers at `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/scene.rs:317` and `scene.rs:345` supply their own default ratio if validation fails. `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/ai/cadence.rs:104` reads both wrappers to derive the AI and snapshot latches from the fixed tick.

**Why this matters for reuse.** This is a small, direct duplication of one existing implementation inside the same owner. A future correction to finite-result handling, representable integer bounds or rounding tolerance currently has two places to update. It is not evidence that either clock should disappear: the clocks express different domain relationships, but their numeric acceptance rule is identical.

**Concrete opportunity.** Share the existing positive-integer-ratio calculation privately within `scene.rs`, leaving both named public methods, their selected field pairs, parse diagnostics, and fallback wrappers intact. No new crate, public trait, generic scheduling service, or sibling dependency is warranted. This is a low-priority local cleanup, most useful when these checks next need changing; there is no demonstrated runtime failure from the duplication itself.

**Preserve these boundaries.** Keep the existing `f32` arithmetic, tolerance, `u32` result and per-clock defaults during a behavior-preserving extraction. Do not opportunistically replace the calculation with `sim_tick::seconds_to_ticks` (`crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/sim_tick.rs:114`): that function rounds an authored duration, whereas these functions reject a noncommensurate rate pair. Do not merge `checked_autosave_interval_ticks` (`scene.rs:275`) either: it multiplies in `f64`, requires an exact whole tick count, explicitly bounds `u64`, and intentionally refuses rounding. Simulation frequency bounds and field-specific messages remain World validation responsibilities. Any stricter treatment of overflow would be a separate behavior change, not an incidental result of reuse.

**Existing and missing verification.** `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/config_tests.rs:273` checks rejection of the 25/10 AI ratio and acceptance of an authored 25/5 ratio with a compatible simulation clock. `config_tests.rs:294` checks default simulation/AI cadence, a nondividing 50/30 pair and an authored 120/30 pair. Adjacent floor/ceiling cases at `config_tests.rs:327` and `config_tests.rs:355` protect the separate simulation-frequency bounds. `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/ai/cadence_tests.rs:52` exercises tick-derived cadence at shipped rates. These should remain boundary tests. A narrow table exercising both checked methods against equivalent field pairs would additionally pin zero, negative, non-finite and near-tolerance cases; no such direct paired test was identified in the inspected files. Autosave tests at `config_tests.rs:124` and `config_tests.rs:141` should remain separate because their rule is different.

**Tradeoff and confidence.** High confidence that the two bodies duplicate a single numeric rule; low severity. One private helper adds a jump when reading either public method, but prevents acceptance semantics drifting. This finding does not assert exhaustive invalid-input coverage or a currently reachable overflow defect.

#### Existing reuse and suspected duplication that is justified

**The layer facades are reuse, not copied implementation.** `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/sim_rng.rs:1`, `world_id.rs:1`, `authoritative.rs:1`, `effect_queue.rs:1` and `world/flags.rs:1` re-export the Contracts owners. Gameplay and World similarly re-export shared vocabulary: see `crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/objectives.rs:2`, `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/objectives.rs:5` and `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/lib.rs:22`. Removing these merely because there are several paths would break compatibility and internal imports without deleting another implementation. The architecture slice `pasm/spec/architecture/reusable-layers.yaml:1` explicitly requires independent branches joined through common contracts and compatibility facades.

**Directive adapters already converge on one interpreter.** `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/directive.rs:545` owns interpretation. Doctrine projects its established fields in `doctrine.rs:126`, then calls that interpreter in `doctrine.rs:174`. World projects its different established fields at `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/config.rs:1857` and calls the same interpreter at `config.rs:1882`; Gameplay's runtime doctrine path uses it at `crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/ai/core.rs:721`. The adapter repetition reflects incompatible authoring field names, not a second rule for requirements, defaults or kind conversion. Tests include `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/directive_tests.rs:18` for typed rejection, `directive_tests.rs:88` for established empty/untargeted defaults, and World cross-surface comparisons around `config_tests.rs:1810` and `config_tests.rs:1830`. No recommendation to coerce the two TOML surfaces into one schema follows from this audit.

**Network and AI admission use one authority rule.** `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/authority.rs:47` is called by `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/command_admission/policy.rs:61` and `crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/command_admission/ai_emit.rs:61`. Those callers supply different available facts, as intended by `CommandClaimant`; they do not need a shared Session dependency. The early AI/local-console branches, crew spectator/AFK checks, summary permission and debug-route ordering are material semantics. Similar-looking producer adapters are not grounds for replacing this shared rule with a sibling import or bypassing Admission.

**History wraps the existing ring implementation rather than rebuilding it.** `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/flags.rs:61` imports `phoenix_math::bounded_history::BoundedHistory`. `HistoryReducer::reduce` at `flags.rs:611` delegates arithmetic to that type after checking fullness. `AiHistory::fold_history` at `flags.rs:731` adds the distinct policy requirements: authored `(fact, ticks)` identity, removal of undeclared windows, one sample per shared AI tick and reset on an absent reading. The underlying storage is at `crates/phoenix-math/src/bounded_history.rs:42`. Gameplay's helm histories also reuse it (`crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/ship/helm_ai/surfaces.rs:366` and `surfaces.rs:387`). Removing the Contracts wrapper would move policy semantics into every caller. Existing tests explicitly compare history behavior with the base helper around `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/flags_tests.rs:845`, protect independent authored windows at `flags_tests.rs:899`, and protect serialization at `flags_tests.rs:1152`.

**RNG and identity machinery have similar handle shapes but different behavior.** The RNG uses `vellum_rng::Pcg32` and delegates stream-name hashing to `vellum_digest::fnv1a` at `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/sim_rng.rs:482`; it does not maintain another PRNG implementation. Running stream state (`sim_rng.rs:317`) and event-keyed generation (`sim_rng.rs:412`) have different reproducibility contracts. Neither is interchangeable with the composite-key derivation in `crates/phoenix-math/src/composite_rng.rs`: replacing a seed derivation changes results even if both are deterministic.

The `LiveStream` and `LiveMint` adapters both check that a restricted scheduler-visible handle references its installed aggregate. However, minting additionally advances namespace sequence counters and synchronizes the tick; RNG snapshots carry PCG positions and preserve a deliberately retired stream slot. Their install operations (`sim_rng.rs:537`, `world_id.rs:458`) share actual cells, not copies of authoritative state. A generic shared-handle abstraction is not justified by the short matching `Arc::ptr_eq` pattern alone. Existing tests at `sim_rng_live_stream_tests.rs:12`, `sim_rng_live_stream_tests.rs:45`, `world_id_live_namespace_tests.rs:13`, `world_id_live_namespace_tests.rs:34` and `world_id_live_namespace_tests.rs:42` protect coherent ownership, replacement and rejection of stale/incomplete installations. The pinned draw test at `sim_rng_tests.rs:19` and stream-state restoration at `sim_rng_tests.rs:177` must remain independent behavioral checks.

**Outboxes and effect queues are not one interchangeable queue.** `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/effect_queue.rs:46` is an owner-specific resource whose values are applied within a tick and must be empty at the fold. `session_io.rs:11` holds lobby messages, drained unconditionally into reliable output at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/lobby/server.rs:1943`. `lifecycle.rs:38` holds simulation output with explicit producer-chosen delivery class and is drained by the fixed-tick broadcaster at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/server_app/broadcast_publish.rs:191`. `messages.rs:81` is a synchronous inter-System queue, while `messages.rs:24` is a per-ship admitted-command component. A shared `Vec` shape does not make their lifecycle, authority, scheduler access or delivery contracts equivalent. The broadcaster's conversion from `SimOutboxEntry` to `OutboundMessage` is a small record projection, not a second transport. No queue merger is recommended.

**The UUID dependency does not remove the domain mint format.** `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/world_id.rs:219` packs a particular namespace/tick/sequence payload, and `world_id.rs:263` accepts only the mint's strict hyphenated marker form before extracting those fields. Generic UUID parsing alone would admit formats and UUIDs this code deliberately excludes. Existing tests at `world_id_tests.rs:35`, `world_id_tests.rs:43`, `world_id_tests.rs:59` and `world_id_tests.rs:80` cover UUID validity, round trips, tuple-compatible sorting and rejection of non-mints. The `uuid` dependency could support some syntactic work, but I did not establish a smaller, equivalently strict replacement for the complete domain codec and therefore do not count it as a reuse finding.

#### Coverage limits

This was a static reuse audit. It did not execute the cited tests or revalidate native/WASM determinism. The full 1,751-line predicate module, its lexer/parser and every diagnostic branch were not inspected exhaustively; history, evaluation, chain resolution, comparison and referenced-operand traversal were the focus. The larger test files were read selectively for the mechanisms discussed. Schedule labels were inspected as an inventory, not checked against every system registration. Logging parsing/filtering and the documented const-target requirement were inspected, but no dependency-level comparison with all Bevy/tracing filter APIs was performed; an assertion that the existing logging wrapper can simply be deleted would exceed this evidence. Repository-wide consumers were traced through searches and selected bodies, not every command handler, script host, snapshot fold, JavaScript counterpart or source-scanning guard. Upstream `vellum` internals were not audited. These limitations do not affect the directly observed cadence duplication, but they preclude a claim that no other reuse opportunity exists anywhere in Contracts or its consumers.


Audit 23/72 · Agent: `/root/audit23_contracts_architecture` · Lens: architecture

### Architecture

Module: Simulation Contracts (`crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/`). Source inspected in `C:/Users/jkeyw/.codex/worktrees/reusable-game-layers/project-phoenix-v2`, against the audit's source baseline `07fc2e025bb2f5385b2fadf4fb468039b77c0b4e` and documentation-only navigation update `2de7d5329fba55e06ad00fbd8ca13f0cb4e8c73c`.

Scope and inspection: the crate manifest and module inventory; detailed reading of `authority.rs`, `control.rs`, `scene.rs`, `authoritative.rs` and its declaration/alias tests, `sim_tick.rs`, `session_io.rs`, `lifecycle.rs`, `messages.rs`, `effect_queue.rs`, `routes.rs`, and `objective_utility.rs`; selected implementation/interface inspection of `flags.rs`, `directive.rs`, `sim_rng.rs`, `world_id.rs`, and logging. Traced command authorization into the parent Simulation and Gameplay, cadence validation into World and cadence consumption into Simulation, and declaration consumers into the authoritative-state enumeration guard. Read the wiki schema, domain vocabulary, index and reusable-layer orientation, and relevant reusable-layers, station-system-authority, world-files and deterministic-simulation PASM material. No nested `AGENTS.md` was found under `crates`; `docs/adr` does not exist in the audited worktree. PASM therefore supplies the relevant recorded architecture decisions.

This is a read-only source audit: no builds or tests were run. Test references below describe inspected source, not newly obtained passing evidence. No replacement Interfaces are designed here.

#### 1. Deepen command authorization so the shared policy owns the summary-input control gate

**Files.** `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/authority.rs:29`, `:47`, `:55`, `:67`; `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/control.rs:18`, `:26`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/command_admission/policy.rs:26`, `:31`, `:61`; `crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/command_admission/ai_emit.rs:61`.

**Problem.** `CommandTargetPolicy` supplies both a `ControlTickPolicy` and a `summary` boolean. `authorize_command` uses `summary` to allow local input when ordinary human input is disabled, to treat AFK summary input specially, and to bypass the ordinary human-input rejection. However, it never reads `control.accept_summary_input`. The parent Adapter must already combine that control fact with the command's eligibility when producing `summary`: it checks `policy.accept_summary_input`, the repair System kind, and the two supported repair-priority payloads at `policy.rs:31`.

Consequently the Interface is wider than its types suggest. The caller must know that `summary` means “this payload qualifies AND the current Control Source allows summary input,” not simply “this is a summary command.” The common policy has the control fact available but relies on a caller having enforced it. A direct call with an Offline control policy and `summary: true` returns Allowed for `LocalConsole`; the current parent does not construct that combination. This is an Interface hazard and a deepening opportunity, **not evidence of a reachable production admission bypass**.

There are two real Adapters at this Seam: the parent resolves network/local Sessions and Station tenure, while Gameplay emits immediate AI commands without a Session dependency. Gameplay correctly supplies false for summary/debug facts because its AI branch only uses `operate_ai`. That division is valuable. The concern is the one general authorization predicate being partly implemented outside the Module that claims to own it.

**Solution.** Keep command-specific eligibility, effective target resolution, Session facts and build-gated debug-route knowledge in their existing owners. Concentrate the combination of summary eligibility with the current control policy inside the shared authorization Module. Make the distinction between eligibility and already-authorized input unambiguous in its documented Interface. This can be a narrow refactor; it does not require moving Gameplay registries or Session state into Contracts.

**Benefits.** Better Locality: changes to how Control Source governs summary input have one policy owner. More Leverage: a caller supplies domain facts without remembering part of the authority algorithm. Direct tests can cross the same shared Interface both Adapters use, with contradictory inputs testing its refusal behavior rather than depending on an outer caller never constructing them.

**Deletion test.** Deleting `authorize_command` would duplicate the AI/human/local distinctions and authorization branch ordering in the two Adapters. It earns its keep. Deepen this existing Module rather than merging Gameplay and Session or inventing another routing abstraction.

**Behavior and invariants to preserve.** AI authorization depends on `operate_ai`; ordinary human input follows `accept_human_input`; supported Simplified repair intents remain distinct from actuator ownership. Preserve spectator rejection, AFK treatment, local-console privileges, registered debug-route behavior, unknown-System reporting, hosted Station resolution and branch order. No post-Admission consumer should branch on who issued a command. Independent simulation siblings remain independent, as required by `pasm/spec/architecture/reusable-layers.yaml:14`.

**Existing tests and missing verification.** Parent policy tests exercise actual Adapters: repair holder admission (`policy_tests.rs:122`), Offline and damaged rejection (`:152`, `:177`), AI versus human (`:192`), unknown Systems (`:329`), local/debug routes (`:360`, `:380`, `:400`, `:440`), and spectators (`:467`, `:485`). Gameplay control-source tests verify derived control policies. `authority.rs` contains no own test module; there is no standalone authority test file in this crate's inventory. Add a compact policy matrix at the Contracts Interface for claimant/control/summary eligibility combinations, while retaining the parent tests that prove real Session and target facts reach that Interface. Search/read coverage did not establish the complete set of summary-intent tests elsewhere in the repository.

**Tradeoffs, confidence and limitations.** Medium-high confidence in the distributed-knowledge observation; medium priority because the inspected production caller is correct. A careless refactor that treats all summary-shaped payloads as eligible would widen authority. Keeping eligibility with the owning Gameplay knowledge and only consolidating the control gate avoids that. Do not add configuration, runtime dispatch or a generic policy framework for this small rule.

#### 2. Concentrate global cadence validation with the cadence calculations it protects

**Files.** `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/scene.rs:19`, `:275`, `:300`, `:317`, `:328`, `:345`, `:365`, `:381`; `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/config.rs:2623`, `:2631`, `:2648`, `:2670`, `:2683`, `:2697`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/ai/cadence.rs:95`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/save_slots_lifecycle.rs:260`.

**Problem.** The shared `GlobalConfig` Module owns the rates, defaults, rate limits and checked integer conversion functions. The World parser owns the sequence of calls that establishes their collective validity: the AI/snapshot relationship, simulation floor/ceiling, exact autosave interval and simulation/AI relationship. Simulation then calls unchecked convenience methods whose documentation assumes `parse_world` has run. Those methods silently substitute default divisors if their checked counterpart fails.

Understanding the valid timing Interface therefore requires reading three Modules: shared calculations, World's parse-time validation, and Simulation's runtime assumptions. The current authored-content path performs the checks, and this audit does not establish a shipped invalid world. The architectural friction is that the validity of a shared value is enforced only by one external constructor while the shared value remains publicly constructible and mutable. New fixtures or another loading Adapter can supply a GlobalConfig without reproducing those assumptions.

This is not a request to move World's composition-wide semantic validation into Contracts. The checks listed here depend only on the `GlobalConfig` fields and constants already owned by Contracts; they do not need entity definitions, World layers or any sibling import. `RouteConfig::validate` at `routes.rs:97` is an existing local precedent: intrinsic validation lives with the shared record while anchor resolution remains a composition concern.

**Solution.** Deepen the existing global timing Module by concentrating its intrinsic timing validation and derivation in one place, invoked by the World parser. Retain World-specific parse context and composition rules in World. Clarify where fallback behavior is intended for fixtures versus where valid authored timing is required. No new package, duplicated mission-input cache or independent clock is needed.

**Benefits.** Improved Locality for changes to cadence rules: an authoring check and the runtime conversion it protects no longer require coordinated edits in different crates. More Leverage for future loading Adapters and tests: they can validate shared timing without parsing unrelated World TOML. Direct Interface tests can cover the complete timing relationship while parser tests continue to verify authored diagnostics and aliases.

**Deletion test.** Removing shared checked conversion methods would spread rounding and commensurability rules across consumers, risking determinism. They are useful depth already. The opportunity is to bring their related validation knowledge behind that same Seam, not delete the Module or replace integer-tick scheduling.

**Behavior and invariants to preserve.** Keep 30 Hz/240 Hz simulation limits and the relationship of the lower limit to the helm integration cap. Preserve the existing floating-point tolerance for rate ratios and exact whole-tick autosave conversion; those are intentionally different policies. Preserve serde defaults and `ai_helm_tick_hz` compatibility. Simulation's no-World fixture behavior at `ai/cadence.rs:109` deliberately uses `(1, 1)`, not authored defaults; concentrating validation must not silently change it. Preserve tick indexing, FixedLast latch timing, and native/WASM agreement. If default-on-invalid behavior is intentionally retained, document and test it rather than accidentally changing it during relocation.

**Existing tests and missing verification.** World tests cover exact/invalid autosave intervals (`config_tests.rs:124`, `:141`), the old AI tick key (`:258`), noninteger cadence (`:273`), simulation/AI divisibility (`:294`), and lower/upper simulation limits (`:327`, `:355`). The current Contracts inventory has no `scene` test module. Add direct timing Interface coverage so intrinsic validity is verified independently of the World parser; retain representative parser integration cases and runtime cadence tests. No full runtime cadence suite was read or run here.

**Tradeoffs, confidence and limitations.** High confidence in the locality problem, medium priority. Moving only parser code without reducing the caller's validation obligations would merely relocate lines. Avoid growing an all-purpose GlobalConfig framework or redesigning every configuration field; the concrete opportunity is the existing timing rules. Error order and wording may be observed by authoring tools/tests, so preserve them initially. This report does not assert a production timing fault or recommend changing accepted content.

#### Examined structures that do not justify a deepening recommendation

- **Contracts as a shared crate is intentional.** `lib.rs:5` exposes shared vocabulary to independent Gameplay, World and Session Modules. Re-export scaffolding and data-only records are appropriate Interfaces here, even when their Implementation is small. The deletion test would push cross-domain types into mutually dependent siblings, contradicting the recorded layer design.
- **State Census overwrite semantics need caution, not an automatic finding.** `authoritative.rs:128` allows redeclaration to replace class/PASM metadata. Although that can make conflicting declarations order-sensitive, `authoritative_owner_alias_tests.rs:51` explicitly tests revising a canonical owner's classification while aliases follow it. Rejecting every changed declaration would remove a tested behavior. This audit did not trace a production conflict or establish a requirement to prohibit revision, so no change is recommended. Exact generic keys and explicit alias validation already provide substantial depth.
- **EffectQueue is not an unnecessary generic event bus.** `effect_queue.rs:20` documents per-owner full drains and ClearedAtFold semantics. Its shallow collection shape alone does not justify replacing it: schedule ownership and fold invariants remain important. `SimOutbox` deliberately exposes classification methods with private entries (`lifecycle.rs:37`), whereas `LobbyOutbox` has different transition/drain semantics (`session_io.rs:6`); combining them merely for a common vector shape would conceal meaningful differences.
- **Logging's Adapter choice is justified.** `logging/mod.rs:12` explains why entity filtering cannot be provided by metadata-only category filtering and why the implementation preserves native and browser formatting. An extra custom tracing layer is not a demonstrated simplification.

Unexplored or partially explored areas: the full predicate parser/evaluator and all 1,182 lines of flag tests; all directive conversion paths and authoring front ends; the complete RNG/identity lock and restoration implementations, their full cross-target callers and continuation proofs; every scheduling label and registration edge; every logging macro/filter branch; small catalogue, fleet, stance, power, modifiers and presentation records beyond inventory/navigation; exhaustive consumers of Objective utility; and the full host/snapshot integration suite. No exhaustive correctness claim is made for these areas. The findings above are limited to the concrete Interfaces and callers described.


Audit 24/72 · Agent: `/root/audit24_contracts_loc` · Lens: loc

### Reducing maintained lines

Module: **Simulation Contracts** (`crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/`). Source inspected in `C:/Users/jkeyw/.codex/worktrees/reusable-game-layers/project-phoenix-v2`, against the source baseline `07fc2e025bb2f5385b2fadf4fb468039b77c0b4e` identified by the audit instructions.

Scope: package manifest and module inventory; focused implementation reads of `flags.rs`, `scene.rs`, `directive.rs`, `sim_rng.rs`, `world_id.rs`, `lifecycle.rs`, `routes.rs`, `objective_utility.rs`, and logging filter/specification parsing. Followed relevant flag and cadence callers into Simulation and World, and inspected associated existing tests. Read the repository operating instructions, wiki schema/index, domain vocabulary, reusable-layer guide and `pasm/spec/architecture/reusable-layers.yaml`. No nested `AGENTS.md` appeared in the crates search; `docs/adr` does not exist in this source checkout. No builds or tests were executed, and no source was edited.

The savings below count implementation deletion after replacement, not movement to another package, removal of explanatory documentation, or loss of tests. Estimates exclude any additional boundary tests that implementation may warrant.

1. **Share the identical cadence-ratio calculation while retaining the two domain-facing methods.**

   **Evidence and callers:** `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/scene.rs:300` (`checked_snapshot_every_ticks`) and `:328` (`checked_sim_ticks_per_ai_tick`) repeat the same finite/positive input checks, division, rounding, minimum-one check, epsilon comparison and `u32` conversion. Only the selected pair of configuration fields differs. World admission uses them separately at `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/config.rs:2631` and `:2697`, allowing distinct authored-field errors. Runtime cadence reads their default-applying wrappers at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/ai/cadence.rs:106` and `:107`.

   **Opportunity:** Keep both named methods and their documentation, but have them call one private helper accepting the two `f32` rates. This removes one maintained copy of the numeric policy without making the admission or runtime caller know how rates are checked. The two current methods occupy 28 implementation lines together; two three-line wrappers and one approximately 14-line helper would occupy about 20. Allowing spacing, this is roughly **7–8 net implementation lines deleted**.

   **Invariants:** Preserve operand order, `f32` arithmetic, the existing `SNAPSHOT_RATIO_EPSILON`, rounding sequence and conversion behavior exactly. Preserve the wrappers' independently selected defaults and the parser's separate field-specific diagnostics. Do not fold `checked_autosave_interval_ticks` (`scene.rs:269`) into this helper: it multiplies widened `f64` values, requires an exact integral result and checks a `u64` bound. That is a different contract, not a third copy of the cadence ratio.

   **Verification:** Existing parser tests at `crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/world/config_tests.rs:277` and `:294` check the two commensurability rules, including a valid alternative ratio and the default relationship. Floor/ceiling coverage follows at `:328` and `:355`. Retain these integration tests. A helper extraction should additionally pin both wrappers against the same table of zero, negative, nonfinite and near-epsilon inputs; the inspected cases do not establish complete epsilon-boundary coverage. No change to scheduling registration is needed.

   **Tradeoff, confidence and limitations:** High confidence in the duplication and narrow extraction; modest savings only. This is worthwhile as removal of duplicate numerical policy, not as a reason to introduce a general cadence subsystem or change questionable edge behavior during the refactor.

2. **Remove the private allocating flag-chain resolver and resolve directly in the borrowed-chain reader.**

   **Evidence and callers:** `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/flags.rs:151` defines private `resolve_chain`, which parses the parent prefixes, finds a borrowed store and allocates a `String` for the remaining key. Its only implementation caller is `counter_in_chain` at `:176`, which immediately borrows that allocated string to call `FlagStore::counter`. `counter_in_owned_chain` at `:192` already performs the shorter equivalent lookup using `strip_parent_prefixes` and a borrowed remainder. `flag_in_chain` at `:198` delegates to the borrowed counter reader. Predicate evaluation calls it at `:957`; direct consumers include `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/debug/scenario.rs:275` and `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/console/weapons/torpedo.rs:431`.

   **Opportunity:** Implement `counter_in_chain` using the same two statements as the owned-chain reader: split the prefixes, then `chain.get(depth).map(|s| s.counter(rest)).unwrap_or(0)`. Delete private `resolve_chain`; update the shared-prefix helper's reference to that deleted function. There is no reason to manufacture a generic chain trait just to remove the final two visibly identical lookup statements. The private helper has seven implementation lines; changing the caller's four-line match to two statements removes two more. This yields about **9 implementation lines deleted**, with a removed allocation on every borrowed lookup. Retain the parent-chain contract documentation at the public reader if necessary rather than counting its removal as savings.

   **Invariants:** Current layer first; no outward fallback for an absent unprefixed name; each `parent:` advances exactly one layer; past-root and missing counters read zero; boolean views remain `counter != 0`. Owned snapshots must remain a supported independent calling shape. Removing the allocating intermediate does not change ownership of stores or expose mutable access.

   **Verification:** `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/flags_tests.rs:121`, `:131` and `:142` cover one parent, multiple parents and traversal beyond the root. Predicate parent lookup is covered at `:299`. Especially useful is `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/world/server_tests.rs:6348`, which compares owned and borrowed readers and checks distinct originating chains through `:6391`. Keep those tests; check an empty chain and a missing local key explicitly if not covered by the full existing test table.

   **Tradeoff, confidence and limitations:** High confidence. This is a genuine helper deletion rather than a relocation, and the existing owned-reader implementation supplies an exact in-repository model. The two public entry points remain because borrowed predicates and owned script snapshots are deliberately different ownership surfaces.

3. **Use one private comparison implementation for integer counters and floating-point facts.**

   **Evidence and callers:** `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/flags.rs:216` and `:230` implement identical six-arm operator matches in `CmpOp::apply(i64, i64)` and `apply_f64(f64, f64)`. The first is called by counter evaluation at `:957`; the second by fact and history evaluation at `:974` and `:996`. Both are private. Rendering uses the independent `symbol` method at `:242`, which is not duplicate evaluation logic.

   **Opportunity:** Make the private comparison method generic over `T: PartialOrd`, retain the six ordinary operators in one match, and call it from all three sites. `PartialOrd` supplies the required `PartialEq` relationship; there is no need for a custom numeric trait or common numeric representation. Deleting the second ten-line method while modifying the first signature produces roughly **10 implementation lines of net deletion**. Keep a short explanation that integer and real-valued operands retain their own representations.

   **Invariants:** Never cast counters to `f64`: values beyond exact floating-point integer precision must continue to compare as integers. Do not replace the operator match with a total-order comparison or `partial_cmp(...).unwrap()`: native floating-point equality/inequality and unordered-value behavior must survive. Missing facts and unresolved parameters must still be rejected by the callers before comparison.

   **Verification:** `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/flags_tests.rs:179` covers parsing all six operators, `:267` evaluates integer comparisons, `:432` checks parameterized fact evaluation, and history comparison cases begin at `:813`. Parsing all six operators is not itself evaluation coverage. Add or extend a compact comparison table to evaluate all operators for integer and float operands, with a large integer and unordered float case if directly testing the helper. Retain absent-fact and unresolved-parameter tests at `:457` and `:465`.

   **Tradeoff, confidence and limitations:** High confidence that the duplicated bodies can be shared without changing semantics; medium priority because the saving is small and a generic signature is slightly less immediately concrete. If maintainers prefer explicit typed methods, retaining thin wrappers would reduce the savings and probably make this change less worthwhile. No public API change or cross-package abstraction is needed.

**Examined candidates that should not be counted as deletion opportunities**

- `SimStream::EntityUuid` is explicitly retired but format-preserving (`crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/sim_rng.rs:122`). `SimRngState` carries a generator per `ALL` entry, and restoration rejects a different count (`:349`). Removing this apparently unused stream changes persisted state and digest positions. Its retained declaration is justified; it is not dead code available for a line-count cleanup.
- The aggregate and live RNG/mint interfaces are not interchangeable duplication. `world_id.rs:434` checks live handle identity, and its installation and fixed-step synchronization preserve cell ownership and tick order; the RNG module follows the same restricted-live-access pattern. Dedicated tests in `world_id_live_namespace_tests.rs` and `sim_rng_live_stream_tests.rs` exercise actual live/aggregate state sharing and stale installation failure. Replacing those independently typed resources with an undifferentiated common state bag would undermine the scheduling contract. No savings claimed.
- `SimOutbox::extend_snapshot` and `extend_reliable` (`lifecycle.rs:60`, `:70`) repeat a short mapping, but their explicit public class choice is an admission-to-delivery guarantee. A private generic extension helper adds enough signature/body overhead to make the net deletion small or nonexistent; pushing individually through existing methods can also change allocation behavior. Not recommended solely for maintained-line reduction.
- The authored Directive adapter preserves distinct World and doctrine field names (`directive.rs:1`, `:146`). The vocabulary, required-field checks and surface-aware diagnostics are already centralized. Deriving enum display/iteration could shorten some declarations, but a source-level review must preserve constant APIs, stable names and ordering; this inspection does not establish a worthwhile net deletion. No speculative savings counted.
- Moving shared Contracts definitions into Gameplay, World or Session would reduce this directory's count while adding forbidden sibling imports or duplicate definitions elsewhere. PASM explicitly makes these independent branches and Contracts their shared seam (`pasm/spec/architecture/reusable-layers.yaml:13`). That is movement or architecture regression, not deletion.

**Coverage limits**

This was a focused source audit, not an exhaustive symbol-use census of every public contract. The largest parser/visitor tail of `flags.rs`, all logging macro expansion behavior, the complete directive diagnostic matrix, full authoritative census/alias implementation, and every consumer of the small data-only modules were not traced exhaustively. Small modules were inventoried; their contents are not represented as fully audited. Existing RNG/mint tests were inspected selectively, not rerun. No dependency-removal claim, serialized-format migration claim, or cross-target verification result is made. The bounded opportunities above require no new dependency, no test deletion, no domain merge and no change to deterministic scheduling.
