Scope: `crates/phoenix-presentation/`


Audit 25/72 · Agent: `/root/audit25_presentation_overengineering` · Lens: overengineering

### OVERENGINEERING

Module: 3D Presentation (`crates/phoenix-presentation/`). Audited source: `C:/Users/jkeyw/.codex/worktrees/reusable-game-layers/project-phoenix-v2`, baseline `07fc2e025bb2f5385b2fadf4fb468039b77c0b4e`; the subsequent documented HEAD changes only navigation documentation.

Scope inspected: crate manifest and public modules; presentation registration; shared render setup; procedural mesh/material caching and LOD retirement; visual fade implementation and test names; pack asset reader and captured-preview entry point; viewer command dispatch, lighting, subject reload, and billboard capture; native render readback. Caller tracing covered the Workshop preview runtime/panel/session, native Workshop billboard adapter, capture/tuning binaries, and native readback integration tests. Orientation included AGENTS.md, wiki schema/index, domain vocabulary, reusable layers, model-preview wiki, and presentation-loading, T4-presentation, and Workshop model-authoring PASM slices. No nested AGENTS.md was found under the presentation crate; `docs/adr` was absent. This is static inspection: no builds, tests, GPU work, or source changes were performed.

#### 1. The retained viewer still contains an unused mutable-asset and browser-baking workflow

**Evidence and callers.** `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/viewer/mod.rs:119` retains `SetLadder`, `ReloadAssets`, and `CaptureBillboard` command variants. Their dispatch at lines 355–393 replaces the live ladder, requests asynchronous reloads, or creates a browser capture request. The exported ladder begin/push/commit transaction starts at line 521; reload is exported at line 576 and five capture bridge functions start at line 597. `viewer/subject.rs:113` retains a `reloading` flag and `viewer/subject.rs:387` consumes Scene modification events to rebuild after explicit reload. `viewer/capture.rs:79` defines the Position/WaitRender/WaitShot state machine, line 105 starts it, line 198 drives it, and lines 305–347 publish an atlas through thread-local storage and polling getters. The plugin unconditionally registers the capture resources and three capture systems whenever the viewer feature is used (`viewer/mod.rs:180`, `viewer/mod.rs:201`).

The current supported caller has different semantics. `editor/workshop-preview-runtime.js:127` boots a disposable captured-source preview. Its entire command switch at lines 140–150 supports LOD selection, lighting, gizmos, distance, and camera; it does not call any of the above ladder/reload/capture exports. `editor/workshop-model-preview.js:26` refreshes by capture/start, and source changes mark the existing picture stale at line 56. `gui/workshop-model-preview-panel.js:5` explicitly documents deliberate Refresh as the boundary for replacing immutable input. Repository-wide searches for the exact export names found their definitions and internal references only, not production or test consumers. This agrees with `pasm/spec/architecture/workshop-model-authoring.yaml:19` and `wiki/concepts/model-viewer.md:12`: the retained ViewerPlugin is shared rendering infrastructure, while Workshop owns authoring and captured revision lifecycle.

Native production billboard generation has a real alternative caller path: `src/native_host/workshop/billboard_capture.rs:166` launches `scripts/capture-billboards.mjs`, through the native job/adoption owner; the bridge dispatches it at `src/native_host/workshop/bridge.rs:358`. Removing browser baking would therefore not remove native Workshop capture.

**Why it matters.** This is more than unused convenience functions: it preserves a second capture lifecycle, offscreen camera state machine, atlas publication state, an independently mutable ladder transaction, and asset reload state inside every preview. Maintainers must still understand these branches when changing subject lifecycle, ECS resources or rendering APIs. Capture systems are scheduled even though supported Workshop controls cannot request a capture. No claim is made about measurable frame-time or bundle-size impact; neither was measured.

**Concrete opportunity / deletion test.** Retire the uncalled ladder-draft, reload, and browser capture exports and their exclusive state/systems, retaining ordinary ladder selection and preview rendering. Remove `respawn_on_asset_reload` only together with its sole triggering reload command. Remove the browser capture module only after checking whether the intentionally retained public WASM exports are a promised external compatibility surface. The comments at `viewer/capture.rs:328` explicitly call this a “retained wasm preview capture API”; that is a reason to verify policy, not evidence of a current in-repository consumer. If compatibility is required, isolate the legacy workflow behind an explicit opt-in rather than attaching it to every supported preview.

**Invariants to preserve.** Captured assets must remain immutable and fail closed for absent files (`viewer/preview.rs:61`); model/entity dispatch must keep sharing the game's constructors; Auto/Base/fixed LOD, camera framing, statistics and ordinary rendering must remain. Native capture's exact-source staging, cancellation and reviewed adoption must remain untouched. This finding does not propose removing the viewer feature or its independent server-free build.

**Tests and missing verification.** The current controls and stale-refresh semantics are exercised by `tests/client/workshop-model-preview-panel.test.js:23` and the stale-source case at line 41. The declared integration proof is `tests/smoke/workshop-preview.render.spec.js`; native job behavior also has `editor/tests/workshop-billboard-capture.test.js` and `src/native_host/workshop/billboard_capture_tests.rs`. The inspected viewer root test (`viewer/tests.rs:4`) only asserts the default model path, and no caller for the legacy capture exports was found in tests. Before deletion, inventory published WASM API compatibility and run existing preview/native capture checks; do not replace them with tests that merely assert removed symbols are absent.

**Tradeoff, confidence and limits.** High confidence that these workflows have no current in-repository caller; medium confidence that immediate deletion is permitted because exports are public and explicitly labelled retained. This is a bounded removal candidate, not an assertion that external users cannot call the exports.

#### 2. Native readback carries an enable/disable synchronization mechanism with no disabling operation

**Evidence and callers.** `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/render_capture.rs:193` stores a private `enabled: Arc<AtomicBool>` in `ImageCopier`. Its only initialization is `AtomicBool::new(true)` at line 224. The private getter at line 227 performs a relaxed atomic read; both the render-graph copy loop (line 254) and buffer mapping loop (line 297) branch on it. There is no store, setter, public access, or other state transition. Extracting copiers clones the Arc every frame at line 233.

Actual users construct and register copiers: `src/bin/capture_billboard.rs:150`, `src/bin/tune_lods.rs:342`, `tests/native_viewscreen_render.rs:133`, and `tests/native_pane_upload_gpu.rs:158`. The private field prevents these callers from toggling it. Consequently the disabled branch is unreachable in the inspected implementation.

**Why it matters / deletion test.** Delete the field, allocation, getter, atomic imports, and two guards: all currently constructible copiers retain exactly their current always-enabled behavior. The current machinery implies a cross-world pause protocol that does not exist, adding reference-count traffic, atomic reads, and a conceptual lifecycle state without serving any caller. This is a small cleanup; it is not a claim of significant rendering performance improvement.

**Invariants to preserve.** Keep extraction of actual copier components, render-graph ordering after the camera driver, correct row padding, mapping completion, channel delivery, and buffer unmapping. This is not a recommendation to delete the shared native readback abstraction: it has multiple concrete production and test callers.

**Tests and missing verification.** `tests/native_viewscreen_render.rs:125` tests a non-flat captured native frame; `tests/native_pane_upload_gpu.rs:127` checks successive uploads into the same persistent texture using the same copier. These cover live behavior rather than an enable API, which currently cannot be exercised. Their GPU checks were read, not run. A removal should preserve them; no artificial disabled-state test is warranted unless a real pause feature is introduced.

**Tradeoff, confidence and limits.** High confidence. Removing this machinery gives up only an unimplemented future pause hook. If a future caller needs pausing, add that contract with both render-graph and readback coordination at that time.

#### 3. Every viewer command explicitly invalidates lighting, rebuilding lights for camera and gizmo operations

**Evidence and callers.** At `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/viewer/mod.rs:396`, `apply_commands` calls `lighting.set_changed()` after every command, including `SetGizmos` (line 289), camera-distance changes (line 320), and full camera changes (line 331). `viewer/lighting.rs:91` tests that change bit, then despawns all `ViewerLight` entities and respawns ambient/directional lights (lines 94–96 and 132–155), updates celestial lighting and emits an info log. The real panel supplies repeated camera commands through its directional buttons (`gui/workshop-model-preview-panel.js:66`) and the runtime switch (`editor/workshop-preview-runtime.js:143`).

**Why it matters / deletion test.** A blanket invalidation creates unnecessary ECS lifecycle work and couples unrelated preview controls to lighting reconstruction. Bevy's `ResMut` assignment already marks actual lighting writes changed: the lighting, ambient and directional command arms mutate that resource at `viewer/mod.rs:273–282`. Remove the unconditional `set_changed()` and rely on those real writes. This does not require a new event bus, dirty-bit protocol or light manager. Replacing lights when lighting genuinely changes is a separate small design choice and is not itself established as overengineering here.

**Invariants to preserve.** Initial resource insertion must still apply initial lighting; lighting mode/parameter commands must still update both Bevy lights and `PlanetLightingOverride`. Skybox brightness updates should continue to operate directly on the skybox. Camera/gizmo/LOD commands must retain their original effects and should not recreate otherwise unchanged lights.

**Tests and missing verification.** `viewer/lighting_tests.rs:4`, line 10 and line 16 cover mode parsing and default ambient parity, but do not assert system-level invalidation or light entity stability. A narrow ECS regression should apply initial lighting, issue a non-lighting command, and verify the existing lights remain, then issue a genuine lighting change and verify both ordinary and celestial lighting update. Existing preview render smoke remains useful for appearance parity. None of these checks were run in this audit.

**Tradeoff, confidence and limits.** High confidence in unnecessary reconstruction; no measured FPS benefit is claimed. Initial change detection and actual light edits must be verified when making the one-line removal. This finding is independent of retiring legacy commands in finding 1: supported camera and gizmo commands already demonstrate it.

#### Inspected complexity justified by current requirements

- `registration.rs:27` orders visual creation, LOD selection, fade driving and billboard orientation deliberately. Billboard alpha combines pose weights and current-frame fade alpha; collapsing that ordering would change rendered behavior. The authoritative marker/readiness split in the presentation-loading PASM also prevents treating visual LOD state as authoritative geometry.
- `server_app_render.rs:95–126` separates geometry and material cache keys so repeated primitives share handles. Real rendering and the viewer use this helper; deleting the cache would lose sharing/batching opportunities. Its presence is not evidence of speculative abstraction.
- `entities/pack_assets.rs:30` and `viewer/preview.rs:61` bind disposable previews to captured bytes. This is an enforced source boundary, not an unnecessary provider layer. Ordinary fallback reads and captured fail-closed reads have different contracts.
- Native render readback is shared by two tools and integration tests. Browser screenshot readback and native buffer mapping are not automatically redundant implementations simply because both return pixels.
- `lib.rs:4` and `entities/mod.rs:1` retain compatibility re-exports across the package extraction. No removal is recommended merely because imports pass through those facades.

#### Coverage limits

This is a targeted overengineering audit, not exhaustive review of the entire presentation crate. Particle effects and dust, radar projection/UI, audio lifecycle, debug/performance overlays, procedural planet texture generation, complete GLB material handling, and native/WebGL flare/occlusion internals were not inspected deeply enough for findings. Asset preloading was considered through its PASM and registration ordering rather than every discovery branch. Existing test sources were sampled for the specific findings; neither rendering output nor native/WASM execution parity was experimentally verified.


Audit 26/72 · Agent: `/root/audit26_presentation_reuse` · Lens: reuse

### Reuse

Module: **3D Presentation** (`crates/phoenix-presentation/`). Source inspected in `C:/Users/jkeyw/.codex/worktrees/reusable-game-layers/project-phoenix-v2`, against the specified source baseline `07fc2e025bb2f5385b2fadf4fb468039b77c0b4e` (the later HEAD change is documentation only).

Scope: focused inspection of the game/Workshop preview reuse boundary: `lib.rs`, `registration.rs`, `render_setup.rs`, `render_capture.rs`, `server_app_render.rs`, `entities/{mod,glb_visual,celestial_visual,mesh_stats}.rs`, `viewer/{mod,preview,subject,lod,camera,lighting,stats,capture}.rs`, corresponding LOD/GLB/render/stats tests, and the comparison implementation in `perf/mesh.rs`. Caller checks included `editor/workshop-preview-runtime.js`, native capture/tuning binaries, repository codec helpers and authored hull rigs. Read the wiki schema, vocabulary, index, reusable-layer guide, operating constraints, and relevant presentation/loading, viewscreen and reusable-layer PASM declarations. No nested presentation `AGENTS.md` was found; `docs/adr` is absent.

This was read-only inspection. No builds, tests, GPU rendering, or browser sessions were run. Findings distinguish observed source divergence from visually reproduced failure.

#### 1. Generated-tier rig composition is repeated at call sites, and the preview has missed the game's orientation fix

**Evidence and callers.** Both game and preview correctly call the shared `spawn_glb_visual` constructor in `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/entities/glb_visual.rs:203`. That constructor applies the supplied rig's complete base transform to the scene child (`glb_visual.rs:279`). However, the callers do not supply equivalent rigs:

- `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/server_app_render.rs:642` obtains the tier declaration, then explicitly handles a declared identity tier by cloning `lods.base_rig` and setting only its scale to unity (`server_app_render.rs:649`). The source explains why: absence of a generated sidecar must not discard the primary hull's rotation and offset. It passes that inherited rig to the shared spawn at `server_app_render.rs:654`.
- `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/viewer/subject.rs:275` obtains the same declaration but passes its identity rig directly to `spawn_glb_visual` at `subject.rs:280`. The primary rig was already resolved earlier in the same system (`subject.rs:183`), so the missing input is available.
- `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/entities/glb_visual.rs:152` implements `declared_tier_rig` as a default rig for `TierRig::Identity`; it deliberately does not know the primary rig.
- This is exercised by real authored content: `assets/models/alliance_destroyer.model.toml:3` declares a Y rotation of `3.14`, and its generated tiers declare identity rigs at lines 78 and 89. `assets/models/alliance_cruiser.model.toml:3`, 81 and 93 use the same pattern.
- The preview is live application code: `viewer/preview.rs:23` boots `ViewerPlugin`; its subject and LOD systems are registered by `viewer/mod.rs:169`. `editor/workshop-preview-runtime.js:1` describes and drives that disposable captured-draft preview.

**Why it matters.** Sharing the final scene constructor has not removed the duplicated composition policy immediately before it. For a generated identity tier, the preview discards orientation/offset that the game preserves. The size-compensation code is shared, so a correct preview size does not imply a correct preview pose. This defeats the preview's role as a reference for the actual game visual and can mislead an author changing a rig to correct what the preview shows.

**Concrete opportunity.** Move the existing game-side rule for constructing a generated tier's effective visual rig into the shared GLB visual helpers, and use it from both callers. Preserve `declared_tier_rig`'s role as a sidecar declaration if other callers need that narrower answer; do not change every identity declaration globally to an inherited rig without inspecting those callers. Reuse the already-resolved primary rig in the preview rather than introducing another fetch or another cache.

**Required invariants.** Identity tiers still request no absent sidecar. Non-identity/baked tiers retain their own sidecar resolution. The primary scale must appear exactly once through the existing `tier_parent_scale_at` convention. Preserve base offset and rotation on the visual child, keep authoritative transforms and markers unchanged, and retain pending/failed asset outcomes. No simulation dependency on rendering is needed.

**Existing verification and gap.** `viewer/lod_tests.rs:417` computes composed **scale**, and tests at lines 446 and 469 cover hull and pipeline conventions. They do not spawn and compare the effective rotation and offset of a generated GLB tier. `entities/glb_visual_tests.rs:103` and 166 cover identity declarations and skipped sidecar reads; `server_app_render_tests.rs:96` protects authoritative geometry across LOD transitions. Add a shared-rule test with nonzero offset, nonidentity rotation and nonunit scale, plus a caller-level comparison of actual preview/game scene-child transforms for an identity tier. A baked-tier counterpart should ensure there is no double transformation.

**Tradeoff, confidence and limitations.** High confidence in the source divergence and current-content relevance; no visual reproduction was performed. A small shared composition helper is warranted here because the duplicated rule has already diverged. Consolidating the entire preview and runtime LOD state machines would be excessive: they differ in fixed-tier controls, authoritative parent ownership and cross-fade lifecycle.

#### 2. Viewer payloads hand-implement JSON although the repository already exposes and uses the permitted serializer seam

**Evidence and callers.** `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/viewer/stats.rs:183` manually assembles the stats object, optional level and nested camera object with `format!`. Its comment cites the rule confining direct `serde_json` use to `codec.rs` as the reason. `viewer/capture.rs:305` does the same for capture metadata and at line 312 claims Rust debug formatting of a path supplies a JSON string. The existing sanctioned implementation is `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/core/codec.rs:4`, `to_json<T: Serialize>`, which wraps `serde_json::to_string`.

This is not a hypothetical new dependency or a proposed bypass of the codec rule. The presentation crate already uses that helper in `server/audio.rs:307`, `server/viewscreen_border.rs:439`, and `perf/native_frames.rs:154`; it already depends on `serde` with derive support (`crates/phoenix-presentation/Cargo.toml:27`). The stats payload has a live consumer that executes `JSON.parse` at `editor/workshop-preview-runtime.js:59`.

**Why it matters.** Manual JSON repeats escaping, optional-value and numeric encoding responsibilities already implemented at the repository seam. Rust `Debug` string escaping is not JSON encoding: some control characters become Rust escapes such as `\0` or `\u{...}` rather than JSON escapes. Ordinary asset paths may never contain these characters, so this is not claimed as a reproduced current-content failure. Manual float interpolation can also produce non-JSON nonfinite tokens if such state arrives. The stronger unconditional finding is maintained parallel serialization logic with an incorrect justification, despite an established reusable helper in the same crate.

**Concrete opportunity.** Represent the existing wire payloads with small `Serialize` structs and call `crate::core::codec::to_json`. Keep the existing camelCase field names using serde renames and model optional level/camera values as `Option`. Preserve the capture buffer's ownership/take behavior; only metadata construction needs changing. Do not import `serde_json` directly into these modules.

**Required invariants.** Preserve field names, numeric types, `null` level/camera semantics, mode names, stats readiness and the JS polling boundary. Stats currently round distance/extent to two decimals and camera values to four; decide explicitly whether that quantization is part of the payload behavior and preserve it numerically if required. A serializer does not itself establish the desired policy for nonfinite values; validate or normalize them deliberately rather than silently treating null as a usable measurement.

**Existing verification and gap.** `viewer/stats_tests.rs:4` and 31 test selected string fragments, including two-decimal formatting. Replace or supplement these with decoding through `codec::from_json` and assertions about the payload contract, including optional camera and level. Add escaping round-trips for metadata strings and explicit nonfinite handling. No capture metadata sibling test was present in the inspected viewer inventory.

**Tradeoff, confidence and limitations.** High confidence that the helper is available and is the intended local reuse seam. Typed payload structs may add rather than remove lines, but eliminate a separate serialization implementation. Stats is a current Workshop path. Capture exports remain registered (`viewer/mod.rs:597` onward), but no current editor/GUI caller of those capture exports was found during the caller search; the capture-specific failure risk should be treated as retained-API exposure rather than a demonstrated active production workflow.

#### 3. Browser capture repeats native capture's target/framing helpers; factor the shared subset without unifying readback backends

**Evidence and callers.** `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/render_capture.rs:58` constructs a transparent RGBA8 sRGB render target with texture-binding, copy-source and render-attachment usages. `viewer/capture.rs:129` repeats that image construction. The native function additionally allocates an `ImageCopier`, whereas the viewer uses Bevy's `Screenshot` observer at `viewer/capture.rs:227`.

The same split appears in camera math:

- `render_capture.rs:143` implements radius/FOV/margin framing; `viewer/camera.rs:65` and `viewer/capture.rs:149` repeat the formula with a margin of 1.8.
- `render_capture.rs:149` implements an orbit transform; `viewer/capture.rs:214` repeats its quaternion, position and `looking_at` sequence.
- The native helpers are already reused by `src/bin/capture_billboard.rs:181`, 341 and 355, and `src/bin/tune_lods.rs:382`, 1174 and 1202. This is an existing two-caller shared core with a third caller repeating its platform-neutral portion.

**Why it matters.** Format/usage changes and camera math fixes have two implementations to update. The duplicated browser framing also hardcodes `FRAC_PI_4` while attaching `game_camera_projection()` (`viewer/capture.rs:148` and 165); normal viewer framing reads the actual `Projection` (`viewer/camera.rs:61`). A future shared projection change could therefore change the rendered FOV without changing the browser capture's fit calculation. This last point is a prospective drift risk, not a claim that the current FOVs differ.

**Concrete opportunity.** Make the existing image-construction and pure framing/orbit subset available from an always-compiled presentation helper, then have the native `create_render_target` add its copier on top and the browser capture add its screenshot workflow. Use the projection actually supplied to the capture camera when obtaining the framing FOV. Keep margins, radius clamps and center selection as explicit caller policy.

**Compatibility boundaries.** The entire native module cannot simply be imported into the viewer: `lib.rs:11` gates it behind native `capture`, and `Cargo.toml:10` activates the optional crossbeam dependency for that feature. The shared subset must not make WebGL2 depend on native readback or require the native capture feature. Native bounds come from measured transformed AABBs (`render_capture.rs:113`), whereas this viewer capture starts from `SubjectState.extents` at the origin (`viewer/capture.rs:119` and 186). Native capture uses a 1.25 margin and tuning uses 1.4, compared with the viewer's 1.8. Those are real policy differences, not duplicate constants to collapse. Normal interactive orbit directly assigns quaternion rotation (`viewer/camera.rs:108`), unlike the capture helper's `looking_at`; only reuse that portion after preserving pole and orientation behavior.

**Existing verification and gap.** `tests/native_pane_upload_gpu.rs:52` and `tests/native_viewscreen_render.rs:61` already consume the shared native readback core. They do not prove a browser screenshot matches the shared image descriptor or framing. Add targeted tests for the common image descriptor and orbit/framing calculations, preserving non-square dimensions, minimum dimensions and caller-selected margins. Do not require running a GPU test merely to validate the arithmetic extraction; a real capture check remains appropriate if changing backend interaction.

**Tradeoff, confidence and limitations.** High confidence in the exact target/orbit duplication; medium priority because the duplicated blocks are modest. The benefit is one implementation of stable mechanics, not a new generalized camera subsystem. Retained browser capture API activity is limited as noted in finding 2. Keep asynchronous native polling and browser screenshot state machines distinct unless separate evidence establishes compatible lifecycle behavior.

#### Existing reuse and superficially similar code that should remain separate

- **Visual construction is already substantially shared.** `viewer/subject.rs:205` uses `server_app_render::procedural_mesh_material`; its billboard branch at line 241 calls `entities::billboard::spawn_billboard_child`; GLB creation uses `spawn_glb_visual`; celestial entity previews call `insert_star_visual` and `insert_planet_visual` (`entities/celestial_visual.rs:16` and 50). Recommending another parallel renderer or replacing the preview with a generic model loader would undo useful reuse.
- **Shared rendering defaults are intentional.** `viewer/mod.rs:248` and `server/renderer.rs:408` both call `render_setup::apply_render_config`. `render_setup.rs:227` centralizes HDR, tone mapping and the WebGL bloom platform gate. `viewer/lighting.rs:59` obtains the game ambient default, while retaining authoring-specific selectable lighting. The UI camera helper `apply_target_hdr` at `render_setup.rs:283` deliberately applies only the shared target format, not a second tone-map/bloom pass. These are different responsibilities, not two versions of camera configuration to merge wholesale.
- **Measurement callers ask different questions.** `entities/mesh_stats.rs:25` and 40 already provide shared triangle/pixel counting. `viewer/stats.rs:60` counts material references on the displayed subject, including celestial materials. `perf/mesh.rs` attributes loader-produced assets to source GLBs. Replacing both with one traversal would conflate on-screen cost and file inventory. No finding is raised for the differing traversals.
- **Preview fallback values are not automatically duplicate runtime defaults.** `viewer/lod.rs:276` has no entity configuration for a model-only preview and uses a stand-in radius derived from extents; `server_app_render.rs:692` inherits the entity's authored mesh fields. These inputs intentionally differ. Reuse their final geometry constructor, already done, rather than forcing the preview to invent authoritative entity state.
- **Compatibility facades are not duplicate implementations.** The simulation re-exports in `lib.rs:4` and `entities/mod.rs:1` preserve paths while presentation owns the GPU-specific modules. Their presence alone is not grounds for deletion or a reverse dependency.

#### Coverage limitations and unexplored areas

The full crate is larger than the inspected rendering/preview seam. No claim of exhaustive reuse coverage is made for audio and its lifecycle, particle effects, radar/UI projection, asset discovery/preload traversal, planet texture decoding, pack/versioned asset readers, visual fading, native/WebGL flare and occlusion implementations, debug overlays, or detailed WGSL shaders. Those modules were inventoried or consulted only where a caller/reference was relevant. Build-script and manifest dependency cleanup was not audited. Native capture/tuning binaries were inspected as concrete helper callers, not comprehensively reviewed. All proposed verification is future work; this audit changed only its assigned report.


Audit 27/72 · Agent: `/root/audit27_presentation_architecture` · Lens: architecture

### Architecture

Module: **3D Presentation**. Scope: `crates/phoenix-presentation/`, with focused caller tracing into host composition and authored model rigs. Source inspected in `C:/Users/jkeyw/.codex/worktrees/reusable-game-layers/project-phoenix-v2` at the audit's specified code baseline `07fc2e025bb2f5385b2fadf4fb468039b77c0b4e` (HEAD's subsequent changes are documentation only).

Read the architecture skill and LANGUAGE.md, repository instructions, wiki schema/index, domain vocabulary, reusable-layers guide, Workshop preview orientation, and relevant presentation/loading, Viewscreen, reusable-layer and T4 PASM material. There is no `docs/adr/` directory in this source checkout, and the nested AGENTS.md search under `crates/` returned none. Inspected crate declarations, registration, GLB composition, game LOD rendering, viewer subject/LOD composition and corresponding tests; sampled pack revision invalidation, render setup and celestial construction. This is a focused architecture audit, not exhaustive coverage of the crate. No builds, tests, GPU sessions or network research were run.

1. **Deepen the model-tier composition module so the game and Workshop do not independently interpret the primary rig.**

   **Files:** `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/entities/glb_visual.rs:91`, `:151`, `:174`, `:203`; `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/server_app_render.rs:602`, `:634`; `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/viewer/lod.rs:276`; `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/viewer/subject.rs:179`, `:267`; `assets/models/alliance_cruiser.model.toml:2`, `:81`.

   **Problem and observed callers:** The shared GLB module already hides asset acquisition and child creation. Its tier helpers also answer useful questions about compensation scale and declared absence of generated sidecars. However, its interface leaves the semantic composition of the primary rig and generated tier to each caller. In the game's `update_mesh_lod`, a declared identity tier is explicitly interpreted as “do not fetch a sidecar,” while preserving the primary rig's offset and rotation: lines 649–652 clone `lods.base_rig` and replace only scale with unity. In Workshop, `poll_pending_model` obtains `declared_tier_rig` and passes that default rig directly to `spawn_glb_visual` at lines 275–288. `showing_for` carries path, variant and scale for a GLB, not the missing primary orientation or offset. The previously resolved `primary_rig` is used for markers and extents, not to supply this transform. Thus the same declared identity tier is composed differently by the two adapters.

   This difference is concrete in the inspected implementation, not merely a risk of future duplication. The Alliance Cruiser primary rig has offset `[0, -0.2, 0.1]` and rotation `[0, 3.14, 0]`, and its generated tiers declare identity. The game's explicit inherited transform and the viewer's identity transform therefore disagree for those tiers. This is especially consequential because Workshop is intended to show the same visual construction as the Viewscreen. No GPU observation is claimed; the discrepancy is established by the transforms each path passes to shared construction.

   **Solution opportunity:** Concentrate the meaning of primary-rig plus selected-tier composition inside the model visual module. Game and Workshop should retain their distinct choices about which tier to show, camera control, local-ship decoration, and replacement/fading policy, but should not each reconstruct the tier's resolved visual transform. This is a deepening opportunity, not a proposal to merge the whole viewer with the game renderer or to invent a general renderer interface.

   **Deletion test / depth:** Deleting `spawn_glb_visual` would redistribute real asynchronous and rig work across existing callers, so it earns its place. Deleting the caller-owned interpretation of identity tiers by moving that knowledge behind its existing seam removes a semantic responsibility from both callers. Merely extracting the game-only `clone` into another helper without having both callers use the same interpretation would not improve depth. There are already two real adapters here: live game presentation and Workshop presentation.

   **Benefits:** Better locality for the rule that determines what a generated hull looks like; greater leverage from the shared module because callers receive complete composition rather than a set of arithmetic ingredients. A correction to orientation, offset, variant fallback or sidecar convention would have one owning implementation and one common test surface.

   **Behavior and invariants to preserve:** Identity declarations suppress absent-sidecar requests; baked tiers retain their actual authored rig; undeclared mod-pack tiers keep the compatibility probe; near and generated tiers retain equal intended world scale; billboard dimensions remain world-size declarations rather than taking GLB compensation. Authoritative primary `ModelMarkers` must remain independent of active visual LOD. Neither the shared constructor nor the game adapter may rewrite simulation transforms. Workshop's camera/extents and gizmo behavior still belongs to its adapter.

   **Existing tests / missing verification:** `entities/glb_visual_tests.rs:103`, `:123`, `:141`, `:166` cover declaration and sidecar rules; `:247` checks shipped declarations against assets. `viewer/lod_tests.rs:446`, `:469`, `:486` check size consistency for hull and pipeline ladders. These scale-focused tests do not establish complete offset/rotation parity between adapters. `entities/glb_visual_tests.rs:321` tests that spawning cannot replace authoritative markers, and `server_app_render_tests.rs:96` tests rendererless authority across a non-unit transition. Preserve those protections and add an interface-level, renderer-free composition regression with nonzero primary offset/rotation for both identity and baked generated tiers. It should compare resulting visual transforms across the actual game and viewer routes, not only assert the return value of the current scale helper.

   **Tradeoffs, confidence and limitations:** High confidence in the duplicated interpretation and transform discrepancy. A deeper module must avoid absorbing the viewer's deliberate authoring fallbacks or game-only fade lifecycle. Full appearance, lighting and captured-pixel correctness remain unverified. No replacement interface is designed in this audit.

2. **Make the asynchronous visual-loading module own request identity, rather than requiring callers to keep a pending handle aligned with a changing tier.**

   **Files:** `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/entities/glb_visual.rs:21`, `:203`, `:213`, `:232`; `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/server_app_render.rs:524`, `:554`, `:582`, `:654`, `:690`; `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/viewer/subject.rs:169`, `:280`; `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/server_app_render.rs:37`.

   **Problem and observed callers:** `PendingSceneHandle` stores only a scene handle. The spawn interface also accepts a model path, variant and possibly pre-resolved rig, but when a pending handle exists it is reused unconditionally at line 214. There is no check that it is the scene for the requested path. Meanwhile the live LOD adapter recomputes its target from current camera distance every frame, leaves `lods.current` unchanged while the new scene is pending, and passes the same entity-level pending handle on its next call. The interface therefore relies on an unstated correspondence between the pending handle and these other arguments, a correspondence the live caller does not enforce.

   **Specific failure hypothesis:** Start with displayed tier C. Moving the camera starts loading tier B; before B resolves, the camera moves far enough to request tier A. The LOD driver now passes A's path and rig selection with B's pending handle. Once that handle is present in `Assets<Scene>`, the constructor can spawn B's scene using the rig associated with A and the driver can mark A current at line 687. A switch through a procedural or billboard tier can also retain the old pending component, because those branches do not consume or remove it. This is a source-derived execution hypothesis, not a reproduced runtime result. Viewer subject replacement gives its caller a different lifetime discipline, illustrating why an implicit per-parent convention is insufficient as the shared interface contract.

   **Solution opportunity:** Put ownership of “which requested visual is this load for?” and retirement of superseded pending work inside the asynchronous visual module. Its callers should choose the desired visual and replacement policy without having to synchronize an unlabelled handle with separately supplied model/rig arguments. Ensure non-GLB transitions also retire obsolete pending work through the same lifecycle. Keep the implementation bounded; this does not require a generic asset job framework or a new backend abstraction.

   **Deletion test / depth:** The pending handle performs necessary asset-lifetime work; deleting it would move retention complexity to every caller and risk losing in-flight assets. The opportunity is to deepen that existing module so identity and lifetime travel together, reducing what callers must know. A standalone “compare these arguments” helper that callers may forget would not remove the interface invariant.

   **Benefits:** Improved locality for stale completion handling and higher leverage from the spawn interface: one module can ensure a completion matches the requested model. Both the game and Workshop benefit, and tests can exercise request changes at that seam without a GPU or full host launch.

   **Behavior and invariants to preserve:** Keep the current visual while its replacement is pending; retain assets across frames; preserve the explicit permanent-failure behavior and settle semantics; do not mutate authoritative markers or simulation transforms. Pack revisions already invalidate visuals in `entities/pack_assets.rs:186` and clear pending handles through `server_app_render.rs:37`; retain that ordering and revision separation. A correction must not accidentally allow late old-pack results to reattach. Preserve game-specific fading and local-ship visibility decoration outside shared loading.

   **Existing tests / missing verification:** The scene materialization test at `entities/glb_visual_tests.rs:321` constructs a loaded scene and manually supplies a pending handle, demonstrating a suitable renderer-free test technique, but it exercises only one request and protects markers. The inspected game rendering tests cover authority and retirement, not successive asynchronous target changes. Add transition tests for pending B followed by A, reversal back to the currently displayed tier, pending GLB followed by a procedural or billboard tier and another GLB, failure of the superseded request, and pack revision invalidation. Assert the scene identity actually attached and the final selected tier, not only that an outcome is `Spawned`.

   **Tradeoffs, confidence and limitations:** High confidence in the missing identity check and live caller's changing arguments; medium confidence in user-visible frequency because async timings were not exercised. Carrying request identity creates some additional local state, but removes a more expensive distributed invariant. Asset cache retention and cancellation are different concerns: superseding the visible request need not cancel globally shared asset loading.

**Inspected structures that do not justify a refactor by themselves:** The crate's simulation re-exports (`crates/phoenix-presentation/src/lib.rs:4`, `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/entities/mod.rs:1`) are compatibility and layering scaffolding, not evidence for deletion. `src/server_app/mod.rs:52` calls presentation registration from host composition, so presentation registration is a real composition seam. `render_setup` and `celestial_visual` also share actual game/Workshop construction and pass the deletion test. The ordering in `registration.rs` between LOD changes, fades and billboard alpha is part of the rendering interface; collapsing it merely to shorten registration would conceal an important invariant. The frame-driven asset poll and fixed-tick mission transition must not be collapsed into one frame system.

**Unexplored areas / coverage limits:** Detailed camera arbitration, radar projection, audio playback and lifecycle, particle effects, web/native flare and occlusion adapters, debug overlays, complete planet texture streaming, capture readback, performance tooling, full shader implementation and full host boot matrix were not exhaustively inspected. Pack asset invalidation was inspected as context for loading lifetime, not audited as a complete content lifecycle. No claim is made that the two findings enumerate all architecture opportunities in this large crate. Existing test references are source evidence only, not claims that tests passed during this audit.

Module: **3D Presentation** (`crates/phoenix-presentation/`). Source root: `C:/Users/jkeyw/.codex/worktrees/reusable-game-layers/project-phoenix-v2`; audited source baseline `07fc2e025bb2f5385b2fadf4fb468039b77c0b4e` (the subsequent HEAD change is documentation-only).

#### 1. Delete the permanently disabled legacy dust renderer, retaining live preload and asset-lifecycle contracts

**Evidence and callers.** `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/server/pfx.rs:3992` defines `legacy_dust_enabled()` as an unconditional `false`, without any target or feature-dependent alternative. All four legacy dust update systems are registered behind that condition at `pfx.rs:322`: `tick_dust_state`, `spawn_dust_motes`, `move_dust_motes` and `sync_dust_materials`. Nevertheless, the plugin initializes `DustFieldState` at `pfx.rs:288` and installs `MaterialPlugin<DustMoteMaterial>` at `pfx.rs:298`. Its module still maintains the old defaults (`pfx.rs:165`), mote/material/quad definitions (`pfx.rs:623`), configuration conversion and interpolation helpers (`pfx.rs:2977`), and emission/movement machinery (`pfx.rs:3250` onward).

This is not an unselected but supported platform renderer. `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/server/renderer.rs:78` installs `NativeVisualPlugin`; `server/native_visuals/mod.rs:29` registers its replacement mote system, and `server/native_visuals/mod.rs:89` implements the active fixed pool. The module gates the flare implementation by target, but does not gate the mote system to native. PASM explicitly records the approved replacement on both native and WebGL2, preserving `[dust].enabled` as the master switch (`pasm/spec/architecture/viewscreen-cameras-debug.yaml:161`). There is no runtime route in the inspected registrations that enables the old emitter.

**Concrete deletion.** Remove the false run condition and its four registrations; remove the legacy-only resource/material registration, types, default table, settings conversion, emitter, movement and math helper implementation once their references are retired. Remove `cleanup_pfx`'s legacy dust resource/reset dependency (`pfx.rs:2730`). This is removal of a second implementation, not relocation of it to another file or conversion into a configurable fallback.

**Important boundary: not every dust-named function is dead.** `pfx.rs:3234`'s `dust_texture_paths` already resolves the *replacement* platform settings and is called by `server/asset_preload.rs:345`. Keep its behavior, including disabled handling, sort/dedup, and selected native/web configuration. The shared `assets/shaders/dust_mote.wgsl` and the mote textures remain consumed by `server/native_visuals/mod.rs:73` and `:128`; deleting the legacy Rust material does not authorize deleting those assets. Do not remove authored config parsing or model fields in another owner simply because their previous renderer has retired.

There is also a real pack-change caller: `entities/pack_assets.rs:195` invokes `pfx::reset_pack_textures` (`pfx.rs:2752`). That function currently resets only `DustFieldState` and despawns only `DustMote`; it does not reach the active renderer's `MotePool` or `NativeMote`. The active pool rebuilds when its configuration differs (`native_visuals/mod.rs:113`), not in response to this legacy reset. **Limitation/hypothesis:** a same-settings pack replacement may therefore need active-pool invalidation, but this audit did not execute that lifecycle. Do not present deleting the dead reset as proving the live pack lifecycle correct. Resolve the accepted-pack lifecycle contract at the active owner and preserve its noninterference with other PFX before retiring this call. PASM still claims dust texture retirement on stack changes (`pasm/spec/architecture/workshop-runtime-assets.yaml:46`).

**Savings.** Approximately **950–1,050 production lines gross**: about 90 lines of defaults, 90 of legacy component/material/geometry definitions, roughly 250 lines from the velocity/settings block before the live texture-path helper, and roughly 580 lines of legacy state/material/emission/movement implementation after it, plus registration/reset removal. These are contiguous, inspectable blocks, not a claim that every line in the entire dust region can be removed. Net savings depend on active-lifecycle repair and coverage; no test or documentation removal is included in this estimate.

**Tests and required verification.** `server/pfx_dust_tests.rs:4` tests pack refresh against manually spawned *legacy* dust, so it cannot establish replacement-renderer retirement. Most of that test file exercises the disabled algorithm directly: old spawn budgets (`:241`), depth bands (`:332`), lifetime (`:369`) and old configuration override rules (`:429`). Preserve required behavior coverage at the active renderer rather than retaining a thousand lines solely to keep obsolete private-function tests compiling. Retarget the pack test to active motes and retain its assertion that unrelated PFX survive. Add focused CPU/ECS coverage for active pool count/config rebuild, mission exit, `[dust].enabled`, visibility outside 3D views and platform-selected texture discovery; preserve the existing active motion-direction regression in `server/native_visuals/tests.rs:3`. The old horizontal-only velocity tests should not be copied as active requirements: the replacement explicitly includes vertical motion (`native_visuals/mod.rs:148`). Native and WASM feature checks and rendered parity evidence would be appropriate during implementation, but were not run here.

**Tradeoffs/confidence.** High confidence that the old runtime renderer is unreachable and provides a substantial deletion. Removing it abandons an already-disabled implementation as a potential fallback; that matches the current PASM decision against a hybrid mode. Medium confidence on the final deletion boundary until pack invalidation and test ownership are reconciled. That caveat is a concrete existing contract, not a reason to retain all unreachable machinery indefinitely.

#### 2. Replace the manually assembled ordinary billboard quad with Bevy's existing rectangle primitive

**Evidence and callers.** `server/pfx.rs:1321` hand-builds a unit XY quad with four positions, +Z normals, image-oriented UVs, triangle-list topology and six indices. The same crate already creates this kind of billboard with `Rectangle::new(1.0, 1.0)` in `entities/billboard.rs:135`, and the active mote pool uses `Rectangle::default()` in `server/native_visuals/mod.rs:128`.

The cached, pinned dependency implementation was inspected: `bevy_mesh-0.18.1/src/primitives/dim2.rs:1055` builds that unit rectangle with the same position-to-UV mapping and normals, and the same diagonal/winding, although vertex order is cyclically shifted. `bevy_asset-0.18.1/src/render_asset.rs:40` confirms its default asset usage is `MAIN_WORLD | RENDER_WORLD`, matching the hand-written code.

**Concrete deletion and savings.** Keep the semantically useful `unit_billboard_mesh` name if desired, but make its body construct `Mesh::from(Rectangle::new(1.0, 1.0))`. That deletes approximately **18 production lines** of manually maintained mesh buffers without moving them into a new project helper. No dependency or feature expansion is required.

**Invariants and tests.** Preserve physical size, +Z-facing normal, image UV orientation, triangle winding and CPU/GPU residency. Compare geometry by position/UV association rather than requiring identical vertex indices. The burst appearance/lifetime test at `server/pfx_burst_tests.rs:6` checks materials, count, scale, fade and expiry, but does not assert this quad's texture mapping; a narrow geometry assertion is missing. The existing pinned-beam tests (`server/pfx_tests.rs:252`, `:690`) must remain unchanged in meaning.

Do **not** apply the same replacement blindly to `ribbon_quad_mesh` (`pfx.rs:1294`): beam and projectile U coordinates intentionally differ, and the beam's constant-U convention is an explicit rendering regression guard. Legacy `dust_quad_mesh` also deliberately flips its UV mapping; it belongs to finding 1's deletion, not to a general claim that every quad has identical semantics.

**Tradeoffs/confidence.** High confidence from pinned dependency source and existing in-crate use. This trades hand-maintained trivial geometry for an already-used dependency primitive. A future Bevy change should be caught by the position/UV assertion, not by retaining copied implementation code.

#### Existing sharing and justified differences

- The existing shared burst spawner, billboard size/pose helpers and render setup already remove real duplication. No saving is claimed for recreating those abstractions or removing their independent tests.

#### Coverage limits

The crate is large; this was not a line-by-line examination of every file. Radar node reconciliation, full camera/interpolation behavior, audio, planet shader/material loading, GLB pack loaders, preload polling internals, full LOD/fade transitions, capture readback, flare shaders and full viewer state machinery were not exhaustively audited. Other than the pinned Bevy primitive implementation, external dependency internals were not inspected. No runtime performance, rendered equivalence or complete feature matrix is claimed. ### OVERENGINEERING

Module: **Model** (`crates/phoenix-model/`). Audit source: `C:/Users/jkeyw/.codex/worktrees/reusable-game-layers/project-phoenix-v2`, source baseline `07fc2e025bb2f5385b2fadf4fb468039b77c0b4e`; the recorded HEAD contains documentation-only navigation changes.
