#### 1. Hand-written `ModifierSource` equality/hash machinery duplicates derivable semantics

**Evidence and current consumers.** `crates/phoenix-model/src/messages.rs:81` derives `PartialEq`, `PartialOrd` and `Ord` for `ModifierSource`, but `messages.rs:112` separately implements the marker trait `Eq`, and `messages.rs:114` implements `Hash` with a full match. Each branch adds a manually assigned integer and then hashes all of that variant's fields. The fields are `Uuid`, `String`, `PowerGroupId` and `SystemId`; the latter two already derive `Eq` and `Hash` at `crates/phoenix-model/src/identity.rs:197` and `identity.rs:262`. There is no specialized equality or field omission to preserve.

The real hashed consumer is `ShipModifiers::int_table` at `crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/modifiers/cache.rs:119`: a `HashMap<(ModifierSource, IntModifierSlot), i32>`. Its insertion/removal/cache behavior is at `cache.rs:240`, `cache.rs:248` and `cache.rs:259`. Floating-point modifiers instead use a `BTreeMap` (`cache.rs:97`), and flags use `BTreeMap<FlagKind, BTreeSet<ModifierSource>>` (`cache.rs:111`). Those intentionally stable orderings rely on `Ord`, not this hash. Browser modifier identity uses serialized enum values through `JSON.stringify` at `gui/sim-state.js:60`; it does not consume the Rust hash tags. The integer debug projection explicitly sorts contributions at `cache.rs:385`, independently of hash-table iteration.

**Why it matters.** This is a small but concrete instance of unnecessary machinery: every new variant requires another hand-written hashing branch and another arbitrary numeric tag, even though all identity fields participate in normal structural identity. The current match is correct, but it maintains roughly thirty lines of logic for semantics the compiler already supplies. The explicit empty `Eq` implementation adds a separate declaration without adding policy.

**Change opportunity and deletion test.** Add `Eq` and `Hash` to the existing derive list and remove the two manual implementations. If deleted in that way, the current collection operations still have structural identity, the ordering used for float accumulation remains unchanged, and serialized messages retain exactly their current shapes. Do not replace the ordered modifier maps or remove the `Hash` capability: the integer table is a real caller.

**Invariants and verification.** Preserve source/slot replacement versus accumulation and independent removal for distinct sources. Existing tests at `crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/modifiers/cache_tests.rs:544`, `:562`, `:581`, `:630` and `:647` cover distinct-source accumulation, same-key replacement, removal, float/integer independence and source cleanup. A focused all-variant equality/hash-consistency check would close the narrow verification gap for the derived identity, especially variants not exercised as integer keys. The serialized enum round-trips must remain unchanged; no test should assert a particular default-hasher numeric output.

**Tradeoffs, confidence and limitations.** High confidence for repository callers. A derived hash is not byte-for-byte identical to the manual one, so this is inappropriate if an external consumer has persisted raw Rust hash outputs. No such contract or caller was found in the repository search; current wire callers do not do that. The benefit is reduced maintenance, not a claimed runtime speed improvement. Integer map iteration already depends on randomized hashing; this proposal must not be presented as a determinism change.

#### 2. Comms wire compatibility has leaked into a second mutable runtime authority field

**Evidence and current consumers.** `CommsMessage` holds both `priority: CommsPriority` and `is_urgent: bool` at `crates/phoenix-model/src/messages.rs:849` and `:855`. The intended wire compatibility is explicit at `messages.rs:760`: an old payload without `priority` may supply the boolean. The decode helper at `messages.rs:863` and custom deserializer at `:906` already perform that migration and normalize the boolean. The serializer at `:938` regenerates the emitted boolean from effective priority.

Nevertheless, `effective_priority()` at `messages.rs:1038` interprets a *runtime* `Routine` plus `is_urgent = true` as `Urgent`, expressly for hand-built legacy values. This makes the runtime priority field non-authoritative on its own. The normal producers already use `CommsMessage::injected`, which initializes both consistently (`messages.rs:1000`): see scripted injection at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/comms/scripted.rs:508`, dialogue follow-up at `console/comms/server.rs:838`, and GM transmission at `gm_comms.rs:187`. Direct legacy mutation remains in tests, including `console/comms/server_tests.rs:1185` and `:3214`.

The actual production cost is visible in `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/console/comms/inbox.rs:173`: acknowledging Critical must update **both** fields at `:176` and `:177`. Consumers must remember to use `effective_priority` rather than the public `priority` field (`inbox.rs:168`, `inbox.rs:211`, `comms/server.rs:1022`, `console/comms/server.rs:151`). The digest reads both physical fields separately at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/sim_digest.rs:1568`. Consequently, inconsistent in-memory values can have different digest state while serializing to the same normalized meaning. This last observation is a representation property, not evidence that a shipped production path currently creates divergent peers.

**Why it matters.** Old JSON/RON input is a real requirement; the extra mutable runtime copy is not required by that compatibility requirement. It creates dual-write obligations and an additional interpretation rule throughout otherwise typed code. The production constructor and boundary decoder already know enough to keep a single canonical priority. Supporting old *Rust literals in the same source tree* imposes continuing runtime complexity that migrating those literals would remove.

**Change opportunity and deletion test.** Keep the legacy boolean in the wire decode helper and emitted wire representation, but represent runtime urgency only through `CommsPriority`. Migrate same-repository literals/tests and runtime reads to that canonical value; remove the boolean fallback from ordinary runtime semantics. This is not a recommendation to remove the custom codec, legacy fields, compatibility fixtures, or `CommsPriority::after_response`. The decoder must still distinguish an absent priority from explicitly supplied `Routine`, and the serializer must still emit the compatibility projection. Those requirements survive the deletion test; the redundant stored boolean does not.

**Invariants and verification.** Preserve JSON and RON acceptance, particularly explicit `Routine` winning over a true legacy boolean. Preserve outgoing Critical-to-urgent compatibility projection, Critical acknowledgement on response/clear, legacy Urgent follow-up behavior, recipient routing, and level-triggered Critical attention. The existing tests at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/core/codec_tests.rs:4894`, `:4910` and `:4947` cover priority precedence, serialized compatibility projection, RON round-trip and old RON fallback. Inbox tests at `console/comms/inbox_tests.rs:133`, `:157` and `:164` exercise Critical state transitions; retain their behavioral assertions while removing setup that fabricates redundant state. Browser fallback at `gui/comms-state.js:47` must remain because phones still read wire payloads.

The digest is a material migration constraint: **do not simply delete its boolean fold**. For valid canonical states, continue folding `priority.is_urgent()` in precisely the former boolean position, followed by the same priority code, preserving existing digest bytes and order. Check snapshot restore/capture and deterministic continuation with existing tests rather than resetting goldens. A focused check that constructor and JSON/RON paths produce identical canonical priority and digest inputs would directly verify the simplification. Public source-level users constructing `CommsMessage` literals must migrate; old serialized records need not change.

**Tradeoffs, confidence and limitations.** Medium-high confidence that the internal duplicate is avoidable; medium confidence in total implementation scope because persistence and digest consumers span Simulation. This is larger than a field deletion and should be done only as a bounded compatibility-preserving cleanup. It does not eliminate most of the manual serializer/deserializer, and no large line-count or performance saving is claimed. A source-compatibility commitment to external Rust struct-literal users would argue for deferring it; none was established by this inspection.

#### Requirements that justify apparently elaborate structures

- **The crate/package seam and `ecs` feature are justified.** `crates/phoenix-model/Cargo.toml:8` gates ECS traits while Content consumes the crate without that feature (`crates/phoenix-content/Cargo.toml:14`) and Contracts selects it (`crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/Cargo.toml:11`). Shared definitions avoid sibling-domain imports; this follows `pasm/spec/architecture/reusable-layers.yaml:10`. Re-export paths are compatibility scaffolding, not evidence for deleting the owning package.
- **The debug catalogue macro has real consumers.** `crates/phoenix-model/src/debug_surface.rs:22` generates the enum, ordered descriptors and name conversions together. Simulation iterates the descriptors and matches adapters (`crates/phoenix-simulation/src/debug/catalogue.rs:77`, `:98`, `:122`); the browser bridge parses names (`src/server/bridge.rs:3294`). Model catalogue tests and Simulation adapter tests enforce completeness. Replacing this with hand-maintained lists would remove syntax while restoring multiple declarations of the same contract.
- **Typed identities and Coordination presentation are not speculative generalization.** Station/System/Console Family distinguish ownership, authority and rendering. `CoordinationPresentation` carries deterministic parameter maps (`crates/phoenix-model/src/identity.rs:147`), and concrete power/shield emitters build it (`crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/ship/power.rs:509`, `ship/shields.rs:477`). String scanning also recognizes its constructors (`scripts/strings-rust.mjs:193`). Replacing these with untyped strings or JSON would discard existing guarantees and move work into callers.
- **Checked readiness is used at an admission boundary.** `ReadinessTally::try_new/checked_add` (`crates/phoenix-model/src/wire.rs:337`) validates fleet aggregates at `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lobby/start_policy.rs:62`. Its error variants and empty-roster behavior serve real policy; they are not merely defensive wrappers around a private trusted count.
- **Shared visual declarations and build provenance are warranted.** The runtime selector (`crates/phoenix-model/src/entity/visual.rs:385`) is used by the rendered game, viewer and preload code (`crates/phoenix-presentation/src/server_app_render.rs:554`, `viewer/lod.rs:133`, `server/asset_preload.rs:194`). Rig generation/capture fields document intentionally separate tooling consumers. Their absence from the game tick is not proof of dead configuration. `TextureSource` also has Content validation and Presentation loading consumers, rather than being a one-use abstraction.
- **The model build script's demo cfg is behavior-bearing.** `crates/phoenix-model/build.rs:3` supplies the cfg used to omit demo-forbidden client message variants (`crates/phoenix-model/src/messages.rs:2592`, `:2618`, `:2679`). It cannot be removed simply because this is a data crate.

#### Coverage limits

This is a targeted overengineering audit, not an exhaustive wire-field liveness proof. I did not trace every SystemControlPayload/ClientMessage/ServerMessage variant, every blackboard field, every authored celestial setting, every GUI console, or every save migration. Full marker/rig transform mathematics and LOD tooling implementation were not independently audited. Existing tests were read where cited, not executed. No claim is made that all public model types are necessary merely because the sampled consumers are; conversely, no deletion is proposed based only on file length, numerous serde defaults, repeated Rust/JavaScript vocabulary, or sparse owner-local tests when integration coverage exists elsewhere.


Audit 30/72 · Agent: `/root/audit30_model_reuse` · Lens: reuse

### Reuse

Module: `phoenix-model`.

Scope: read-only inspection of the model package in `C:/Users/jkeyw/.codex/worktrees/reusable-game-layers/project-phoenix-v2`, at the supplied source baseline `07fc2e025bb2f5385b2fadf4fb468039b77c0b4e` (the supplied current HEAD differs only in navigation documentation). Inspected `Cargo.toml`, `build.rs`, public module exports, identities, shared wire records, tags and their tests, the debug catalogue and tests, rig declarations, entity-schema declarations, and selected behavior/codec/default sections of `messages.rs`. Traced relevant callers into Gameplay, Simulation and Presentation, and inspected existing codec and modifier tests. Read the repository operating instructions, wiki schema/index, domain vocabulary, reusable-layers guide, codec/message-flow navigation, reusable-layers PASM, and relevant portions of the Comms and radar PASM slices. No nested `AGENTS.md` was returned under `crates`; `docs/adr` was absent in this checkout.

This is a reuse audit, not a proposal to merge separately owned simulation branches or to make phones load WASM. Inspection found two small concrete opportunities. The large payload inventory was sampled rather than exhaustively compared field-by-field with every JavaScript consumer. No builds or tests were run, as directed.

#### 1. `ModifierSource` manually implements the fieldwise hash operation already provided by Rust's derive machinery

**Evidence and callers.** `crates/phoenix-model/src/messages.rs:81` derives `PartialEq`, `PartialOrd` and `Ord` for `ModifierSource`, but `messages.rs:112` supplies an empty manual `Eq` implementation and `messages.rs:114` implements `Hash` by matching every variant. The hash implementation writes an explicit byte tag and then hashes each payload field. All fields already support `Eq` and `Hash`: UUID, String, `PowerGroupId` and `SystemId`; the identity newtypes derive them in `crates/phoenix-model/src/identity.rs:197` and `identity.rs:262`. The same package already derives those traits on other compound data, rather than maintaining a parallel field walk.

This is live code, not an unused compatibility artifact. `crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/modifiers/cache.rs:119` stores integer modifiers in `HashMap<(ModifierSource, IntModifierSlot), i32>`; insertion and removal at `cache.rs:240` and `cache.rs:248` use the key. By contrast, the float modifier table and flag sources are explicitly ordered collections (`cache.rs:97` and `cache.rs:111`). Their deterministic accumulation and event sequence depend on `Ord`, not on these manually assigned hash tags.

**Opportunity.** Derive `Eq` and `Hash` on `ModifierSource` and remove the two manual implementations. This replaces handwritten standard behavior with an existing language capability; it needs no dependency or new abstraction. It also means adding a variant cannot forget to include its fields in the equality-compatible hash walk. The benefit is small but concrete: one fewer exhaustive parallel representation of the enum.

**Compatibility differences and invariants.** A derived hash is not byte-for-byte the current hash: its enum discriminant representation need not be the explicit `1u8` through `7u8` used today. This change is therefore appropriate only for the observed in-process keyed-collection contract, not for a persisted digest built from `std::hash::Hash`. The searched callers showed the integer table and a `HashSet` test, not a persisted protocol hash. Preserve enum declaration order, serde variants and payload fields; deriving Hash must not be accompanied by reordering variants, changing `Ord`, or replacing the float table with a HashMap. `cache.rs:113` explicitly explains why the integer table tolerates arbitrary traversal order, and its debug projection is sorted before emission (`cache.rs:318`). Existing RandomState hash maps already do not promise stable iteration order.

**Existing verification and gaps.** `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/core/codec_tests.rs:4600` tests equal `(id, tag)` World sources deduplicate in a HashSet while different fields remain distinct. `crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/modifiers/cache_tests.rs:544`, `:581`, `:618` and `:630` cover integer aggregation, removal and independence from float modifiers. The deterministic removal-order test is at `cache_tests.rs:332`. These are relevant existing checks to retain. The inspected hash test covers World rather than every variant; a targeted collection round-trip across the remaining variants would close that small verification gap. There is no reason to pin derived hash bytes as a new compatibility contract.

**Tradeoffs, confidence and limits.** High confidence in the duplicated fieldwise behavior; medium-high confidence that replacement is behavior-preserving in repository consumers. The explicit hash numbers are currently visible implementation details and would change. A final implementation review should search any newly introduced downstream hash consumer before making the edit. This recommendation does not claim standard hashes are portable native/WASM digests.

#### 2. Model already supplies `parse_tags`, but callers rebuild exactly the same conversion pipeline

**Evidence and actual duplication.** `crates/phoenix-model/src/tags.rs:96` defines `parse_tags(&[String]) -> Vec<EntityTag>` as iteration, `EntityTag::from_str`, filtering unknown values, and collection. `crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/entities/tags.rs:2` re-exports that model module, so Gameplay already has the correct dependency and access path.

Nevertheless, `crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/radar_config.rs:84` repeats the pipeline separately for `shows` and `selects` in `RadarConfig::from_toml`, and `radar_config.rs:104` repeats both again in its `Deserialize` implementation. `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/console/weapons/blackboard.rs:1104` and `:1110` repeat it for the tactical radar's configured tag lists. This same blackboard file already uses `parse_tags` at `:1451` and `:1508`, establishing that the helper is suitable in this subsystem rather than merely similar in name.

**Opportunity.** Use the existing model helper at those six conversions. Within RadarConfig, `from_toml` can additionally delegate to the existing `Deserialize` path via `toml::from_str::<Self>(...).map_err(|e| e.to_string())`, avoiding a second complete RawRadarConfig-to-RadarConfig conversion. The model helper itself needs no redesign. Most edits would be in callers outside this assigned package; they are reported here because the reuse opportunity is specifically an already-public Model operation being bypassed.

**Semantics that must survive.** The existing helper is exactly compatible with the observed pipelines: it preserves recognized input order and duplicates, drops unknown strings, accepts lowercase canonical names, and recognizes `torpedo` as an alias for Missile (`tags.rs:65`). Do not replace these callers with direct `Vec<EntityTag>` deserialization: the enum's serde derive only has `rename_all = "snake_case"` (`tags.rs:15`), and thus does not embody the permissive unknown-dropping policy or the custom `torpedo` alias. Do not deduplicate or sort the resulting lists as part of reuse.

Likewise, `RadarConfig::default()` supplies an asteroid filter (`radar_config.rs:39`), whereas an explicitly parsed empty configuration defaults `RawRadarConfig.shows` to an empty list (`radar_config.rs:53`). Delegation to its existing `Deserialize` implementation preserves this distinction; delegating to `Default` and patching fields would not. `deny_unknown_fields` and the current string error conversion must remain intact.

**Existing verification and gaps.** `crates/phoenix-model/src/tags_tests.rs:6` checks canonical round-trips, `:30` checks unknown/case-sensitive behavior, and `:45` checks that unknown list entries are dropped. `crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/radar_config_tests.rs:51` checks unknown tag dropping, and the same file exercises explicit parser and nested configuration behavior. The inspected Model tests do not explicitly pin the `torpedo` alias or order/duplicate retention; those are useful focused assertions if the conversion callers are consolidated. Existing integration tests for radar projection should be retained, not replaced by an implementation-mirroring test.

**Tradeoffs, confidence and limits.** High confidence, low-risk cleanup. This reduces duplicated conversion code and concentrates the permissive tag policy, but it does not fix a demonstrated runtime defect or change parsing performance materially. The benefit is maintenance consistency, not a case for extracting another generic parser. Other repository tag pipelines were not exhaustively enumerated; the six listed locations were inspected directly.

#### 3. Apparent duplicate codecs and data records that are justified by current requirements

These are reviewed non-findings, included to delimit the reuse recommendations and prevent incompatible consolidation.

**Comms runtime record versus decode helper.** `crates/phoenix-model/src/messages.rs:802` defines `CommsMessage`; `:863` defines the similar `CommsMessageWire`; `:897`, `:906` and `:938` implement decoding and encoding. The duplicate fields are substantial, but the helper owns a different semantic state: an absent priority must be distinguishable from explicitly supplied Routine. Missing priority falls back to legacy `is_urgent`; present priority is authoritative and normalizes the legacy boolean. The present-field deserializer reads a bare enum so RON can consume the same shape that serialization emits. An ordinary `Option<CommsPriority>` derive would expect a different RON shape, and simply deriving serialization on the runtime record would also bypass `effective_priority` normalization of hand-built legacy records. The current serializer additionally omits empty body parameter maps.

Existing tests directly protect these distinctions: `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/core/codec_tests.rs:2080` checks omitted empty params; `:4822` and `:4832` cover older range/thread defaults; `:4895` tests legacy urgency and explicit-priority authority; `:4911` covers critical-to-legacy projection; `:4942` covers old RON without priority. There is no identified existing repository codec helper that can replace this behavior wholesale. A borrowed serialization projection might move field enumeration elsewhere, but would not establish meaningful reuse and is not recommended by this audit. Confidence: high for retaining the explicit compatibility boundary.

**Declarations versus runtime types and compatibility facades.** `crates/phoenix-model/src/wire.rs:15` defines StationDef as a wire description with optional/defaulted fields, while the authored StationConfig also carries simulation authoring meaning. Similar shape alone is not proof they should become one type. Model cannot import Gameplay without reversing the boundary declared by `pasm/spec/architecture/reusable-layers.yaml:11`. The tag facade cited above is already a re-export, not a copied enum. Shared defaults for sensor projection and minimum power already reuse model functions: `wire.rs:368`, `:371`, `:374`, `messages.rs:1294`, `:1299`, `:5173`, and Gameplay's re-exports in `crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/entities/config/sensors_navigation.rs:93`. The delegating power-default wrapper is not a second independent numeric definition.

**Debug identity generation and renderer reuse.** `crates/phoenix-model/src/debug_surface.rs:23` already generates identity, names and lookup from one catalogue; `debug_surface_tests.rs:4` checks catalogue/name alignment. Similarly, the pure LOD selector at `crates/phoenix-model/src/entity/visual.rs:385` is consumed by both live rendering (`crates/phoenix-presentation/src/server_app_render.rs:554`) and viewer selection (`crates/phoenix-presentation/src/viewer/lod.rs:133`). Those are examples of existing reuse, not redundant implementations to delete. Independent LOD assertions under `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/entities/config_tests.rs:9282` onward are verification rather than duplicated production code.

**Boundary of coverage.** The full ~5,800-line `messages.rs` payload inventory, all JavaScript wire consumers, every authored celestial default and every build-script counterpart were not exhaustively compared. No claim is made that every similar enum string conversion is safely interchangeable: casing, optional fields, serde container shape, alias handling and constant-call usability can differ. In particular, this report does not recommend speculative schema generation, combining typed identifiers into one string type, collapsing native/browser adapters, or removing independent codec tests. All compatibility conclusions above are confined to the named inspected paths.


Audit 31/72 · Agent: `/root/audit31_model_architecture` · Lens: architecture

### Architecture

Module: **Model** (`crates/phoenix-model/`). Source inspected in `C:/Users/jkeyw/.codex/worktrees/reusable-game-layers/project-phoenix-v2`, against the instructed source baseline `07fc2e025bb2f5385b2fadf4fb468039b77c0b4e` (the current documentation-only revision does not change this code).

Scope and inspection: package manifest, build script, public module inventory, identities, wire value records, Debug Surface catalogue, tags, texture and rig declarations; selected hull and visual declarations and the complete LOD selection implementation; targeted sections of `messages.rs`, especially Comms compatibility, lobby readiness, identity re-exports, defaults, and public type inventory. Callers were followed into Session start policy and roster/session tally production, native GM readiness projection, Simulation Comms inbox and projection, Presentation LOD callers, and existing codec/configuration tests. Read the supplied instructions, the architecture skill and LANGUAGE.md, AGENTS.md, wiki schema/index, CONTEXT.md, reusable-layer guidance and PASM reusable-layers, Comms and session architecture material. No `docs/adr/` directory or nested Model AGENTS.md was found. This is a read-only audit; no commands built or tested the project.

The following are deepening candidates, not reports of demonstrated player-visible failures. Model intentionally holds shared vocabulary without Simulation or renderer dependencies. Deleting it would redistribute shared types among independent branches and undermine their dependency rules. Its small declaration modules and compatibility re-exports therefore do not fail the deletion test merely because their implementations are short.

#### 1. Concentrate the ReadinessTally invariant at the value seam

**Files.** `crates/phoenix-model/src/wire.rs:253`, `:337`; `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lobby/start_policy.rs:55`; `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lobby/session.rs:389`; `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/gm_roster.rs:116`; `src/native_host/native_gm/start.rs:56`.

**Problem and caller evidence.** `ReadinessTally` presents a fallible constructor that guarantees `ready <= connected`, but both fields remain public and its derived deserializer does not call that constructor. Its Interface consequently includes an unstated distinction between a valid tally and an arbitrary pair of unsigned counts. The start policy must reconstruct each incoming tally through `try_new` before aggregation (`start_policy.rs:62`), and the native GM presentation independently repeats that operation before `checked_add` (`start.rs:61`). Session and GM roster producers construct field literals after counting connected/ready records. Those producers are reasonable, but all consumers must know whether a particular origin established the invariant.

The subtle case is the public aggregation operation: `checked_add` verifies the final pair and arithmetic overflow, not the validity of each operand. An invalid pair such as connected=1, ready=2 combined with connected=1, ready=0 produces a valid aggregate pair. This explains why the caller-side revalidation is meaningful; removing it as apparent duplication would weaken refusal behavior. This is a counterexample derived from the implementation, not a claim that current production callers exploit it.

**Solution.** Deepen the existing value Module so construction, decoding and aggregation share responsibility for what constitutes a valid readiness count. Keep roster counting and permission to launch in Session. Decide explicitly how invalid externally supplied values reach the existing refusal path before simplifying callers; early decode rejection and `InvalidReadiness` policy refusal are observably different outcomes. The opportunity is to remove repeated knowledge of the invariant, not to move lobby policy into Model or introduce another abstraction around two integers.

**Benefits and deletion test.** Deleting `try_new` or `checked_add` would spread overflow and subset checks into callers, so the Module earns its keep. Strengthening its existing seam offers more Depth: callers receive a readiness value with a reliable meaning, rather than a value plus a validation ritual. Locality improves because future validation changes have one implementation and one narrow test surface. Native readiness display and multiplayer start policy gain the same Leverage without knowing each other's implementation.

**Behavior to preserve.** Zero connected participants must not count as all ready (`wire.rs:345`); counts remain `u32`; overflow must remain explicit; hostile readiness must never grant a start; forced start must retain its GM authentication and world-validation rules. The native GM projection deliberately exposes invalid totals as `participants: None` while retaining `u64` display totals (`start.rs:46–66`); preserving that diagnostic behavior matters.

**Existing tests and missing verification.** `crates/phoenix-sim-session/src/lobby/start_policy_tests.rs:137` tests constructor rejection alongside StartGrant invariants. Native GM totals are exercised in `src/native_host/native_gm/start_tests.rs:249` onward. Those are useful caller-level checks. Add owner-local tests for each invalid construction route and for aggregation with an invalid operand masked by another cohort if arbitrary pairs remain representable. Preserve the higher-level refusal/display tests rather than assuming value tests replace admission coverage.

**Tradeoffs, confidence and limitations.** Medium priority, high confidence in the observed seam leakage. Public-field changes may touch many fixtures and alter serde failure timing. The current revalidation is defensive and correct; this is maintenance friction, not an asserted launch vulnerability. All external tally ingress paths were not traced exhaustively.

#### 2. Hide Comms compatibility priority state from runtime mutation

**Files.** `crates/phoenix-model/src/messages.rs:760`, `:802`, `:906`, `:938`, `:970`, `:1038`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/console/comms/inbox.rs:163`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/console/comms/server.rs:149`, `:2368`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/comms/server.rs:1021`.

**Problem and caller evidence.** `CommsMessage` stores both authoritative `priority` and legacy `is_urgent` as publicly mutable fields. Deserialization correctly normalizes the boolean from explicit priority and falls back to the old boolean only when priority is absent. Serialization also projects the boolean. Despite those existing normalization seams, runtime `effective_priority` must still repair hand-built legacy values: Routine plus true means Urgent. Callers therefore cannot simply treat the `priority` field as authoritative.

This reaches an actual mutation site: `CommsInbox::acknowledge_priority` must write both `priority = Routine` and `is_urgent = false` (`inbox.rs:176`). Changing only the nominal priority would leave a critical message effectively Urgent. The inbox's priority query and critical-thread detection call `effective_priority`, as do the two Comms contact projections and the message projection. Tests also manufacture states by independently setting these fields (`console/comms/inbox_tests.rs:132`, `:156`, `:163`). The Interface exposes compatibility bookkeeping to gameplay callers even though a compatibility Adapter already exists in Model's serde implementation.

**Solution.** Concentrate legacy normalization and projection in the Comms value Module, with runtime priority changes maintaining one meaning without requiring paired field writes. Migrate hand-built fixtures deliberately. Keep old JSON/RON shapes readable and continue emitting the compatibility field. This is not a proposal to delete compatibility support, merge all Comms logic into Model, or move thread lifecycle policy out of the inbox.

**Benefits and deletion test.** The custom serde implementation passes the deletion test: deleting it would either break old saves/peers or force format-specific rules into multiple callers. The deepen-able part is the mutable two-field state exposed beyond that seam. Hiding that bookkeeping yields Locality for normalization and priority consistency while preserving the inbox Module's separate Leverage over thread history. Callers would not need to remember that lowering Critical requires clearing a second field.

**Behavior to preserve.** An explicit wire Routine must override a contradictory legacy true flag. Missing priority plus true must become Urgent. Critical must serialize a true legacy projection. RON must retain its bare enum representation rather than requiring `Some(...)`; `deserialize_present_comms_priority` documents the actual compatibility issue at `messages.rs:887`. Reading does not acknowledge a critical interruption; existing inbox lifecycle decisions remain unchanged. Contact urgency remains a derived unread-message projection, not interchangeable with stored message priority. The authored World request's separate legacy `urgent` handling (`crates/phoenix-sim-world/src/comms/content.rs:250`) serves a different input seam and should not be deleted merely because it resembles the message fallback.

**Existing tests and missing verification.** `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/core/codec_tests.rs:4895` explicitly covers old urgency and explicit-new-field precedence; `:4911` covers stale boolean serialization and JSON/RON round trips; `:4942` covers legacy RON and explicit Routine precedence. `console/comms/inbox_tests.rs:132` onward exercises critical messages and invalidation. Retain these fixtures and add a value-level priority-transition regression that asserts both runtime interpretation and serialized output after Critical acknowledgement. Test through the same value seam callers use, with inbox tests retaining thread lifecycle coverage.

**Tradeoffs, confidence and limitations.** High confidence in caller knowledge leaking through paired writes; moderate refactor priority because present runtime writers already do the right thing. A change may intentionally stop supporting contradictory hand-built Rust literals while preserving all persisted/wire compatibility. That distinction must be explicit and fixture migration reviewed. No failure in deployed Comms behavior was demonstrated.

#### 3. Bring pure Model behavior tests to the Model Interface

**Files.** `crates/phoenix-model/Cargo.toml:7`; `crates/phoenix-model/src/identity.rs:31`; `crates/phoenix-model/src/entity/visual.rs:354`; `crates/phoenix-model/src/messages.rs:906`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/entities/config_tests.rs:9282`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/core/codec_tests.rs:2450`, `:4895`.

**Problem and evidence.** Model's default package deliberately avoids ECS and rendering, but important tests of its own pure behavior still live under Simulation. Examples include constructor-only `ActionCorrelationId` invalid-value assertions at codec_tests.rs:2450, LOD selection tests beginning at config_tests.rs:9282, and direct `serde_json`/RON tests of `CommsMessage` at codec_tests.rs:4895. These tests exercise Model values without needing a live World or simulation schedule. A maintainer following the owner-targeted workflow cannot establish these particular contracts using Model's tests alone.

The LOD Module is not an unnecessary extracted helper: the game renderer (`crates/phoenix-presentation/src/server_app_render.rs:554`), viewer (`crates/phoenix-presentation/src/viewer/lod.rs:133`) and preload selector (`crates/phoenix-presentation/src/server/asset_preload.rs:194`) actually share it. Its pure Interface is exactly the correct test surface. Similarly, the correlation constructor already has a strong private-value seam and need not be redesigned merely to improve test placement.

**Solution.** Move the self-contained value/serde/selection cases to owner-local tests, adding only the small test-format dependencies needed there. Keep full message-envelope codec, authored-content integration, renderer and deterministic-mission tests in their existing owning/composition Modules. Select cases by dependencies and behavior, not by searching names and bulk-moving everything.

**Benefits and deletion test.** This is deepening of the package's verification seam rather than a request for more production abstractions. Model's behavior and its tests acquire Locality, and its standalone, renderer-free build yields more Leverage to maintainers. Deleting the tests would lose real behavioral coverage; moving pure tests preserves that coverage while removing their dependence on unrelated composition machinery. No line-count saving is claimed.

**Behavior to preserve.** LOD's empty-list result, stale current-index clamp, exact inward/outward hysteresis inequalities and first-frame selection must stay pinned. Comms serialized shape and missing-field precedence must stay pinned in both JSON and RON. Correlation IDs must retain the nonempty, maximum-byte and visible-ASCII rules. Keep independent package dependency constraints intact; Model tests must not import Simulation simply to reuse fixtures.

**Existing coverage and missing verification.** Owner-local tag and Debug Surface catalogue tests already illustrate the desired placement (`tags_tests.rs:6`, `debug_surface_tests.rs:4`); the ViewMode test is local as well. The gap is test locality, not absence of all coverage. Verify moved cases still execute without the optional `ecs` feature, and retain caller-specific behavior checks where they genuinely cross another seam. No tests were executed in this audit.

**Tradeoffs, confidence and limitations.** High confidence for the cited tests because their bodies directly exercise Model types/functions. This does not establish that every similarly named Simulation test is movable. Adding JSON/RON dev dependencies slightly enlarges the Model test graph, but avoids pulling in the parent Simulation to test its child. Integration tests that prove authored content or cross-format codec routing remain valuable where they are.

#### Justified structures and inspection limits

- The Debug Surface macro (`debug_surface.rs:23`) owns enum values, stable order and wire names together. Its catalogue/conversion test confirms that shared responsibility. Replacing it with hand-maintained parallel matches would reduce Depth, not improve it.
- Plain shared records, optional ECS derives and the `entity` re-export facade preserve declared independent branches and historical import paths. No wholesale getter layer, trait abstraction or relocation into a simulation sibling is warranted by the inspected evidence.
- `ActionCorrelationId` (`identity.rs:28–66`) already validates construction and deserialization around a private representation. It is a positive example of a value Module with a small Interface; no replacement is proposed.
- `CivilianOrder::validate` (`wire.rs:312`) was traced to both admission/script callers and authored traffic validation. Separate checking at those real ingress seams is justified. No recommendation to remove one was made.
- `messages.rs` is large, but size alone does not establish an architectural defect. This audit did not exhaustively examine every snapshot, blackboard, ClientMessage/ServerMessage variant, field default or target-specific demo-gated variant. Celestial configuration and remaining hull/visual fields were surveyed by declarations rather than exhaustively validated. Rig marker consumers, all transport-related message variants, full serialization/digest field coverage, generated browser schema checks and complete native/WASM build graphs were not audited. Findings above are limited to the traced value and caller seams, and make no claim of exhaustive correctness or passing validation.


Audit 32/72 · Agent: `/root/audit32_model_loc` · Lens: loc

### Reducing maintained lines

Module: **Model** (`crates/phoenix-model/`). Source inspected in `C:/Users/jkeyw/.codex/worktrees/reusable-game-layers/project-phoenix-v2`, against source baseline `07fc2e025bb2f5385b2fadf4fb468039b77c0b4e` (the supplied audit instructions identify current HEAD as a subsequent documentation-only change).

Scope: crate manifest/build script; module exports; identities, tag conversions, debug-surface catalogue, wire records; entity celestial/hull/visual declarations and rig declarations; targeted sections of the 5,844-line message module, especially defaults, blackboards, discriminants and legacy wire behavior. Caller searches covered the content, gameplay, simulation and presentation crates. Inspected existing simulation codec, entity-config and rig-test evidence. No nested AGENTS.md was found under crates; `docs/adr` does not exist in this source tree. No source changes, builds or tests were performed.

#### 1. Inline the nine private star-default constants into the existing Default implementation

**Files and evidence.** `crates/phoenix-model/src/entity/celestial.rs:4` through `:38` declares nine parameterless private functions, each returning a literal: radius, two mesh segment counts, four colours, halo multiplier and animation speed. Their only references found by repository-wide symbol search are the nine field initializers in `StarConfig::default` at `:59`–`:73`. `StarConfig` already has container-level `#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]` at `:42`; none of these functions is named by a field-level Serde attribute. The same file demonstrates the simpler arrangement in `PlanetCityConfig::default` at `:217`, whose literals are directly beside their field names.

**Observed callers and cost.** Gameplay owns the containing entity declaration (`crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/entities/config.rs:263`) and the extracted star section (`crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/entities/spawner.rs:43`). Presentation consumes the values in `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/entities/star.rs:94` and `:111` and `entities/celestial_visual.rs:22`. These consumers need the `StarConfig` fields and its defaults, not the private getter names. The extra functions create another location to visit to determine the default object without furnishing a second consumer or a validation seam.

**Concrete deletion.** Put the existing literal values directly into `StarConfig::default` and delete all nine functions. This retains one authoritative default object, reduces indirection and does not introduce a macro, dependency, derived-schema system or new shared numeric constants. This is actual deletion: **27 nonblank function lines, approximately 36 physical lines including their separating whitespace**, with no increase in initializer count. It does not count deleting documentation or tests.

**Behavior to preserve.** Keep exact f32 literals and arrays, field types/order, all derives, `deny_unknown_fields` and container-level defaults. An absent star section remains absent; an explicitly empty `[star]` still means a present star populated with these defaults. No rendering behavior, deterministic arithmetic or serialized field names change. Do not broaden this deletion to adjacent `default_planet_*` functions: those are referenced by field-level Serde defaults on types that still have required fields, such as `PlanetConfig.surface` and `PlanetSurfaceConfig.albedo` (`celestial.rs:111` and `:133`).

**Existing verification and gap.** `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/entities/config_tests.rs:1640` covers an empty star section, `:1655` covers overrides, `:1682` rejects unknown star fields and `:1694` parses the shipped sun template. Preserve these independent tests. A literal-for-literal review plus those cases is proportionate verification for implementation; no new test framework is justified. This audit did not execute them or establish that every colour component is individually asserted.

**Tradeoff/confidence.** High confidence, modest benefit. Named functions could become useful if independent consumers emerge, but none currently exists and the field names already explain the literals. The proposal is restricted to these nine proved single-use private helpers.

#### 2. Let BaseTransform's existing Default implementation own sparse rig defaults

**Files and evidence.** `crates/phoenix-model/src/rig.rs:4`–`:10` defines `zeros` and `ones`; `:18`, `:21` and `:24` reference them through field-level Serde defaults. `BaseTransform::default` at `:28`–`:36` repeats the same field-to-helper mapping. All three fields have explicit defaults and the type rejects unknown fields. `ModelRig.base` already defaults through `BaseTransform` when the entire base section is absent (`rig.rs:81` onward).

**Observed callers and cost.** These declarations are read by content's rig parser (`crates/phoenix-content/src/rig.rs`) and consumed by presentation; for example, `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/server_app_render.rs:409` reads `rig.base.scale`, `viewer/lod.rs:195` uses that scale in tier resolution and `entities/glb_visual.rs:130` resolves the sidecar scale. The helpers do not express validation, normalization or target-specific behavior. The field-by-field Serde declaration and `Default` implementation maintain the same identity transform in parallel.

**Concrete deletion.** Add container-level `default` to the existing `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]` on `BaseTransform`, remove its three field-default attributes, write the existing zero/one arrays directly in its existing `Default` implementation and delete `zeros` and `ones`. The external type, parser and callers are unchanged. Estimated reduction is **nine nonblank lines** (six helper lines and three attributes), approximately **eleven physical lines** with current spacing. This is a narrowly bounded use of an existing trait implementation, not a new abstraction or moving declarations elsewhere.

**Behavior to preserve.** Missing offset and rotation must remain `[0.0, 0.0, 0.0]`; missing scale must remain `[1.0, 1.0, 1.0]`. In particular, deriving ordinary Rust `Default` would give a zero scale and is incorrect. Sparse `[base]`, empty `[base]`, absence of `[base]`, explicit authored values, type errors and rejection of unknown fields must all retain their current behavior. Serialization derives and field order remain untouched; the change concerns missing-field deserialization only.

**Existing verification and gap.** `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/entities/model_rig_tests.rs:56` has `sparse_sidecar_uses_base_defaults`; `:73` has `empty_sidecar_is_identity_rig`; `:168` checks the default Bevy transform; `:317` checks unknown sidecar fields; and `:577` covers XYZ Euler transform behavior. Preserve all of them. For implementation, confirm the unknown-field test covers an unknown base key as well as other sidecar levels, and extend only if that case is missing. Check a partially specified base with authored non-unit scale to ensure container defaults fill holes rather than overwrite supplied values. These are verification recommendations, not executed results.

**Tradeoff/confidence.** High confidence in the equivalence for these three fields. Container defaults make the manual `Default` implementation the clear source of truth, though a reader must navigate there instead of following field attributes. This pattern is already used by `StarConfig` in the same crate. Do not apply it indiscriminately to structs with required fields; doing so can silently relax authoring validation.

#### 3. Remove the private power-floor forwarding function, retaining its canonical wire default

**Files and evidence.** `crates/phoenix-model/src/messages.rs:5161` names the local `default_min_power_level` as the deserialization default for `PowerGroupEntry.min_level`. The private function at `:5173`–`:5175` merely calls `crate::wire::default_min_power_level`, defined at `crates/phoenix-model/src/wire.rs:374` to return `1`. Its adjacent comment expressly notes that Serde accepts the cross-module path. Search found this attribute as the local wrapper's only caller. The shared function itself is re-exported by gameplay's ship configuration (`crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/ship/config.rs:221`) and used to define the reactor floor (`modifiers/power_system.rs:246`).

**Observed callers and cost.** Gameplay publishes the authored floor at `crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/ship/power.rs:663`–`:685`. Legacy messages that omitted it need the same floor as authored configuration. The local wrapper serves readability only and cannot supply independent policy. There is therefore one removable indirection, but the shared wire function and its public compatibility path are required.

**Concrete deletion.** Change the attribute to `#[serde(default = "crate::wire::default_min_power_level")]` and delete the three-line local forwarding function. Retain the useful compatibility explanation beside `min_level`; remove only commentary explaining the wrapper that no longer exists. **Three executable/declaration lines** are saved; total physical savings are not estimated because they depend on how that useful explanation is retained. Do not claim the comments as unnecessary protocol documentation.

**Behavior to preserve.** Missing `min_level` must decode to `1`; an explicitly authored `0` must remain `0`. `commanded_level` independently defaults to `0`. Never replace the attribute with bare `#[serde(default)]`, and never hardcode a second `1` next to the payload. Shared power-floor callers and the public function remain in place.

**Existing verification and gap.** `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/core/codec_tests.rs:5661` (`power_group_entry_round_trips`) includes a legacy payload omitting both fields. At `:5699` it compares the decoded floor against the gameplay configuration function, then explicitly asserts `1`; the following case addresses an authored zero floor. These tests exercise the actual compatibility boundary and should remain unchanged. No new coverage is evidently necessary for changing only the function path, but the existing case should be run during implementation.

**Tradeoff/confidence.** High confidence in equivalence, very small standalone payoff. Keeping the wrapper is defensible as a local naming preference; this is an optional cleanup to combine with nearby work, not a reason to reorganize the model crate.

#### Retained code, limits and areas not exhaustively explored

- **Message declarations are contracts, not boilerplate to erase.** This audit sampled `messages.rs`; it did not trace every field and enum variant through Rust producers, pure-JavaScript consumers, recovery data and compatibility fixtures. No unused-payload or unused-variant deletion is recommended. Splitting its 5,844 lines into files would improve navigation but would not reduce maintained lines.
- **Custom defaults are often semantically necessary.** `CameraView::default` (`messages.rs:647`) chooses `camera_fore`; `ViewMode::default` (`:688`) selects a data-bearing Camera variant. Radar defaults at `:1885`, the Captain blackboard at `:3971`, Command at `:4047`, Sensors at `:5378` and Navigation at `:5509` contain nonzero ranges, stable system ids or specific state. Deriving zero/empty defaults to save lines would alter behavior. No new derive dependency or custom macro is proposed merely to compress these initializers.
- **Tag conversion similarity is insufficient for removal.** `tags.rs:54` accepts the legacy `torpedo` spelling as `Missile`, whereas `as_str` at `:75` returns the canonical `missile`; Serde uses the enum's snake-case names. Existing `tags_tests.rs` supplies independent round-trip and filtering checks. Although the crate already depends on Strum, replacing these functions with derives would need deliberate alias and error-behavior treatment and a measured net reduction. This audit did not complete that comparison and does not count it as a finding.
- **Debug-surface generation is already doing useful deletion.** `debug_surface.rs:22` derives the catalogue, stable ordered list and conversions from one invocation. Removing its public outputs without tracing consumers would undo the consolidation; it is not redundant simply because generated outputs resemble one another.
- **Compatibility exports and build configuration remain.** `lib.rs`, `entity/mod.rs`, feature-gated ECS derives and the demo-build configuration serve declared layer/API and build requirements. No removal is justified by their small size. No test or facade relocation is counted as savings.
- **Unexplored depth:** most individual message payload consumers, full visual/LOD semantic implementations, every identity-conversion caller, target-specific compile combinations and build-script deduplication across crates. The findings above concern local, source-demonstrable deletion only; they make no claim that the full module is free of larger opportunities.


## 9. Content preparation

Scope: `crates/phoenix-content/`


Audit 33/72 · Agent: `/root/audit33_content_overengineering` · Lens: overengineering

### Overengineering

Module: Content preparation (`crates/phoenix-content/`).

Scope: inspected the crate manifest and module inventory; archive envelope/path admission, entity merge policies, include resolution and provenance, template preload state, overlay storage/accessors, content ledger, manifest shapes, String Table validation, rig helpers, sound-cue validation, and the asset-validation entry points. Read the opening contracts and selected implementations of model metadata/semantic validation and inspected their test inventories. Traced consumers into Gameplay, Simulation, Presentation, Workshop, browser bridge and existing integration tests. Source root was `C:/Users/jkeyw/.codex/worktrees/reusable-game-layers/project-phoenix-v2`, at the audit's stated source baseline. No code was changed and no tests or builds were run.

Orientation included `AGENTS.md`, `wiki/SCHEMA.md`, `CONTEXT.md`, `wiki/index.md`, `wiki/concepts/reusable-layers.md`, `wiki/concepts/asset-preload.md`, and PASM's `architecture/reusable-layers.yaml` and `architecture/world-content-packs.yaml`. No nested `AGENTS.md` was returned under `crates`; `docs/adr` is absent in this checkout. This audit does not assess scenario design or pacing.

#### 1. The obsolete structural merge remains a production public API even though its only executable consumers are tests

**Files and evidence.** `crates/phoenix-content/src/entity_override.rs:1` explicitly warns that no entity path uses the plain merge and that using it would lose systems, doctrine and shield arcs. Nevertheless `:16` exports `merge_toml` publicly and maintains a recursive implementation through `:34`. The active merge instead recursively calls its own policy-aware `merge_at` at `:330`; it does not invoke the plain merge. Whole-repository Rust search found executable references to `merge_toml` only in itself and six cases in `crates/phoenix-content/src/entity_override_tests.rs:47`, `:55`, `:151`, `:170`, `:183`, and `:202`. The function also leaks through wildcard compatibility exports in `crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/entities/entity_override.rs:2` and `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/entities/entity_override.rs:1`.

**Observed cost and deletion test.** This is a second maintained algorithm with the wrong semantics for the module's current production clients, accompanied by a warning explaining why callers must avoid it. Removing it from the normal production API would not change any located runtime call. The comment at `entity_override.rs:13` says it is the primitive the entity merge is defined in terms of and a reference point for an old differential test; that is conceptual history, not an implementation dependency in this revision. The current implementation has its own table recursion, and the search did not locate the claimed differential call. The cost is modest but concrete: another public choice for authors to distinguish, duplicated structural recursion, and six tests exercising an algorithm production no longer calls.

**Change opportunity.** Stop exporting the plain merge as normal content functionality. First confirm the intended compatibility promise for downstream users of this public crate/facade. If its historical reference behavior still merits testing, retain a private test-only reference helper; otherwise move the existing structural expectations onto the current policy-aware public entry point before deleting the obsolete helper. Do not delete coverage merely to remove lines. The precedent already exists nearby: `merge_keyed_array` at `entity_override.rs:423` and `merge_named_array` at `:474` are explicitly test-only because their implicit policy is inappropriate for production callers.

**Behavior that must survive.** Preserve deep table merge, scalar and ordinary-array replacement, input immutability and key ordering. Preserve the actual distinction between `ComposeFragments` and `InstanceOverrides`: composition unions tags and supports keyed removals; instance overrides replace tags and reject removals. This recommendation does not collapse those policies or replace keyed merging with the obsolete structural routine.

**Verification and tradeoff.** Existing owner-local tests cover scalar/array replacement (`entity_override_tests.rs:44`, `:52`), nested table retention (`:135`), insertion (`:161`), false values (`:180`) and ordering (`:188`). Those tests currently call the old function, so retaining their assertions at the production entry point is necessary. Policy-specific coverage includes `the_two_layers_disagree_only_where_they_are_meant_to` (`:514`), tombstone rejection (`:697`), keyed order (`:785`) and transition replacement (`:855`). Missing verification is whether any consumer outside this repository intentionally imports the public generic helper; repository search cannot establish that. Confidence is high for absence of in-repository runtime usage, medium for immediate public removal. Benefit is reduced API ambiguity and obsolete machinery, not a material runtime speed improvement.

#### Machinery examined and justified by current requirements

- **The content-local JSON codec is intentional, not a needless wrapper to remove.** `crates/phoenix-content/src/codec.rs:2` merely delegates decoding, and its three callers are asset validation (`pack_asset_validation.rs:25`, `:240`, `:279`). However `AGENTS.md:897` explicitly confines `serde_json` to codec modules. Importing Simulation's codec would also reverse the package boundary. Retain this small local seam under current policy.

- **Injected entity parsing and source resolution preserve actual package independence.** `include_resolve.rs:493` accepts a parsing callback; Gameplay supplies its full `EntityConfig` parser in `crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/entities/include_resolve.rs:10` and `:16`. Content cannot import Gameplay without violating the declared dependency direction. `ResolvedTemplate` retains TOML text, a value and provenance (`include_resolve.rs:465`): these are not interchangeable surplus representations. Original text is retained for uncomposed source locations, composed text feeds parsing/digests, and Workshop consumes provenance at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/workshop/entity.rs:775` and `:784`. Parent integration tests in `entities/include_resolve_tests.rs:700` and `:968` cover provenance correspondence and reporting. I found no defensible deletion of provenance or the injection seam.

- **Asynchronous preload has real state distinctions.** `template_preload.rs:4` separates delivered raw files, paths requested as entity roots, and settled resolutions. A fragment can later be promoted to a root, fragments must not automatically become spawnable, and a terminal failure must not be emitted repeatedly. The browser delivery path repeatedly drains after caching reveals nested entity templates (`crates/phoenix-simulation/src/entities/config_cache.rs:466`, `:500`). Existing tests at `config_cache_tests.rs:562`, `:624`, `:638`, `:679`, and `:712` exercise composition, once-only settlement, errors, shared dependencies and delivery ordering. `drain_resolved_templates` clones all raw strings at `template_preload.rs:82`; this is a plausible allocation simplification candidate, but no measured loading regression was established. Replacing the small scan with dependency indexes or a generalized task graph would add machinery without demonstrated need, so it is not recommended by this audit.

- **Native/WASM overlay storage is required adaptation.** `overlay.rs:95` explains why browser thread-local storage and native process-global storage differ. The native lobby installs on Bevy workers while other systems and delivery threads read the stack. The target-specific accessors centralize that distinction rather than spreading it through callers. Immutable asset bytes, the original source archive and the revision counter serve different consumers; model caches react to revision changes, and Workshop needs the exact accepted source. Simplifying to one thread-local store would break native reads. Test guards address the resulting shared native state rather than constituting an optional framework.

- **The live/frozen ledger and uncovered-spawn diagnostics protect resume semantics.** `ledger.rs:155` freezes a source set; `:163` serves it; `:210` reports computed spawn paths absent from that set without mutating the resume identity according to mission progress. Existing tests in `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/content_ledger_tests.rs:14`, `:44`, `:63`, `:204` and `:249` cover ordering, freeze stability, path normalization, script-only template edits and once-only reporting. Deleting the frozen/live distinction would change content identity, not merely simplify storage.

- **Archive and asset validation are admission safeguards, not a second renderer.** `archive.rs:146` validates local headers, CRCs, duplicate names, central-directory agreement and the complete envelope. `pack_asset_validation.rs:216` provides dependency preflight separately from decoding at `:269`; browser preflight uses it in `src/server/bridge.rs:3997`, while runtime pack admission calls member validation in `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/world/mod_pack.rs:444`. Workshop also calls it at `workshop/mod.rs:413`. Metadata validation refuses values outside the browser's 32-bit address space before glTF accessors cast them (`pack_asset_validation/metadata.rs:6`), while semantics checks reflect Bevy reader requirements (`semantics.rs:1`). Tests explicitly exercise unsafe references, sparse expansion, cycles, depth, topology, decoded external bytes and compressed-texture fallbacks in `pack_asset_validation_tests.rs:6`, `:28`, `:60`, `:97`, `:118`, `:175`, and `:229`. Removing these checks on the assumption that the glTF library or renderer already performs them is not supported.

#### Coverage limits

The report's positive finding is based on a repository-wide symbol trace, not a claim about external crate users. Asset metadata/semantic validation and the large include/provenance test suites were sampled rather than inspected statement by statement. String Table parsing, sound-cue rules, manifest decoding and rig path helpers were read, but their complete browser counterparts and all UI consumers were not audited for equivalence. No performance measurements, adversarial fuzzing, renderer execution, native/WASM parity run or validation suite was executed. Compatibility facades, independent test placement and deliberate JavaScript/Rust protocol counterparts were not treated as evidence of overengineering.


Audit 34/72 · Agent: `/root/audit34_content_reuse` · Lens: reuse

### Reuse

Module: Content preparation (`crates/phoenix-content/`).

Scope: read-only audit of the supplied source worktree, `C:/Users/jkeyw/.codex/worktrees/reusable-game-layers/project-phoenix-v2`, against source baseline `07fc2e025bb2f5385b2fadf4fb468039b77c0b4e` and its documented documentation-only successor. Inspected the module inventory and manifest; archive/path admission; entity merge policies; include path resolution; template preload; ledger normalization; overlay lookup; manifest parsing; string catalogue parsing; sound catalogue validation; rig helpers; and the asset-validation entry points. Sampled asset metadata safeguards and associated tests. Traced representative callers in simulation pack admission, Workshop, native private audition and rig loading. Read the workspace/wiki guidance, domain vocabulary, reusable-layer architecture, world-content-pack and authored-live-audio PASM slices. No nested AGENTS.md was found under `crates`; `docs/adr` is absent. No code was changed and no tests were executed.

#### 1. Sound catalogue path validation repeats the archive asset predicate, with a real additional restriction that must remain

**Evidence and callers.** `crates/phoenix-content/src/archive.rs:83` implements `is_pack_asset_path`: nonempty slash components, no `.` or `..`, no trailing dot/space, no backslash/colon/control characters, followed by supported directory/extension combinations. Its sound branch at `archive.rs:100` accepts `assets/sounds/` and `wav`, `ogg`, or `mp3`. `crates/phoenix-content/src/sound_cues.rs:63` independently repeats the same component checks, sound directory and extensions. It additionally rejects `%`, `?`, `#`, `<`, `>`, `"`, `|` and `*`.

Both are active admission rules. `is_allowed_content_path` delegates to the archive predicate at `archive.rs:50`; simulation pack admission checks allowed paths at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/world/mod_pack.rs:497`. Sound catalogues call the private sound predicate from `Catalog::validate` (`sound_cues.rs:94`) and `validate_all` (`sound_cues.rs:145`). Pack admission calls `validate_sound_catalog` at `mod_pack.rs:537`, Workshop does so at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/workshop/mod.rs:442`, and native private audition enters `validate_definition` at `src/native_host/audio/private_audition.rs:50`.

**Opportunity.** Reuse `archive::is_pack_asset_path` as the baseline in the sound predicate, keeping an explicit sound-directory check and the additional forbidden-character check. This removes a repeated component walker and repeated extension list without changing either public contract. It is a small local cleanup, not a reason to introduce a general path-policy framework.

**Compatibility details.** A direct replacement with `is_pack_asset_path` alone would be wrong: that function also accepts model and image paths, and it deliberately has a broader accepted character set. Preserve the sound-only namespace and the stricter sound-reference characters. Conversely, tightening the archive predicate globally would change accepted model/texture/audio member names and is not justified by this reuse finding. Do not replace either predicate with include canonicalization: `include_resolve.rs:544` normalizes paths, whereas these predicates reject noncanonical member names. Preserve nested sound folders, interior spaces, uppercase filename characters and lowercase recognized extensions. The proposed factoring preserves the current `assets/sounds/custom/sonar ping.wav` case.

**Why it matters.** Two predicates maintain the same portable path baseline, so a future baseline correction can reach one admission route but miss the other. Today their extra restrictions are visibly different, not proven bugs. The gain is one implementation of their common baseline, with a clearly retained stricter sound policy.

**Verification.** Existing coverage includes `sound_cues_tests.rs:41` for nested/custom paths and traversal refusal; `pack_asset_validation_sound_catalog_tests.rs:4` for local sound dependencies, rejection of remote/traversing references and one decode for multiple cues; and `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/world/mod_pack_tests.rs:240` for malformed portable asset paths. Before consolidating, add a table of the predicates' intentional differences: archive acceptance versus sound rejection for `%`, `?`, `#`, angle brackets, quote, pipe and star; rejection of empty/dot/trailing-dot/trailing-space components by both; and rejection of otherwise valid non-sound assets by the sound predicate. The sampled tests do not establish that full differential matrix.

**Tradeoff and confidence.** High confidence in the duplicated baseline and exact policy difference; moderate value because the duplication is short. Coupling sound validation to archive classification makes future widening of audio formats shared, which is desirable only if the additional sound-specific restriction remains explicit. No performance claim is made.

#### 2. Similar implementations that should remain distinct, or are already shared

These are investigated non-findings, included to distinguish actual missed reuse from apparent duplication.

**Entity composition versus instance override.** `entity_override.rs:250` delegates to the policy-aware implementation at `:262`; its path-aware walker at `:330` shares mechanics across the two policies. `MergePolicy::array_rule` at `:194` supplies the distinction, and include provenance uses that same policy vocabulary. Fragment tags union; instance tags replace. Only fragment composition accepts tombstones, while instance overrides reject them before merging. Empty authored arrays clear the list and matched keyed entries retain their position. The plain `merge_toml` at `:16` is explicitly retained as the structural reference used by differential tests; substituting it for the entity merge would lose doctrine and system-array semantics. Existing coverage in `entity_override_tests.rs:697`, `:739`, `:748`, `:785` and `:831` pins tombstone rejection, clearing, nested arrays, ordering and whole AI-rule replacement. No additional reusable generic merge implementation was identified that preserves these semantics. Keep the shared policy engine and independent reference coverage.

**Ledger and include path normalization.** `ledger.rs:88` already delegates to `include_resolve::canonical_template_path` at `include_resolve.rs:544`. Template preload likewise uses this canonicalizer (`template_preload.rs:54`, `:69`) and the shared incremental include resolver (`:81`). The separate asset `local_reference` at `pack_asset_validation.rs:66` is not an overlooked replacement opportunity: it rejects URI punctuation and empty path segments and bounds parent traversal to the content root. Include paths intentionally admit lexical normalization and relative `..`. Sharing the normalization function without the correct admission policy would weaken one contract or reject valid authored includes.

**Archive integrity and JavaScript counterparts.** CRC is already `vellum_digest::crc32_ieee`, re-exported at `archive.rs:107`; there is no local Rust CRC algorithm to delete. The text ZIP reader at `:132` delegates to the binary reader at `:146`, preserving exact binary members and applying UTF-8 conversion only in the text facade. The Rust archive reader and `editor/mod-pack-export.js` are target-language counterparts, not two replaceable same-language helpers. The Rust reader's local/central-directory agreement, duplicate rejection and complete-envelope checks are intentional. `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/world/mod_pack.rs:50` is a re-export, not a second implementation. Existing archive cases at `mod_pack_tests.rs:240` and `:256` guard duplicate names and ambiguous/incomplete envelopes. No already-used equivalent Rust ZIP dependency was found in the inspected Cargo declarations/search.

**CSV counterparts.** `string_catalogue.rs:45` and `gui/csv.js:2` implement closely corresponding state-machine parsers, including doubled quotes, multiline fields and malformed unterminated-quote rejection. Both remain needed because native Rust admission and pure-JavaScript clients cannot directly call the other implementation. The production Rust caller is pack admission at `mod_pack.rs:526`. The JS implementation is already shared by runtime strings, catalogue handling, Workshop localization and scripts. The Rust parser strips all leading BOM characters whereas the JS parser skips one leading BOM; this is a small observable difference, not evidence that Rust should import browser code. Shared parity fixtures would be the safe reuse surface if extending this area. Existing integration cases at `mod_pack_tests.rs:666`, `:682`, and `:715` cover translation-only packs, malformed/schema rejection and nonblocking fallback diagnostics. No existing Rust CSV dependency suitable for a behavior-preserving substitution was established by this audit.

**Asset validation uses the actual dependencies, with additional admission guarantees.** `pack_asset_validation.rs:53` calls Bevy image decoding, `:274` calls the Platform audio decoder, and `pack_asset_validation/metadata.rs:6` invokes glTF parsing and ultimately the library's normal validation. The surrounding metadata checks guard raw references and portable counts before typed access; they are not a redundant handwritten replacement for the library validator. The dependency-name walk at `pack_asset_validation.rs:216` and complete validation at `:269` serve different stages: preflight must identify immutable dependencies before their bytes are available, while complete validation checks resolved bytes, accessor spans and renderer constraints. A shared prepared-model cache might reduce repeated work, but no existing equivalent cache was identified; that would be a new design rather than reuse and is not recommended here. Tests in `pack_asset_validation_tests.rs:15`, `:28`, `:60`, `:118`, `:151` and metadata tests exercise the extra refusal guarantees.

**Rigs, manifests and overlays.** The rig parser and path helpers live once in Content (`rig.rs:3`, `:23`, `:49`); `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/entities/model_rig.rs:197` re-exports them. The pack manifest parser (`manifest.rs:142`) reuses the base scenario parser and adds pack identity rather than duplicating `ScenarioEntry`. Overlay text lookup (`overlay.rs:42`) and provenance lookup (`:51`) are deliberately different: provenance checks both text and binary members, while text lookup returns text only. Combining these purely because both iterate newest-first would risk changing binary provenance or text precedence. No deletion is proposed for these facades or policy-specific lookups.

#### Coverage limitations

This was a source audit, not a runtime or exhaustive dependency-library review. The full glTF semantics implementation, every include provenance branch, the complete native/browser overlay lifecycle, all downstream ledger freeze/restore consumers and every integration fixture were not read line by line. External library internals were not inspected. Representative callsites and tests were checked without executing them. Searches across adjacent modules were for concrete existing alternatives, not a full audit of those modules. Beyond the narrow sound-path baseline opportunity above, the inspected similarities do not establish additional safe implementation consolidation.
