No nested AGENTS.md was found; `docs/adr/` is absent in this worktree.

This is an architectural inspection, not a correctness certification. No builds or tests were run, per the audit instructions. Findings below describe opportunities rather than observed production failures.

1. **Deepen the accepted-pack handoff so host adapters do not reconstruct acceptance invariants.**

   **Files:** `crates/phoenix-content/src/overlay.rs:7`, `:174`, `:180`; `crates/phoenix-content/src/manifest.rs:130`, `:142`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/world/mod_pack.rs:54`, `:62`, `:72`, `:322`, `:638`; `src/server/bridge.rs:3952`; `src/native_host/host_lobby/packs.rs:302`, `:313`, `:318`.

   **Problem and observed callers:** The content overlay module advertises that `push_mod_pack` receives a validated pack and that nothing partial is installed. Its actual interface accepts an unrestricted `ActivePack` whose identity, files, assets, source archive and manifest are all public and separately assignable. The semantic validator appropriately resides in Simulation, but its `ValidatedModPack` also exposes loose fields and a separate `is_accepted()` check. Both real host adapters must know the complete handoff ritual: inspect findings, gate on acceptance, reparse the already-validated manifest, extract `(id, name, version)`, convert the text map, preserve all binary/source fields, then push the overlay.

   The browser and native callers each turn a failed manifest reparse or absent header into an empty identity with `unwrap_or_default()`. I did not establish a reachable path where a successful validator currently returns an invalid manifest; the concern is that the interface permits such drift and callers silently choose a fallback for an invariant supposedly established below them. Adding another accepted field requires edits to multiple adapters, and the public overlay mutation itself cannot distinguish accepted production content from a test fixture.

   **Solution:** Concentrate the accepted-result conversion and its relationship to findings in the module that already performs semantic validation. Have the host adapters transfer the prepared accepted pack rather than repeat identity extraction and field assembly. Keep Content's storage implementation independent of the semantic validator: this is a deepening of the validation-to-installation seam across the existing dependency direction, not a proposal for Content to import Simulation. Do not add a generic installer abstraction or redesign the public shape in this audit; the concrete opportunity is to discharge the existing acceptance obligation once.

   **Benefits / deletion test:** Deleting either host's field-by-field conversion after concentrating it removes duplicated invariant knowledge; deleting the validator or overlay would redistribute substantial behavior into callers and would be harmful. The leverage is that both native and browser adapters receive the same accepted content, identity and archive retention without learning the internal result layout. Locality improves for new pack members and diagnostics because their acceptance-to-installation handling changes once. The interface becomes a useful test surface for proving refusal leaves the active stack unchanged and success transfers exactly the candidate that was checked.

   **Behavior that must survive:** Errors block the whole pack while warnings remain non-blocking; validation sees candidate content before active overlays before base content; later accepted packs shadow earlier ones without clearing the stack; duplicate-pack-id refusal remains in semantic validation; exact source archive bytes remain available for Workshop cloning; immutable binary assets survive installation; revision changes continue to refresh presentation caches. These are current requirements, including the atomic push described in `pasm/spec/architecture/editor-authoring.yaml:164`. Do not relocate entity/world semantic validation into Content: `pasm/spec/architecture/reusable-layers.yaml` explicitly keeps it beside the simulation registries.

   **Existing tests and missing verification:** `src/native_host/host_lobby/packs_tests.rs:59` tests that a non-archive is refused without installation, and `:82` covers conflict reporting. Simulation's `world/mod_pack_tests.rs` exercises the semantic validator, while `entities/config_cache_tests.rs:360` and following exercise overlay mutation and precedence. Presentation's `entities/pack_assets_tests.rs:37` and following exercise binary asset installation, reorder and removal. These remain valuable adapter/integration tests. Add or concentrate direct tests of the shared accepted handoff: non-blocking warnings, matching identity, exact text/binary/source-archive transfer, and no accepted result on rejection. Native tests alone do not exercise the wasm-gated bridge body; both callers should consume the same tested conversion.

   **Tradeoffs, confidence and limitations:** Moderate implementation scope because semantic validation and storage deliberately have different owners. A sealed accepted type is not automatically required, and low-level fixture construction may remain useful. Confidence is high that the caller knowledge is duplicated, medium on priority: the two existing callers currently perform the same guarded sequence, so this is prevention and locality improvement rather than evidence of an acceptance bypass.

2. **Give the extracted content mechanisms an owning-crate test surface, while retaining Simulation's integration tests.**

   **Files:** `crates/phoenix-content/src/lib.rs:5`, `:6`, `:7`, `:9`, `:10`; `crates/phoenix-content/src/template_preload.rs:81`, `:116`; `crates/phoenix-content/src/ledger.rs:141`, `:155`, `:233`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/entities/config_cache.rs:169`, `:211`, `:1435`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/entities/config_cache_tests.rs:560`, `:609`, `:624`, `:638`, `:679`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/entities/include_resolve.rs:4`, `:97`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/content_ledger.rs:2`, `:151`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/content_ledger_tests.rs:4`, `:14`, `:30`, `:44`, `:63`, `:249`.

   **Problem and observed callers:** Several substantial Content implementations were extracted, but mechanism-only regression tests still live in Simulation and are reached through compatibility re-exports. For example, `a_delivered_fragment_is_never_offered_as_an_entity_template` only clears preload state, records raw text, drains results and checks that no entity appeared. Exactly-once emission, cycle settlement and shared-fragment deduplication likewise exercise the content state machine. Basic ledger tests cover reset, order-independent folding, changed bytes, freezing and canonical path spelling without requiring simulation behavior. These are precisely the public behaviors the owning Content module promises.

   Consequently, the prescribed owner-targeted iteration command for Content does not run those regression tests. A maintainer following the crate's production files must discover a second package's compatibility facade to find the test surface. This reduces locality and obscures which behavior can be verified without game assembly. This is not an assertion that workspace CI lacks coverage: the existing tests still execute when Simulation is tested.

   **Solution:** Place the mechanism-only tests beside their Content implementation using the repository's sibling-test-file convention. Keep tests that parse complete EntityConfig values, preload actual world references, bind script-only templates, interact with host caches or validate full packs in their current game-owning modules. For include/preload tests that mix both responsibilities, preserve the integration assertion and identify the content-only behavior separately rather than moving game parsers downward. Use Content's public interface as the test surface; do not expose internals just to relocate tests.

   **Benefits / deletion test:** This improves test locality rather than reducing production lines. It does not justify deleting compatibility facades: they preserve real callers and are explicitly part of the layer architecture. Nor should tests be deleted merely because they resemble each other. Moving a truly content-only case makes the owner-targeted test command verify the behavior owned there; preserving the adapter-level tests verifies that callers use the seam correctly. Leverage comes from being able to change the include/preload or digest implementation with fast, relevant feedback in its owning package.

   **Behavior that must survive:** Native/WASM path canonicalization, deterministic ordered ledger folding, frozen digest stability under later lazy spawns, one warning per uncovered path, include cycles as permanent errors, fragments never becoming spawnable merely because they arrived, and each entity root being emitted at most once. Do not collapse `ComposeFragments` and instance-override policies: their different treatment of tags, keyed arrays and removal markers is intentional and already tested in Content's `entity_override_tests.rs`.

   **Existing tests and missing verification:** The cited tests are existing coverage to preserve, not proposed new quotas. `crates/phoenix-content/src/pack_asset_validation_tests.rs`, its metadata/semantics tests, `entity_override_tests.rs` and `sound_cues_tests.rs` already demonstrate the owning-crate arrangement. The gap is distribution of mechanism tests, not a blanket absence of tests. No claim is made that every include-resolution test can move unchanged: Simulation's `ResolvedTemplate` parsing adapters and world-aware ledger tests have legitimate dependencies and belong above Content.

   **Tradeoffs, confidence and limitations:** Mostly organizational work, and less urgent than a functional regression. Splitting mixed tests can accidentally reduce integration coverage or duplicate fixtures; review each case rather than relocating files wholesale. Confidence is high for the quoted pure ledger and fragment-only tests, moderate for a broader include/preload migration until each test's parser dependency is classified.

Inspected structures that do not warrant an architecture finding:

- The include resolver is a deep module. `include_resolve.rs:606` and `:633` share closure walking while expressing final absence versus pending fragments. The filesystem/host adapters in Simulation and pack-local `PackFragments` are real variations, not a hypothetical seam. Removing this module would spread composition, provenance and error handling across hosts and validators.
- Native overlay storage differs from browser storage intentionally. `overlay.rs:89` onward explains native worker/delivery-thread access and keeps the public reader behavior consistent. `config_cache_tests.rs:412` exercises cross-thread visibility. Replacing native storage with thread-local storage to match the browser would discard a demonstrated requirement.
- The ledger's thread-local storage should not be called a bug merely because the overlay is process-global: `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/world/server.rs:1262` explicitly documents exclusive host-thread freeze execution. This audit did not prove an offending ledger reader/writer schedule and does not recommend a global ledger replacement.
- Content's use of actual image/audio decoders is purposeful asset admission, not evidence that content preparation should depend on the whole Presentation module. The implementation validates what later consumers can load. Independent simulation siblings and their tests must remain independent.

Unexplored or only sampled areas: exhaustive ZIP malformed-input handling; every glTF accessor, topology and expansion-limit path; all CSV edge cases and browser localisation counterparts; complete sound decoder behavior; rig sidecar consumer behavior; every include-provenance branch; all live browser preload callbacks and same-page lifecycle transitions; all pack tests and runtime scheduling paths. The two findings are grounded in the traced handoff and test ownership, not an exhaustive claim about those areas.

#### 1. Retire the unused structural merge implementation, keeping its six behavioral tests on the real instance merge

**Files and observed callers.** `crates/phoenix-content/src/entity_override.rs:16` implements a complete recursive structural `merge_toml`. Its documentation at line 1 explicitly says no entity path calls it anymore, but still describes a differential-test reason to keep it public. A repository-wide Rust symbol search found only its own recursive call and six calls in `crates/phoenix-content/src/entity_override_tests.rs:47`, `:55`, `:151`, `:170`, `:183` and `:202`; no current differential consumer was found. The live recursive merge is separately implemented by `merge_at` at `crates/phoenix-content/src/entity_override.rs:330`, reached through `merge_entity_config_toml` at line 250 and `merge_entity_config_toml_with` at line 262. The Gameplay and Simulation modules re-export this public surface (`crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/entities/entity_override.rs:2`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/entities/entity_override.rs:1`), but those facades do not supply a runtime caller of the old algorithm.

**Concrete deletion.** Replace those six test invocations with the already-existing `instance` test helper (`crates/phoenix-content/src/entity_override_tests.rs:7`), then remove the obsolete structural implementation. These fixtures cover scalar replacement, ordinary array replacement, recursive table preservation, adding a missing section, false-valued scalar replacement and sorted table keys. None supplies doctrine or a tombstone, so all six operations have the same intended result under the real instance policy. Keep all assertions and fixtures. Update the nearby references to the retired function so they describe the live structural behavior accurately.

**Why this matters.** The current tests spend coverage on an implementation production does not execute. Deleting it reduces maintained algorithms and makes those same assertions detect regressions in the merge that actually prepares entity content. This is true deletion, not relocation of an old algorithm into a test module, and requires no new abstraction.

**Required invariants.** Do not make the instance and fragment policies identical. Instance tags still replace; fragment tags union; doctrine reconciles by identity; keyed fragment entries retain inherited order; empty arrays clear; instance `_remove` markers are rejected; fragment tombstones are consumed. Keep the policy-aware merge and provenance table intact. The six substitutions are justified by these particular fixtures, not by a claim that the old and current algorithms are equivalent for all TOML.

**Coverage and missing verification.** Retain the six tests above and the policy-specific tests starting at `entity_override_tests.rs:76`, `:99`, `:122`, `:514` and `:602`. Include integration tests such as `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/entities/include_resolve_tests.rs:666` (shield-arc ordering), `:705` (keyed provenance), `:748` (removed provenance) and `:1107` (byte stability) continue to cover behavior downstream of the unchanged live algorithm. Before implementation, repeat the whole-repository caller search, including any newly introduced consumers. Targeted Content tests and the relevant Simulation merge/include tests would verify the change; this audit did not execute them.

**Savings.** Approximately 20 implementation lines (the function at lines 16–35), with six same-size test-call substitutions. Obsolete API documentation would also need updating, but documentation removal is not included in the savings estimate.

**Tradeoffs, confidence and limitations.** High confidence for in-repository runtime behavior and coverage preservation. The old function is public through compatibility facades; an external consumer absent from this checkout cannot be excluded by repository search. If compatibility policy explicitly requires preserving that API, retain it and do not report a deletion saving. Adding a generic structural-policy mode to the live merge merely to preserve an unused API would likely cost more complexity than these lines justify.

#### 2. Share the identical error/warning finding assembly without changing the caller-facing helpers

**Files and observed callers.** `crates/phoenix-content/src/findings.rs:44` and `:63` repeat the same `WorldFinding` and `SourceLocation` construction, including path/reference ownership and `line_of` lookup. Their sole behavioral difference is `Severity::Error` versus `Severity::Warning`. Both are real APIs: `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/world/validate.rs:251` calls `error`, while lines 295 and 323 call `warning`. The smaller archive-scoped helpers already demonstrate this pattern: `crates/phoenix-content/src/string_catalogue.rs:28` delegates error creation to the severity-parameterized constructor, and member helpers at lines 34 and 40 adjust the source file on an existing finding.

**Concrete deletion.** Keep the two named methods and their signatures. Let the warning helper use the existing construction path and change only the resulting severity, deleting its duplicate struct literal. Alternatively, a private common constructor can serve both, but its argument list and wrappers should be counted before adopting it: with only two callers, a new six-argument constructor can erase the line saving. No registry, builder object or public generic diagnostic interface is needed.

**Why this matters.** The shared source-location behavior needs only one maintained implementation. Call sites keep the explicit semantic distinction between warning and error. The proposed reduction deletes repeated field assembly rather than compressing formatting or moving it elsewhere.

**Required invariants.** Preserve exact severity, category, message, file, reference and the first matching one-based source line. Empty or absent needles must still yield `None` (`crates/phoenix-content/src/findings.rs:90`). Archive-scoped findings must retain `line: None`; this is not a proposal to replace archive creation with a fabricated source-text lookup. `has_error` at line 84 must continue to distinguish activation-blocking findings.

**Savings.** Roughly 7–9 implementation lines, depending on the formatting of the delegation. This is a small cleanup suitable when touching findings, not a reason for a broad refactor or a claim of material crate-wide shrinkage.

**Tradeoffs and confidence.** High confidence that assembly is identical. Delegating warning construction through a method named `error` is slightly less direct than the current literal; if that harms local clarity, the small line saving is not worth pursuing. A shared private constructor has clearer semantics but needs an actual net-line calculation before being described as a LOC improvement.

#### Reviewed candidates that should not be counted as deletions

The shared accessors at lines 142–168 already contain the target split. Native readers operate across Bevy workers and delivery threads; replacing the lock with thread-local state to remove branches changes which packs readers see. Keep revision invalidation, newest-wins lookup (`:42`, `:222`), ordered conflict reporting (`:62`), reorder semantics (`:198`), and the native test guard (`:287`). The text and binary maps serve different accepted content and are not redundant copies of one payload. No justified LOC reduction identified here.
- **Ledger live/frozen state.** `crates/phoenix-content/src/ledger.rs:64` onward holds live content, frozen digest input and once-per-path notices. These are distinct states. Deleting the freeze or notice state would either make digest identity depend on mission progress or lose the computed-template diagnostic. `normalize_key` at line 88 already delegates to the canonical include-path mechanism. Tests at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/content_ledger_tests.rs:44`, `:63`, `:249` and `:287` explicitly cover freeze stability, slash equivalence, notice deduplication and reset. Replacing the small forwarding function with a re-export is not a substantive maintained-logic saving.
- **Two archive output forms.** `crates/phoenix-content/src/archive.rs:132` is an UTF-8 projection of the one byte decoder at line 146, not a second ZIP parser. Existing Workshop and mod-pack tests use the text form (`crates/phoenix-simulation/src/world/mod_pack_tests.rs:411`; `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/workshop/tests.rs:183`). Moving decoding into tests or repeating conversions at each caller would not reduce total maintained work. The local-header, central-directory and end-record checks establish agreement and completeness; none was shown redundant merely because another ZIP reader might accept the same happy-path file. The deliberate JavaScript producer/reader counterpart remains required by pure-JavaScript authoring.
- **Manifest readers.** `crates/phoenix-content/src/manifest.rs:142` parses the shared scenario schema and pack header separately. A flattened deserialize wrapper might remove a few statements or one parse, but may change error precedence and diagnostics for malformed documents. Existing tests at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/world/manifest_tests.rs:819`, `:835` and `:847` cover header, defaults and missing-header behavior. No proven net LOC benefit justifies changing that contract on this evidence. The separate optional content-identity read at `manifest.rs:76` also intentionally does not impose identity requirements on the scenario-list parser.
- **Admission validation beyond upstream libraries.** `crates/phoenix-content/src/pack_asset_validation/metadata.rs:7` validates unsafe raw indices and portable sizes before upstream glTF validation; subsequent passes constrain hierarchy, expanded allocation, supported renderer formats and byte ranges. These are different guarantees. Tests at `metadata_tests.rs:4`, `:15`, `:58`, `:77` and `:96` pin adversarial cases. Calling an upstream parser and deleting those passes would not preserve admission semantics. The image/audio decode paths already reuse the production libraries/platform decoder (`pack_asset_validation.rs:269`).
- **Similar path rules and language counterparts.** Include paths lexically collapse parent segments (`include_resolve.rs:544`), archive members enforce an allowed namespace (`archive.rs:42`), asset references prohibit URI escapes (`pack_asset_validation.rs:66`), and sound file names apply additional forbidden-character rules (`sound_cues.rs:63`). Treating these as identical normalization is unsafe. No existing Rust CSV replacement was found for `string_catalogue.rs:45`; adding a dependency or routing through JavaScript is not a demonstrated behavior-preserving deletion. The tiny codec facade and rig/model re-exports are deliberate seams, not duplicated implementations.

#### Limits of inspection

All production files in the Content crate were inspected, but supporting tests outside it were sampled and searched rather than reviewed exhaustively. Asset-admission test fixtures, every native host acceptance test, external users of public re-exports, and exact upstream-library behavior beyond what the checked-in adapters/tests state were not exhaustively inspected. No performance or allocation measurements were made. The report claims only the countable local reductions above; it does not infer an unobserved large deletion opportunity from the crate's documentation volume or test volume.
