Inspected owner-local test cases and selected native host callers/tests, content audio admission, simulation compatibility facades, and the pinned Vellum surface/pane/runtime APIs. Read the workspace instructions, wiki schema/index, vocabulary, reusable-layer design and relevant native-delivery design material. No nested crate AGENTS.md was returned by the search; `docs/adr` is absent in this source tree. No tests, builds, SDK or hardware runs were performed.

#### 1. The captured-pointer path duplicates the ordinary hit path's coordinate conversion

**Evidence and actual callers.** `crates/phoenix-platform/src/input_routing.rs:78` implements `PanePlacement::local_of_window`: subtract the pane rectangle origin, choose the window scale when positive or fall back to 1.0, divide, and cast to `i32`. `PaneRouter::resolve_in_window` uses it at line 154 after checking containment; `resolve_desktop` uses the same helper at line 167 after removing the desktop origin. However, `PaneRouter::project_into_pane` at line 179 repeats the entire scale fallback, offset, division and cast at lines 181–189. Both implementations operate on the same `PanePlacement`, window-local physical input, and CSS output. The duplicated block is not a separate native/WASM implementation or a game-specific policy.

The second path has real callers: `src/native_host/panes/ultralight.rs:1901` and `:1927` project mouse capture motion/releases, and `:2040` and `:2050` project touch motion/releases. These callers require capture to survive a pointer moving outside its starting pane. Ordinary pointer hit selection uses the router's containment path. Their selection semantics differ, but their arithmetic does not.

**Concrete opportunity.** Inside `project_into_pane`, reuse `p.local_of_window(wx, wy)` and return its `local_x` and `local_y`. Correct the private helper's comment at `input_routing.rs:76`, which currently says it assumes an inside point: its body neither clamps nor tests containment, and it already computes valid signed coordinates outside the rectangle. Keep containment in the two hit-selection methods and keep the public return types intact. This requires no new abstraction, dependency, or interface.

**Why it matters.** A later change to DPI handling, fallback or coordinate rounding currently needs two matching edits to avoid a drag changing coordinates when capture takes over. The maintained duplicate is small, so this is a low-priority consistency improvement, not a performance or correctness emergency. Savings would also be small; the benefit is one implementation of the conversion rule.

**Behavior that must survive.** Preserve half-open hit bounds, desktop-to-window translation, truncating `as i32` conversion, existing nonpositive-scale fallback, no capture containment check, signed out-of-pane coordinates, and `None` for a missing pane. Do not replace capture projection with `resolve_in_window`, which would send the gesture to a different pane or lose it entirely.

**Verification.** Existing root tests include `a_drifted_contact_projects_past_its_pinned_panes_edge` at `src/native_host/input_routing_tests.rs:701` and `a_left_drag_keeps_its_down_and_up_on_the_pane_it_began_on` at `:722`. They verify the distinct capture behavior and missing-pane handling. The focused raster test at `crates/phoenix-platform/src/render_geometry_tests.rs:7` also exercises pointer coordinates independently of reduced render size. For a change, add a narrow equivalence case showing an inside point has identical ordinary-hit and explicit-projection coordinates at fractional scale, and an outside negative coordinate remains unclamped. Existing tests were read, not run.

**Tradeoffs, confidence and limits.** High confidence that the arithmetic is identical and directly reusable. The helper returns a `PaneHit`, so the capture path would briefly construct that small value to select two fields; this is simpler than maintaining a second formula. No claim that this establishes actual mixed-DPI or touch-device behavior; those remain hardware acceptance concerns.

#### Reviewed similarities that are justified, rather than reuse findings

- **Platform facades already share production implementations.** `src/native_host/input_routing.rs:5`, `src/native_host/bridge_profile.rs:82` and `:150`, `src/native_host/panes/upload.rs:2`, `src/native_host/panes/render_geometry.rs:2`, and `src/native_host/panes/pane_thread.rs:1366` re-export platform code. Likewise, `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/native_file.rs:2` and `native_capture.rs:1` preserve compatibility names. These are not parallel implementations to delete. Root tests exercising those paths continue to test the shared mechanisms through real application seams.

- **`FrameRect` versus Vellum `DirtyRect` is an explicitly separate boundary type.** `crates/phoenix-platform/src/frames.rs:32` documents why the protocol carries its own rectangle, and lines 50–102 provide its operations and conversions. In the external Vellum repository at pinned dependency revision `606b0c06a6e6419a5b2a8c4f5bfb255146f87814`, the inspected local dependency implements the same [full rectangle](C:/Users/jkeyw/.cargo/git/checkouts/vellum-5e38bc80bbf18dd2/606b0c0/crates/vellum-ultralight/src/surface.rs:53), [empty check](C:/Users/jkeyw/.cargo/git/checkouts/vellum-5e38bc80bbf18dd2/606b0c0/crates/vellum-ultralight/src/surface.rs:72) and [pixel count](C:/Users/jkeyw/.cargo/git/checkouts/vellum-5e38bc80bbf18dd2/606b0c0/crates/vellum-ultralight/src/surface.rs:110) semantics; I inspected that local dependency source, rather than inferring capability from its name. Vellum additionally clamps and unions dirty rectangles. Platform's type supplies `Serialize` and `const` methods while Vellum's corresponding methods are not `const`; replacing it with an alias changes more than a name. Platform explicitly wants producers that need not expose Vellum types. Keeping these few elementary operations local is justified. The relevant contract is half-open coordinates with degenerate rectangles empty and counts widened to `u64`. Do not replace upload refusal with Vellum's clamping: `upload.rs:147` rejects a stale/out-of-bounds region, which is deliberately different from producer-side dirty-bound clipping.

- **Ultralight support already delegates the SDK-specific work.** `crates/phoenix-platform/src/ultralight.rs:69` dispatches to the dependency's `copy_frame` or `copy_frame_bgra`; line 90 evaluates the supplied script; line 97 uses Vellum's `bridge::split_records`; lines 132–145 build a `PaneSpec`, request an ephemeral `PaneSession`, create the view and load its URL. The pinned dependency owns raw runtime/view operations and its surface module owns the pixel copying. Platform adds readiness, visibility application/retry, injected drain script and platform errors. Its `apply_visibility` at line 47 hides the document at the paint source while retaining subscriptions and queues, rather than merely suppressing a compositor texture. No interchangeable dependency method was established for that combined lifecycle. `surface.rs:33` and its recording implementation support SDK-free bridge tests; a recording test double is not a second renderer.

- **The frame pool and GPU upload are complementary to dependency surface copying.** `frames.rs:185` returns allocation ownership on drop, and `PooledFrames::configure` at `:291` replaces the return channel when configuring a generation. `upload.rs:287` implements bounded deferral, replacement and full-frame promotion; `:374` transfers main-world uploads into the render world; the render path at `:438` uses Bevy's `RenderQueue::write_texture`. These mechanisms do not reimplement Vellum's dirty-row copy. Nor would ordinary replacement of an `Image` asset have matching behavior: this path deliberately preserves the texture and its bind groups and uploads a strided subrectangle. Existing tests at `upload_tests.rs:149`, `:184`, `:206`, `:240`, `:268`, `:308` and `:332` cover layout, stale sizes, recycling, deferral/promotion, bounded retry and renderer-free disposal. The observations in `surface_stats.rs:379` follow the buffer's ownership with one terminal outcome; a timing log alone does not substitute for this lifetime trace. No established equivalent pool/upload/attribution implementation was found in the compared dependency APIs.

- **Monitor discovery and retained identity intentionally answer different questions.** `monitors.rs:110` assigns identities for a single enumeration; `:254` preserves known identities with ordered stronger-to-weaker matching. Its matching at `:210` accounts for twin arrival and resolution renegotiation using the previous roster. Host `src/native_host/bridge_profile.rs:1015` instead asks which authored assignments match currently present identity forms; it already reuses `identity_forms` from `monitors.rs:162`. Replacing either algorithm with the other would change its inputs and guarantees. Geometry tiling likewise has one shared implementation at `geometry.rs:45`; the two split-axis branches are a short implementation of different axes, not evidence of another competing tiler.

- **Native writes and capture policy are already consolidated without erasing caller policy.** `native_file.rs:33` provides the write/sync/rename mechanism; `write_preferences` at `:59` chooses the preference policy, while unique temporary naming uses exclusive creation. Preference crash-debris recognition at `:64` deliberately recognizes a narrower namespace than arbitrary `.tmp` files. The existing tests at `native_file_tests.rs:4` and `:44` pin replacement failure cleanup and name ownership. `native_capture.rs:8` walks only into callback-admitted directories; `:30` reads one overflow byte; `:57` checks the resulting map after insertion. Concrete callers include `src/native_host/workshop/mod.rs:162` (capture-wide byte accounting), `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/workshop/provider.rs:825` and `provider/test_snapshot.rs:81` (their own admission and map checks). Their policy differences are not a reason to collapse them into an indiscriminate recursive copy. The owner tests at `native_capture_tests.rs:20`, `:46`, `:60` and `:72` explicitly cover those semantics.

- **Audio is a wrapper around the already-used decoder, not a duplicate decoder.** `audio_decode.rs:21` calls Symphonia's probe/codecs and `SampleBuffer` interleaving, then enforces the additional mono/stereo, fixed format, finite-sample, nonempty and decoded-memory limits at lines 57–78. Content validation already reuses this production decoder at `crates/phoenix-content/src/pack_asset_validation.rs:274`. The authored PCM test is `audio_decode_tests.rs:2`. This audit found no second platform decoder to replace. The wrapper's admission rules cannot be dropped merely because Symphonia can decode the stream; malformed and unsupported-format coverage beyond the inspected PCM test would require a separate verification review.

#### Coverage limitations

The audit read all platform production modules but traced selected host callers rather than every native-host branch or every test fixture. It inspected the pinned Vellum surface semantics and runtime interface/dispatch, not proprietary SDK internals. It did not exhaustively inspect Bevy, wgpu or Symphonia internals or establish that every available dependency capability has been considered. It did not evaluate other auditors' findings. Native hardware input, monitor hotplug, GPU lifetime behavior, actual audio devices, and all-target compilation remain unverified here. No speculative library adoption is recommended, and the lack of another concrete reuse finding is limited to these inspected implementations and comparisons.


Audit 47/72 · Agent: `/root/audit47_platform_architecture` · Lens: architecture

### Architecture

Module: `crates/phoenix-platform/`.

Scope: read-only inspection of the extracted platform mechanisms at code baseline `07fc2e025bb2f5385b2fadf4fb468039b77c0b4e` in `C:/Users/jkeyw/.codex/worktrees/reusable-game-layers/project-phoenix-v2`. The audit instructions identify current HEAD `2de7d5329fba55e06ad00fbd8ca13f0cb4e8c73c` as a documentation-only successor. No builds, tests, hardware runs, or source changes were performed.

Inspected areas: package manifest and feature selection; module exports; document surfaces and the Ultralight adapter; frame buffers, staging pools and selected GPU upload paths; monitor identification and rematching; geometry and input-routing interfaces; native replacement and directory capture; audio decoding; observer interface declarations. Traced concrete consumption through native pane surfaces, the pane thread and Ultralight host, bridge-profile compatibility exports, simulation compatibility exports, native Workshop capture, content audio validation and Grid file output. Examined existing platform test declarations and selected native host tests, including pool exhaustion/recycling and monitor/geometry tests. Read the architecture skill and its language, repository operating instructions, wiki schema/index, domain vocabulary, reusable-layer guidance and PASM reusable-layer model; inspected relevant native-delivery and native-bridge-operation design material. No nested AGENTS.md appeared in the source inventory; `docs/adr/` was absent.

#### 1. Concentrate successful surface lifecycle changes and frame-pool reconfiguration at the same seam

**Files:** `crates/phoenix-platform/src/frames.rs:209`, `:274`, `:291`, `:319`; `src/native_host/panes/pane_thread.rs:491`, `:834`, `:888`, `:902`, `:1180`; `src/native_host/panes/pane_thread_doubles_tests.rs:202`.

**Problem:** The frame-pool module earns its keep, but its interface leaves a significant lifecycle obligation in a second layer of the caller. `PaneFrameSink` supports staging, publishing and dropping a pane, while `PooledFrames::configure` is outside that interface. The native thread's `apply_thread_command` separately decodes Create and Resize, derives buffer length and pixel mode, calls `PaneLoop::apply`, and then checks `driver.contains(id)` before configuring the concrete pool (`pane_thread.rs:497–515`). The loop itself handles the actual creation, resize, epoch update and forced-copy state (`:834–886`, `:902–916`); Close does reach the sink directly (`:888–900`). Thus the seam treats destruction as part of the operation while construction and resize require an outer coordination ritual.

The distinction matters beyond tidiness. `PooledFrames::stage` refuses a missing pool or a length mismatch (`frames.rs:320–329`); resize must replace the recycle channel even when byte length stays equal (`:272–306`). A caller can drive the public loop with the real pool and omit a step that the loop's type-level interface does not expose. The production wrapper currently supplies that step, so this is an architecture opportunity, not a demonstrated shipping failure. Tests also reveal the knowledge: `pane_thread_loop_tests.rs:80–89` manually configures the real pool before driving an iteration. The recording sink has its own convenient pool behavior (`pane_thread_doubles_tests.rs:245–266`), so using that adapter does not by itself establish correct orchestration with the production adapter.

**Solution:** Deepen the lifecycle operation so a successful Create or Resize and its necessary staging-pool transition are owned together, with the outer thread limited to command transport and event forwarding. Keep Station/HUD/lobby selection in the host: the platform implementation should receive mechanical pixel mode and dimensions, not learn `PaneKind` or game roles. This is a change in ownership of sequencing, not a proposal for a new public interface shape.

**Benefits:** Better locality for the invariant “the surface and its frame pool describe the same generation and size.” The leverage is a single operation that callers and tests can use without replaying a hidden setup recipe. It would let interface tests exercise production pooling during successful creation, failed creation, resize and close, instead of proving the loop and its surrounding wrapper separately.

**Deletion test:** Deleting `PooledFrames` would scatter bounded allocation, recycle-channel retirement, fill initialization and late-return protection into producers; do not delete it. Deleting the *outer command-specific lifecycle duplication*, after placing that responsibility with the operation that changes the surface, would remove caller knowledge. Moving all of `PaneLoop` into Platform would be the wrong response because it contains Phoenix bridge and role policy.

**Invariants and verification:** Preserve bounded three-buffer staging with the configured cap; no allocation-per-frame fallback; dirty pixels retained when staging is unavailable; opaque BGRA versus transparent RGBA fill; old-generation return channels retired even for equal-size resizes; frame epochs owned by the texture producer; close preventing resurrection; and unchanged reliable-message/admission behavior. Existing `src/native_host/panes/pane_thread_thread_tests.rs:393–424` directly verifies bounded recycling and equal-length generation isolation. `pane_thread_loop_tests.rs:411` covers attribution across resize. Platform upload tests verify recycling, deferred-upload supersession and renderer-less draining (`upload_tests.rs:206`, `:240`, `:268`, `:332`). Retain these and exercise the lifecycle seam with the real pool, particularly failed creation and resize with outstanding buffers. No existing test results were executed or claimed here.

**Tradeoffs, confidence and limits:** Medium-high confidence in the split responsibility; medium confidence that a refactor is worthwhile immediately. There is currently one production thread orchestrator, and its code is explicit and small. Avoid growing a large generalized lifecycle framework merely to hide that wrapper. The gain should be measured in eliminated caller obligations and more direct tests, not fewer source lines.

#### 2. Put reusable mechanism verification beside its owning module while retaining host-policy tests

**Files:** `crates/phoenix-platform/src/monitors.rs:110`, `:254`; `crates/phoenix-platform/src/geometry.rs:46`; `crates/phoenix-platform/src/input_routing.rs:113`; `crates/phoenix-platform/src/frames.rs:274`; `src/native_host/bridge_profile_tests.rs:26`, `:583`, `:1251`; `src/native_host/input_routing_tests.rs:439`; `src/native_host/panes/pane_thread_thread_tests.rs:393`.

**Problem:** Several substantial platform implementations have no local test module even though their pre-extraction tests remain in the root host. The monitor algorithms, pane tiling, focus traversal and production frame pool can be tested through the Platform interface without constructing a Phoenix host, yet their relevant assertions remain interleaved with native host policy tests. For example, monitor identity tests begin at `bridge_profile_tests.rs:26`, tiling tests at `:583`, and stable-rematching tests use `identify_stable` at `:1251`. The same file also checks Station counts, Viewscreen restrictions and profile serialization, which genuinely belong to the host. `input_routing_tests.rs:439–499` tests `FocusRing` directly. The production pool test at `pane_thread_thread_tests.rs:393` uses `PaneKind` only to select ordinary pixel modes.

This is not missing repository-wide coverage: those tests still exercise the extracted implementation through re-exports. The friction is locality and the package-level test surface. A maintainer working in Platform cannot find or run all of these reusable contracts in the owning package; focused validation of that package omits important behavior while root-host tests retain unnecessary knowledge of mechanism internals. By comparison, native file replacement, native capture, render geometry and upload already declare sibling tests in the platform package.

**Solution:** Move tests that exercise only platform interfaces into sibling test files of their owning Platform modules, using platform vocabulary and `PixelMode` rather than host `PaneKind`. Retain all integration tests that prove Phoenix choices and composition: Station occupancy rules, authored layout order, Viewscreen protection, role-specific visibility, input suppression, admission and recovery. Where an existing test mixes mechanism with policy, retain the host assertion and extract only an independently meaningful mechanism case; do not duplicate whole suites.

**Benefits:** Higher locality for future changes and greater leverage from owner-targeted validation. Tests and ordinary callers cross the same seam, and a platform change can be assessed without importing the game host. This also makes the claimed independent package easier to navigate: its algorithm, contract and evidence are found together.

**Deletion test:** Deleting the root's compatibility re-exports would not remove useful complexity; it would only churn established callers. They are explicitly required scaffolding and are not the target. Relocating mechanism tests preserves their behavior and moves their ownership; deleting them would reduce assurance and is not proposed.

**Invariants and verification:** Preserve literal fixtures and assertions when moving tests. Monitor identity must keep conservative collision/rematch ordering and its documented physical-device limitations; odd-pixel tiling must keep complete, non-overlapping coverage; focus ordering and captured-pointer behavior must stay unchanged; pool tests must preserve equal-byte-length generation cases. Keep the existing host integration tests to prove callers actually apply those answers. The intended follow-up validation would run the owning package with the relevant feature combinations and the affected root suites; the audit did not run either.

**Tradeoffs, confidence and limits:** High confidence in this locality issue and in the availability of already-written tests. The exact partition needs care: bridge-profile tests mix pure monitor rules with game-specific profile validation. This is a bounded architectural maintenance change, not additional runtime depth or a reason to create another abstraction. Do not claim that test relocation alone verifies real monitor hotplug, DPI, Ultralight or GPU behavior.

#### Qualified observations and opportunities not promoted to findings

- The document-surface seam is real: `RecordingSurface` and `UltralightSurface` both implement it. Its documentation at `surface.rs:37–38` says an encoded `ServerMessage` is pushed, while `ultralight.rs:90–94` evaluates a script and the real host constructs that script at `src/native_host/panes/surface.rs:128`. The recording adapter even has a Phoenix-specific failure string (`surface.rs:92`). Those are misleading residues in a reusable interface, but the inspected caller uses the correct script semantics; this does not establish a production defect or justify moving game-message serialization into Platform. Clarifying that contract is useful, but too small to sell as a separate deepening project.
- Native file replacement is already a deep module: Grid uses `native_file::replace` (`examples/grid/src/main.rs:69`), and host/simulation use compatibility paths. Temp ownership, sync, rename and cleanup stay together (`native_file.rs:33–55`). Preference and unique naming policies serve different existing callers; flattening them would move recovery assumptions outward.
- Native capture helpers are intentionally smaller. Their module comment assigns admission and check timing to callers (`native_capture.rs:1`), and actual callers differ: native Workshop checks remaining aggregate bytes (`src/native_host/workshop/mod.rs:180`), while test capture uses a per-file bounded read and insertion checks (`crates/phoenix-simulation/src/workshop/provider/test_snapshot.rs:87–88`). A generic capture engine that silently standardizes those checkpoints would change behavior. No such consolidation is recommended from this inspection.
- The monitor identity module carries substantial rematching behavior behind a small interface; its known limitations are explicitly documented at `monitors.rs:84–109` and `:246–250`. There is no evidence here for replacing that conservative strategy or pulling Station layout policy into it.
- Optional `audio`, `surface`, `render` and native Ultralight features are justified by distinct consuming configurations (`Cargo.toml:8–12`, `src/lib.rs:6–25`). The forbidden game dependencies do not appear in the platform manifest. The GPU upload adapter and Bevy-free frame lifetime implementation must remain usable under their intended feature selections; their separation is not accidental duplication.

Unexplored or only sampled: the full observer implementation and every observer test; every keyboard/touch routing branch; the entire root Ultralight host and all its frame-stat consumers; real SDK failure behavior and view/runtime destruction ordering; GPU pixel correctness after full-upload promotion; browser audio behavior; exhaustive filesystem race and Windows sharing behavior; and all workspace feature combinations. Host code was followed far enough to establish concrete consumers and sequencing, not audited exhaustively. Conclusions are source-based and confined to the opportunities above.


Audit 48/72 · Agent: `/root/audit48_platform_loc` · Lens: loc

### Reducing maintained lines

Module: Platform (`crates/phoenix-platform/`). Source inspected in `C:/Users/jkeyw/.codex/worktrees/reusable-game-layers/project-phoenix-v2`, at the assigned source baseline `07fc2e025bb2f5385b2fadf4fb468039b77c0b4e` plus the documented navigation-only commit.

Scope inspected: the manifest and module gates; all production files (`audio_decode`, `frames`, `geometry`, `input_routing`, `monitors`, `native_capture`, `native_file`, `render_geometry`, `surface`, `surface_stats`, `ultralight`, and `upload`); all five separate crate test files; relevant host input/geometry tests and selected host surface-attribution and frame-pool integration references. Read the project instructions, wiki schema, domain vocabulary, wiki navigation and reusable-layer guide, and relevant portions of the reusable-layer and native-host PASM slices. The source worktree has no `docs/adr` directory; the nested `AGENTS.md` search under `crates` returned no files.

This is a read-only source audit. No builds, tests, SDK or hardware runs were performed. Savings below mean removal of repeated implementation after accounting for its replacement, not moving lines into another crate, shortening documentation, or deleting tests.

#### 1. Reuse the existing coordinate transform for captured pointer projection

**Evidence and callers.** `crates/phoenix-platform/src/input_routing.rs:78` implements `PanePlacement::local_of_window`: select a positive scale factor or fall back to `1.0`, subtract the pane rectangle origin, divide each coordinate, and cast to `i32`. Both ordinary window hits (`input_routing.rs:150`) and desktop hits (`input_routing.rs:163`) already use it. `PaneRouter::project_into_pane` at `input_routing.rs:179` repeats exactly that scale selection and arithmetic, returning a tuple instead of a `PaneHit`.

The repetition is live: the native mouse adapter calls captured projection at `src/native_host/panes/ultralight.rs:1901` and `:1927`; the touch move/end paths use it at `:2040` and `:2050`. Ordinary hit routing is used at `:1838` and `:2009`. These are two routes into the same coordinate system, rather than two different transforms.

**Concrete reduction.** Keep the public methods and their return types. Inside `project_into_pane`, find the placement as today, call `p.local_of_window(wx, wy)`, and return its `local_x` and `local_y`. The existing mapping closure is eleven lines long; a closure containing a local hit and tuple is four lines, removing approximately seven implementation lines without introducing a helper or changing callers. Adjust the private helper's comment at `input_routing.rs:76`: the current statement that the point is assumed inside is unnecessary for its arithmetic and would be misleading once shared with capture.

**Why it matters.** This deletes a second implementation of the DPI fallback and rounding rule. Future fixes to physical-to-CSS conversion then apply equally to a fresh hit and a captured drag. The benefit is modest in absolute size, but does not require an abstraction or API expansion.

**Invariants.** Do not reuse `resolve_in_window` here: that would reintroduce containment and break capture. Captured points must remain negative or beyond the pane bounds when appropriate. Preserve `None` for a missing placement, the `> 0.0` scale predicate (including its existing behavior for NaN), subtract-before-divide order, Rust float-to-integer conversion, and use of physical display scale rather than the optional raster reduction scale. Window selection, desktop-origin subtraction and contact ownership remain outside this shared arithmetic.

**Existing verification and gaps.** `src/native_host/input_routing_tests.rs:44`, `:59`, `:74`, and `:87` cover ordinary conversion, scale, fractional scale and pane offset. `:701` checks projection beyond a captured pane and an unknown pane; `:722` exercises a cross-pane mouse drag. `crates/phoenix-platform/src/render_geometry_tests.rs:7` checks that half-size raster rendering retains the normal physical-to-CSS pointer coordinates. Those tests must survive unchanged. The inspected capture test uses scale `1.0`; add a narrow equivalence case with a fractional scale and nonzero pane offset, plus a negative captured coordinate, if implementing this change. A fallback-scale case would pin the exact existing predicate. No new hardware verification should be necessary for this arithmetic-only refactor, but tests were not run in this audit.

**Tradeoff, confidence and limitation.** High confidence: the arithmetic is textually identical and the existing helper performs no containment. The only clarity cost is that the capture path briefly constructs a `PaneHit` to return two fields; this is preferable to introducing a second shared coordinate type solely to save lines. This is a small maintenance improvement, not a performance claim.

#### 2. Share the axis-independent portion of pane tiling

**Evidence and callers.** `crates/phoenix-platform/src/geometry.rs:43` exposes `pane_rects`. Its two branches (`:52` and `:67`) each calculate the base extent, initialize an offset, iterate over the pane count, give all remaining pixels to the last pane, push a rectangle and advance the offset. The meaningful branch difference is the selected dimension and the mapping from offset/extent into `x`, `y`, `width`, and `height`.

`src/native_host/bridge_profile.rs:150` re-exports this implementation; it is not a second tiler. The live occupancy law uses it at `src/native_host/bridge_layout.rs:1042`, after selecting the monitor's authored split and whole occupancy. That caller is why replacing the general operation with separate one-pane/two-pane special cases would be a regression in ownership and generality.

**Concrete reduction.** Select the axis length once from `PaneSplit`, perform the base/remainder iteration once, and retain a small `match split` only when constructing the rectangle. Keep the public function, allocation and order unchanged. The current outer match occupies lines 51–80 (30 lines); a normally formatted shared loop with the two explicit rectangle literals is approximately 22–24 lines. Expected production saving is therefore only about 6–8 lines, subject to formatting, with one rather than two copies of remainder arithmetic. This is genuine deduplication of the algorithm, not a proposal to compress the struct literals onto single lines.

**Invariants.** Keep `count == 0` returning an empty vector, one pane covering the entire monitor, all earlier panes receiving the integer base extent, and only the last pane absorbing the remainder. In particular, do not switch to boundaries computed as `i * extent / count`, which distributes remainders differently for more than two panes. Preserve pane order, support for counts above two, zero-size dimensions and current integer-width semantics. Do not mix this physical tiling with `PaneRenderScale::geometry`, which controls raster size and device scale rather than display geometry. Keep authored split choice in the caller.

**Existing verification and gaps.** `src/native_host/bridge_profile_tests.rs:583`, `:597`, `:619`, and `:629` cover whole-monitor, side-by-side, odd-width and stacked tiling. Input tests at `src/native_host/input_routing_tests.rs:155` and `:184` consume that geometry for routing. `src/native_host/bridge_layout_tests.rs:1247` compares the live layout's answer against the profile's tiling, protecting the authored-order integration. Preserve all of these. Before implementation, add compact cases for zero panes, both splits with three panes and a remainder, and a dimension smaller than the count; those general cases are claimed by the public documentation but were not present in the directly inspected geometry tests. The additional verification may offset the small production line saving, which is acceptable: coverage must not be sacrificed for a net metric.

**Tradeoff, confidence and limitation.** Medium confidence as a worthwhile cleanup, high confidence that the algorithm is duplicated. A loop with axis selection adds conceptual indirection compared with two obvious branches. Adopt it only if the final implementation remains plainly readable; do not introduce an axis trait, closure-based rectangle factory or generic geometry framework for this saving. This is lower priority than finding 1.

#### Inspected candidates that do not justify deletion

- **Frame ownership and upload machinery are required behavior.** `crates/phoenix-platform/src/frames.rs:185` drops the trace before recycling the byte allocation. `:291` creates a new return channel on configuration, isolating old-generation returns even when byte counts match. `:309` and `:320` return staged and asynchronously returned allocations with length/cap checks. Extracting their tiny repeated push predicate into a method would add approximately as much code as it removes; deleting either path breaks a distinct lifetime route. `upload.rs:287` carries inherited deferral age, full-frame promotion and explicit superseded disposal. `upload.rs:351` installs different behavior with and without a render sub-app, and `:491` prevents renderer-less accumulation. These are not replaceable by a single unconditional upload or `Vec::clear`. Existing `upload_tests.rs:60` and `:116` cover attribution and renderer-less disposal; its subsequent tests cover layout bounds, recycling, deferral and promotion. No LOC recommendation depends on simplifying those contracts.

- **The platform rectangle and SDK rectangle intentionally have different ownership.** `frames.rs:30` explains why `FrameRect` exists independently of `vellum_ultralight::surface::DirtyRect`; conversion implementations at `:78` and `:89` keep the producer-facing vocabulary stable. Deleting the platform type merely to remove field-copy lines would leak the concrete producer through the reusable frame protocol. The optional `surface` dependency uses the SDK-independent half, while `lib.rs:21` separately gates the real SDK adapter to native targets. Retain this distinction and the `render` gate at `lib.rs:18`.

- **Atomic file policies are not duplicate writers.** `native_file.rs:32` already centralizes write, flush, close, rename and owned-temporary cleanup. Its two opening modes (`:39`) preserve preference replacement versus unique `create_new` ownership; their different names and recovery rules cannot be collapsed into one flagless path. Actual consumers include `src/native_host/layout_store.rs:119`, `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/workshop/provider.rs:1014`, and the independent Grid host at `examples/grid/src/main.rs:69`. `native_file_tests.rs:4` checks successful replacement and failure cleanup, and `:45` checks debris recognition. Replacing this with a short direct write would violate the requested atomic file contract.

- **Capture helpers retain intentional admission timing.** `native_capture.rs:58` inserts before checking the resulting map. Its explicit count refusal happens before summing byte lengths; deleting that branch because `check_size` also checks count changes the short-circuit cost and can expose unnecessary accumulation to an already-refused input. Native Workshop, editable capture and Test capture call these helpers with different admission and byte checkpoints (`src/native_host/workshop/mod.rs:162`, `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/workshop/provider.rs:825`, `provider/test_snapshot.rs:81`). The dedicated tests preserve overflow-byte detection, callback refusal and replacement counting. No verified deletion opportunity is claimed here.

- **Monitor matching, observation and SDK boundaries are not code-shape duplication.** The ordered four-pass rematch at `monitors.rs:177` onward distinguishes suffixed identity, same-position base identity, same-position mode renegotiation and position-independent base identity. Collapsing those predicates would change which screen wins. `surface_stats.rs:278` clones a snapshot while `:288` closes and transfers the recording; these similar constructors implement different ownership contracts. `FrameTrace` terminal guards at `:409` and `:421` ensure an upload followed by drop does not produce a second terminal event. Their small shared guard is not a worthwhile generic callback abstraction for this lens. `ultralight.rs:37`/`:44` retain retryable loaded/visibility state, and its load/script/frame errors carry deliberately different meanings. No broad wrapper deletion is supported by the inspected callers.

#### Coverage limitations

All crate production files and its separate test files were inspected, but external consumers were traced selectively around proposed changes and protected contracts. The entire native-host adapter, every host monitor-rematching or pool integration test, the external Vellum implementation, and every sound-format fixture were not exhaustively reviewed. No claim is made about hardware behavior, GPU correctness, SDK teardown on every target, or current pass/fail status of tests. The audio decoder was read and its PCM fixture inspected; its bounded-memory, format-change and nonfinite-sample checks yielded no justified line reduction. Compatibility facades, source documentation and independent tests are retained rather than counted as removable duplication.


## 13. Maths

Scope: `crates/phoenix-math/`


Audit 49/72 · Agent: `/root/audit49_math_overengineering` · Lens: overengineering

### Overengineering

Module: Maths (`crates/phoenix-math/`). Source inspected in `C:/Users/jkeyw/.codex/worktrees/reusable-game-layers/project-phoenix-v2`, against the supplied source baseline `07fc2e025bb2f5385b2fadf4fb468039b77c0b4e` and documentation-only HEAD `2de7d5329fba55e06ad00fbd8ca13f0cb4e8c73c`.

Scope: the crate manifest, module exports, all five implementation modules (`simmath`, `composite_rng`, `bounded_history`, `audio_config`, `lod_tune`) and their five test siblings. Caller tracing covered the composite-key steering decision, AI history reducers and helm histories, trigger-fire and GM activity rings, presentation/native audio consumers, the LOD tuner, and the simulation transcendental vector battery. Orientation included AGENTS.md, wiki/SCHEMA.md, CONTEXT.md, wiki/index.md, the reusable-layers guide, and the reusable-layers, deterministic-simulation and relevant data-driven-fine-system-ai PASM declarations. No nested AGENTS.md was found under crates; `docs/adr` is absent. This was read-only inspection: no builds, tests or edits to audited sources were performed.

#### 1. Low priority: two unused composite-random projections expand the public contract beyond the actual consumer

**Files and observed behavior.** `crates/phoenix-math/src/composite_rng.rs:125` exports `unit_interval`, which maps the seed's high 53 bits into `[0, 1)`. The same module at `:143` exports `bounded_index`, which performs a modulo mapping and defines zero length to return zero. Repository searches for both identifiers found their definitions and their own test calls only: `crates/phoenix-math/src/composite_rng_tests.rs:45`, `:115` and `:139`. No runtime, tool or integration-test consumer was found.

The actual consumer constructs the five-field key at `crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/ship/helm_ai/mod.rs:809`, obtains textual key components at `:815`, and calls only `signed_choice` at `:821`. That produces the orbit-direction memory value. `pasm/spec/architecture/data-driven-fine-system-ai.yaml:140` explicitly describes that signed decision and its stable world/ship/system/transition/occurrence key. It does not require a general suite of random projections.

**Deletion test and current cost.** Removing just `unit_interval` and `bounded_index` leaves that runtime path, its hash, its seed, and its signed outcome untouched. This is a small case of supplying additional reusable API without a present use: the cost is two public promises, their documentation, and tests for mappings no product code consumes. In particular, the zero-length index policy becomes a supported behavior despite no caller establishing that it is useful. There is no demonstrated runtime performance problem, and no claim that these short functions account for significant complexity.

**Concrete opportunity.** Remove the two unused projections if the crate's supported audience is the inspected workspace. Keep `CompositeKey`, field ordering, `key_from_name`, `composite_seed`, `signed_choice`, and all their existing reproducibility coverage. No replacement interface, generic distribution abstraction, dependency or caller rewrite is needed. If a documented external consumer needs either projection, retain that function instead: the deletion claim is explicitly limited to repository callers, not all possible downstream users of public re-exports.

**Invariants and verification.** Preserve the FNV byte order, per-field finalization, integer wrapping behavior, top-bit signed decision and exact recorded seed values. The fixture in `crates/phoenix-math/src/composite_rng_tests.rs:23`, order-sensitivity test at `:72`, field-participation test at `:50`, and signed-choice tests at `:99` and `:131` protect the required path. Only tests exclusively specifying the removed API would become obsolete; the shared same-key test should retain its remaining assertions. There is no need to change deterministic goldens or snapshot formats. A subsequent implementation should compile the consumers and run the owner-local RNG tests; no such validation was run for this audit.

**Benefits, tradeoff and confidence.** A slightly narrower public API and less unused policy to maintain, balanced against a very small saving and possible external API compatibility. Confidence is high that these functions have no in-repository consumer at this revision; confidence that removal is worth prioritizing is moderate to low. This is a cleanup candidate, not a reason to redesign the RNG module.

#### Mechanisms examined and retained by the deletion test

- **The pure crate boundary and libm wrappers are required indirection.** `crates/phoenix-math/Cargo.toml:8` explains the absence of Bevy; the actual runtime dependency section at `:18` contains libm and serde. `crates/phoenix-math/src/simmath.rs:7` records the native/WASM requirement, and `clippy.toml:72` supplies the forbidden-method enforcement. PASM makes shared arithmetic a determinism precondition at `pasm/spec/architecture/deterministic-simulation.yaml:184`. Replacing these one-line wrappers with native float methods would undermine that invariant; importing libm directly everywhere would distribute policy and naming across consumers rather than remove the need for it. Even wrappers with few ordinary gameplay callers are exercised by the cross-target vector battery at `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/simmath_vectors.rs:488`, including tan at `:515`, atan at `:531` and ln at `:557`. Their deletion is therefore not equivalent to removing the unused projections above. Existing local bit-pattern coverage is at `crates/phoenix-math/src/simmath_tests.rs:13`, paired sin/cos checks at `:44`, and scaled-distance coverage at `:55`. Those are valuable independent boundary checks, not overhead to prune.

- **Bounded history is real state, not a generic collection framework without consumers.** `crates/phoenix-math/src/bounded_history.rs:43` stores the numeric window; its fullness gate at `:73`, capacity changes at `:85`, reducers at `:118` and `:128`, maintained-level predicate at `:153`, and net change at `:178` correspond to actual decisions. `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/flags.rs:611` gates authored reducers on a full window. Helm sets range capacity at `crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/ship/helm_ai/facts.rs:1391`, reads maintained distance at `:1418`, and reads separation progress at `:1504`. Replacing the ring with a single running aggregate would change behavior when an old bad sample ages out. Removing capacity reconfiguration would also change authored tuning behavior. Tests in `bounded_history_tests.rs:47`, `:64`, `:109`, `:135` and `:164` cover those distinctions.

- **The generic ring also has concrete reuse, while the separate numeric storage has a compatibility constraint.** `BoundedRing<T>` at `crates/phoenix-math/src/bounded_history.rs:206` is used by `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/debug/scenario.rs:152` for trigger fires and `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/gm_activity.rs:324` for activity entries. Trigger capture retunes capacity at `debug/scenario.rs:190`; GM activity clears and refills the retained ordered window. The numeric and generic containers do repeat eviction machinery, but the numeric type's serialized shape is explicitly retained at `bounded_history.rs:193`, and snapshot-bearing AI memory consumes it. Neither deleting a container nor introducing a generic trait/delegation layer is justified by overengineering evidence alone. Any future consolidation must demonstrate preserved wire shape and semantics instead of assuming that two similarly shaped types can be replaced transparently. Ring behavior tests start at `bounded_history_tests.rs:232`; the numeric suite independently protects full-window meaning.

- **Audio's separate authored and wire shapes enforce a current boundary.** `crates/phoenix-math/src/audio_config.rs:178` holds ship configuration, `:210` holds world configuration, and `:228` defines the merged payload. `build_audio_payload` at `:302` is consumed by presentation at `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/server/audio.rs:156` and native audio at `src/native_host/audio/mod.rs:644`. The forcefield projection to a file-only record at `audio_config.rs:220` deliberately excludes Rust-owned envelope tuning. Deleting it in favor of serializing all authored data changes a tested boundary (`audio_config_tests.rs:275`). The envelope functions have live presentation calls at `server/audio.rs:228`, `:247` and `:248`, and listener geometry at `:300`. Optional sections mean silence, not an unnecessary fallback configuration hierarchy. Whether audio vocabulary belongs in a differently named crate is an ownership question; moving it would not delete the needed calculations or schemas and is not an overengineering finding here.

- **The two LOD knee rules serve different tool decisions.** `crates/phoenix-math/src/lod_tune.rs:84` defines unsigned distance from the normalized chord; `:152` defines the increasing-cost rule and its one-sided criterion. Callers are `src/bin/tune_lods.rs:1243` for range selection and `:976` for decimation. Image RMS also serves the capture stability check at `:1156`, beyond its comparison calls at `:729` and `:898`. The increasing rule rejects a concave rise, covered by `crates/phoenix-math/src/lod_tune_tests.rs:98`; the ordinary rule accepts diminishing-return bends, covered at `:58`. Replacing both with one unsigned rule loses behavior. A configurable strategy abstraction would add machinery around three direct functions without an identified benefit. Their Bevy-free location allows the algorithm tests to remain independent of GPU capture.

#### Limits of inspection

The five implementation modules were inspected in full. Caller evidence was focused on establishing current need and safe deletion boundaries; downstream audio playback, renderer capture orchestration, snapshot encoding and complete mission simulation were not audited end to end. Repository symbol search establishes in-tree references, not use by unpublished external crates. The report makes no new claim of native/WASM equality or passing tests: existing test sources and declared requirements were inspected, not executed. No additional high-impact overengineering candidate was established in this scope.

### Reuse / reinventing existing code

Module: Maths (`crates/phoenix-math/`). Inspected all five implementation modules, their unit-test files and the manifest at the audit worktree `C:/Users/jkeyw/.codex/worktrees/reusable-game-layers/project-phoenix-v2`. Caller tracing covered policy histories, helm recovery keys, trigger and GM activity recorders, asteroid identity generation, presentation audio and the LOD tuning executable. Read the repository operating instructions, wiki schema/index, vocabulary, reusable-layer guidance and the reusable-layers and deterministic-simulation PASM slices. No nested crate AGENTS.md files were found; `docs/adr/` is absent in this worktree. This is a read-only source audit; no checks were executed.

#### 1. The numeric history and generic ring repeat the same capacity-management implementation

**Evidence and actual consumers.** `crates/phoenix-math/src/bounded_history.rs:43` and `:206` define separate `capacity` plus `VecDeque` storage. `BoundedHistory::set_capacity`, `push` and `trim` at `:85`, `:94` and `:111` duplicate the generic operations at `:257`, `:267` and `:282`. Both explicitly clear on zero capacity, preserve data when capacity stays unchanged, and evict oldest values until within the limit. The generic ring is already used by `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/debug/scenario.rs:152` and `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/gm_activity.rs:324`; the numeric history is used by `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/flags.rs:704` and the helm histories in `crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/ship/helm_ai/surfaces.rs:366` and `:387`. Policy folding at `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/flags.rs:730` resets authored capacity each fold and clears the window when a fact disappears. These are live implementations, not dead compatibility copies.

**Opportunity.** Share only the private queue mutation primitives within this module while retaining both public types and their storage fields. The existing generic implementation establishes the reusable eviction behavior; factoring that behavior into private generic operations would give both wrappers one implementation. Prioritize `push`/trim and capacity changes, rather than generating an abstraction for trivial accessors. This is a small maintenance improvement: any future change to capacity handling would otherwise need two implementations kept aligned.

**Important constraint.** The comment at `bounded_history.rs:193` explicitly justifies separate storage to preserve the numeric window's snapshot shape. That is a valid reason not to replace `BoundedHistory` with a nested `BoundedRing<f64>` casually. A field-preserving helper extraction avoids that problem entirely. Preserve the `capacity, samples` serialization layout, default zero capacity, oldest-first iteration, copied numeric values versus borrowed generic values, and numeric-only reducers. In particular, do not turn a partially filled window into a qualifying full window or change the current `>= capacity` fullness test. Do not add validation or normalization during deserialization as an incidental reuse change.

**Verification.** Existing tests exercise numeric history fill/eviction, zero capacity, resizing, idempotent capacity updates and trend/fullness semantics (`bounded_history_tests.rs:16`, `:87`, `:109`, `:135`, `:151`); generic counterparts start at `:232`, `:250`, `:260`, `:271` and `:297`. The policy-memory RON roundtrip at `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/flags_tests.rs:1152` covers histories inside a real serialized consumer. A same-version roundtrip alone does not prove historical wire compatibility: capture a pre-refactor RON fixture and verify deserialization and unchanged output, especially if an implementation chooses a wrapper instead of preserving fields. A shared sequence of pushes and capacity edits applied to both types could also pin equivalence without deleting their independent public behavior tests.

**Tradeoff and confidence.** High confidence in duplication; moderate value because the bodies are short and stable. Keeping the duplication is defensible if a helper obscures the bounded-queue rule. No change to public types or dependency direction is necessary, and a blanket generic-history redesign would exceed the benefit.

#### 2. The LOD knee functions can reuse normalization while retaining different selection rules

**Evidence and actual consumers.** `crates/phoenix-math/src/lod_tune.rs:84` and `:152` independently implement identical input-length checks, endpoint selection, min/max accumulation, span calculation and nonpositive-span rejection. The subsequent rules intentionally differ: `find_knee` uses unsigned perpendicular distance, whereas `find_knee_increasing` uses the largest positive vertical drop below the chord. `src/bin/tune_lods.rs:1243` invokes the former for range tuning and `:976` invokes the latter for decimation selection. Their adjacent documentation is explicit that the signed rule rejects bends on the wrong side of the chord.

**Opportunity.** Extract a small private normalization preparation helper used by both functions, returning the existing scalar bounds and spans, and keep their scoring loops separate. This removes duplicate input policy and axis preparation without asking callers to select a configurable algorithm or introducing a new dependency. The opportunity is shared preparation, not replacing either function with the other.

**Invariants.** Preserve ascending-x expectations, fewer-than-three/mismatched-input rejection, current `f64` operation ordering, exact min/max fold behavior, first-best tie selection (`>` rather than `>=`), and `None` when no qualifying interior point exists. Do not algebraically substitute the vertical-drop formula for the unsigned perpendicular formula on the grounds that they are proportional in real arithmetic: rounding and sign handling are part of the existing behavior. These helpers are offline tuning math, with their explicit presentation/tooling exclusion at `lod_tune.rs:24`; they need no new simulation libm pathway, but stable tuning outputs still matter.

**Verification.** `lod_tune_tests.rs:46`, `:51`, `:58` and `:71` cover sample count, straight lines, the falling elbow and x-scale invariance. The increasing cases at `:85`, `:90`, `:98`, `:108`, `:123` and `:137` cover straight/concave rejection, accelerating costs, noise and scale. Add or preserve direct cases for mismatched arrays, repeated x endpoints, flat y, and tie selection if extracting preparation; the current inspected tests concentrate on normal curves. A before/after comparison on tuner curve inputs would detect incidental selection changes.

**Tradeoff and confidence.** High confidence in duplicated preparation; low-to-moderate payoff. A tiny local helper is proportionate; a generic curve-fitting framework is not. No evidence supports outsourcing the alpha-aware image metric or knee algorithm to an already-used library with identical semantics.

#### Examined similarities that should not become reuse findings

- **libm wrappers are the intended reuse boundary.** `simmath.rs:55` through `:128` already delegate to `libm`; they do not reimplement transcendental algorithms. The module's rationale at `:6` and the PASM cross-host precondition at `pasm/spec/architecture/deterministic-simulation.yaml:184` require this common backend. Replacing wrappers with native float methods, Bevy vector operations or another numeric implementation risks native/WASM divergence. `simmath_tests.rs:14` pins exact float bits, `:44` checks paired and individual sine/cosine agreement, and `:55` checks scaled hypot behavior. Similar-looking `sqrt(x*x+y*y)` would also lose hypot's overflow/underflow behavior. Existing compatibility imports are not duplicate math implementations.

- **Composite-key values and stateful RNG have different contracts.** `composite_rng.rs:99` folds five ordered fields and finalizes after each; `:112` finalizes a name hash. Helm transition entry uses these keys at `crates/phoenix-sim-gameplay/src/ship/helm_ai/mod.rs:815`. `crates/phoenix-sim-contracts/src/sim_rng.rs:38` instead describes persisted PCG draw streams. Substituting a stream draw for a composite value would make results depend on call order. `composite_rng_tests.rs:23` pins exact outputs, with order-sensitivity checks at `:72`. Even within this module, the FNV byte loop at `composite_rng.rs:87` resembles the loop at `:112`, but extracting their handful of statements is at most a minor local cleanup; it does not justify a repository-wide hashing abstraction.

- **The identical SplitMix64 implementation is deliberately independent.** `composite_rng.rs:76` and `crates/phoenix-simulation/src/asteroids/lifecycle.rs:574` have the same arithmetic. However, the asteroid comment at `lifecycle.rs:569` explicitly preserves its recorded identity mapping independently of fleet RNG decisions. The caller at `:534` hashes all cell/slot fields first, then performs two finalizations at `:559`; composite RNG finalizes after each field. They cannot share the higher-level derivation. Publishing a common low-level finalizer is technically possible but offers only a few lines while coupling two independently pinned contracts. Current requirements justify keeping the local twin. Asteroid tests at `lifecycle_tests.rs:25` and `:38` cover identity distinctness/repeatability; they should remain independent of composite-key fixtures.

- **Audio authored and wire shapes have intentional differences.** `audio_config.rs:178` and `:228` share most types already. The payload merges world red-alert data and strips forcefield envelope parameters down to the file (`:302`), so a type alias or wholesale serialization of authored configuration would be wrong. The tests at `audio_config_tests.rs:166`, `:275` and `:305` pin the merge, field exclusion and omitted sounds. `listener_relative` at `audio_config.rs:374` uses permitted presentation-only trigonometry and supplies Web Audio coordinates to `crates/phoenix-presentation/src/server/audio.rs:300`; replacing it with simulation/vector math is not demonstrated reuse and would add dependencies this crate deliberately excludes.

#### Scope limits

All production files in the assigned crate were inspected, and its test cases were read or inventoried to establish the relevant contracts. Repository-wide searches traced the concrete overlapping implementations and selected live consumers described above. This report does not claim a full audit of every math expression in the gameplay, presentation, JavaScript or external fleet dependencies. The external `vellum-rng` implementation was not inspected, and no behavior equivalence with it is asserted. Cross-target execution, historical snapshot compatibility and tuner output comparisons were not run; they are verification requirements for a later change, not evidence established by this audit.
